# Changelog

What changes in Search from one version to the next, newest first.

**Unreleased** gathers what is done since the last version, as it lands:
every fix and every addition gets its line the day it is merged. When a
version ships, the section takes its number and date, its gist becomes the
paragraph in `NOTES.md` (what Settings and the updater show), and the list
is what gets posted as the update. What is planned but not done yet lives
in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).

## Unreleased

### Added

- Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › Smooth scrolling with a mouse wheel, off unless you turn it on: each notch of a wheel that steps in lines, a Logitech MX Master's for one, glides over a few frames as in Chrome, where WebKit moves the page the whole 40 points of a line in one frame. A trackpad, and a mouse that scrolls smoothly on its own, are left as they are, and with ⌘, ⌃ or ⌥ held a notch goes to the page as it comes, for zooming. `python3 Tests/wheel_glide.py` (idea 60)
- New Tab in Group in a group heading's right-click menu, across the top and in the column, as Chrome's: a new tab at the end of that group, in front, the group opened if it was folded. With New tab in a floating field on, the field comes up and the tab it makes goes there. `python3 Tests/new_tab_in_group.py`
- Settings › Tabs › In the tab bar › Clicking the tab you're on edits its address only, off unless you turn it on: a click on the tab you're on opens its address to edit without the site card under it. Site Information… in the tab's right-click menu still shows the card. `python3 Tests/site_card_click.py` (idea 951)
- Open Link in Private Tab in a link's right-click menu, after Open Link in New Tab (and Open Link in Split View), as the bookmarks bar already has it: the link in a private tab in front, with nothing of the page it came from. Not on a private page, where Open Link in New Tab is private already. `python3 Tests/link_menu.py`. Thanks [@xibrox](https://github.com/xibrox) ([#1384](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1384), [#902](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/902), idea 568)
- Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Show the bookmarks bar in the sidebar, off unless you turn it on: with the bookmarks bar shown and the tabs down a side, it moves from above the page to the top of the column, a row for each of its bookmarks and folders before your pinned rows, the same in every space of the profile. A folder opens as a menu, as on the bar, and a right-click offers what the bar's does; with the column as icons, each is a tile. Show Bookmarks Bar hides and shows it there too. `swift test --filter SideBookmarksTests`. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1390](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1390))

### Changed

- A sidebar hidden until the pointer reaches the edge comes out sooner and is easier to find: it waits 0.05 s for the pointer to settle instead of 0.15 s, and the edge that brings it is 10 points wide instead of 6. The strip across the top, whose edge is the way to the menu bar, waits as before. ([#1279](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1279), idea 471)
- The email field for news and the iPhone beta (the What's New card, the welcome and Settings › About) asks "Did you mean name@gmail.com?" under it when the domain looks like a typo (gmail.con, gnail.com, studio.cmo); the address in the line fills the field, and pressing Join again sends it as typed. Worked out on the Mac, nothing is sent to check it. `swift test --filter EmailTypoTests`
- With the tabs across the top, what follows an open group (another group, or a tab in none such as ⌘T's) stands 10 points after its band instead of a tab's 2, so a tab after the group plainly isn't in it. Folded groups and the column are as before. `python3 Tests/group_band.py` (idea 953)
- On macOS 15, Settings › Extensions says under a Manifest V3 extension whose pages need WebAssembly, Bitwarden's among them, that it won't run there: WebKit on macOS 15 refuses WebAssembly in these extensions' own pages whatever they ask, and the popup only said WebAssembly isn't supported. On macOS 26 it runs. `swift test --filter ExtensionLimitsTests`. Reported by [@Rokai-Jibe](https://github.com/Rokai-Jibe) ([#1383](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1383))

### Fixed

- Switching spaces with the tabs in the column, by swiping, ⌃1–⌃9 or the space's menu, the space's icon at the foot of the column turns over to the new space's as its tabs and colour come in. It went on showing the last space until the space was taken, a moment after the hand was still. `python3 Tests/space_switch_paint.py` (idea 954)
- Swiping to another space with the tabs in the column, the column takes that space's colour as its tabs come in, as a click or ⌃1–⌃9 already brought it. It kept the last space's colour until the space was taken, a moment after the hand was still, and with fingers resting on the trackpad not until a click in the column. `python3 Tests/space_switch_paint.py` (idea 954)
- Switching spaces with the tabs in the column, the tabs in a group come in set in under its name and, holding no page, dimmed, as they stay: drawn on their way in from the map of every space's tabs, a group without a colour had its tabs flush left and none of them dimmed, and as the space was taken they jumped in and faded. `python3 Tests/space_switch_paint.py` (idea 955)
- Saved passwords are harder to steal through a page's tricks: a frame from another site, or one it names to pass for Search's own, is never filled, and a pick with the caret in such a frame fills nothing rather than the page's first password box; a box kept out of sight (tiny, faded out, hidden, clipped away or far off the page) is passed over for the visible one; and a password is put only in a box that hides what is typed, never in a plain text box. Only the page's own frames can talk to the browser's password filling. `python3 Tests/forms_frames_attack.py`.
- With the tabs across the top, the cross on a tab in a group is a minus, with Put Away as its tooltip, as in the column: pressing it lets the tab's page go and keeps the tab in its group, which looked as if the cross had done nothing. A tab put away is now dimmed there too, whatever Dim sleeping tabs says. `python3 Tests/close_in_group.py`, `python3 Tests/group_band.py` (idea 950)
- On a new tab with a background picture or theme (Settings › Themes), the field no longer stands in a big haze, white in light mode and dark in dark mode: the patch of plain ground laid under it to soften the wall, and the breathing glow, are gone there; the field keeps its own shadow. The plain new tab is as it was. Checked rendered off screen in light and dark (idea 952)
- A fast scroll down no longer goes back a page now and then, and a swipe back is called off with a short pull back. Two fingers that set off a little sideways while the page was still gliding from the last flick read as a swipe; a new gesture within a moment of scrolling up or down now has to be plainly sideways to be one, and never a mere flick. And once pulled far enough to go, the swipe went on letting go anywhere past that point, so taking it back meant pulling nearly all the way; drawn back 20 points from the furthest the fingers went, it lets go of the page, with the lighter tap to say so. `python3 Tests/swipe_intent.py`. Reported by [@AKwasTaken](https://github.com/AKwasTaken) ([#1389](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1389))
- With the Home bookmarks on a new tab, the empty side of a Split View lists the open tabs to bring in under the Home tiles instead of over them. `swift test --filter HomeGridTests`. Reported by [@ryadhiprasetya](https://github.com/ryadhiprasetya) ([#1381](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1381))
- Similarweb's panel shows its numbers: its spinner turned for good. Its worker reads the Cookie header of its own request in a `webRequest` listener, and here nothing told an extension of a fetch the browser sends for it. An extension's `onBeforeSendHeaders` and `onSendHeaders` listeners now hear the fetches the browser sends from the same page or worker, as one request from no tab, as in Chrome: only for an extension that holds `webRequest`, only for a site it has been granted, and the Cookie header only to a listener that asked for `extraHeaders`. `python3 Tests/extension_own_requests.py`. Thanks [@realaeeron](https://github.com/realaeeron) ([#1376](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1376), [#1324](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1324))
- With one button per space, a coloured space's button wears its colour only while that space is on screen; the others are grey alike, so a coloured one no longer reads as the space you're in. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1385](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1385))
- Opening YouTube from the address field now and then saved its home page into Downloads as www.youtube.com.html instead of showing it: with YouTube's service worker asleep, WebKit can hand the tab an empty answer, no address and no type, as the page moves to a new process, and Search took that for a file. It now asks for the page once more, which comes through; once only, so a page that keeps answering so is stopped rather than loaded for ever. Thanks [@gxlactuss](https://github.com/gxlactuss) ([#1387](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1387))
- Continue Anyway on a site whose certificate isn't trusted now trusts that certificate alone, for the rest of the launch, not the site: a different bad certificate on the same name, as someone in the middle would show, asks again. `swift test --filter CertificateExceptionTests`. Thanks [@datasciritwik](https://github.com/datasciritwik) ([#1377](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1377))
- A tab whose video was in full screen, left for another tab and come back to, showed a big white screen with the video out of full screen: the page now comes back where it was, the video playing in it. `swift test --filter StageFullScreenTests` (idea 936)
- A pin renamed in Arc or Zen and brought over showed its address instead of its name once browsed in and closed: at rest on its pinned page it shows its own name again, the page's title only while it shows the page. `python3 Tests/pin_names.py` (idea 937)
- Floating a music video from a YouTube Music playlist shows the video alone, not the top bar, side drawer and the player bar's stacked buttons over it, nor the video a window's height below the little window while the player was still sliding up. Tests/float_song.py.
- In full screen, the bar with the window's buttons comes down with the menu bar no taller than macOS's own, as in Safari: on 1.0.18 it was 36 points taller and grey, and pushed the tabs and the page down that much more. `swift test --filter TopEdgeTests` (idea 934)
- With the tabs in the column, a press-drag in Amazon's search box selects its text instead of moving the window. The box lies just under the thin band along the top, in the 32 points macOS takes as title bar, where a press moved the window whatever was under it; there too the window now holds still over a field, a button, a link or text, and still moves over empty page. `python3 Tests/top_band_field.py` (idea 935)
- A saved password is offered on focus in more sign-in shapes, as Safari and Chrome offer it: a sign-in after a sign-up form, or after a password box kept out of sight; boxes inside web components (open shadow roots), each in its own too; a sign-in in a frame of the page's own origin, the list hung where the frame puts the box; and a text box marked `autocomplete="current-password"`. Before, only right-click › AutoFill filled them. A frame of another origin is still offered and filled nothing, and nothing is filled without a pick. `python3 Tests/login_shapes.py` (idea 938)
- Show the bookmarks bar in the sidebar is counted among the settings iCloud sync understands, as every other synced setting is: it was synced but left off that list. `swift test --filter SyncTests`
- Ads on albumoftheyear.org are blocked as uBlock Origin and EasyList block them: Block ads and trackers now also stops Playwire's ad stack (cdn.intergient.com) and the scripts that bring ads back past a blocker or look for one (Blockthrough's btloader.com, ad-delivery.net, mentorsticks.com, dns-finder.com, Confiant), and Cloudflare's analytics beacon on other sites' pages, and hides the site's empty ad boxes. (idea 685)
- A page opened from disk shows the styles and pictures a folder or two above its own, as Safari and Chrome show them: a static site's lessons/one.html reading ../assets/style.css opened unstyled, since the page could read only its own folder. Never as far as the home folder, a folder directly in it (Documents, Desktop, Downloads…), the disk or a volume. `python3 Tests/local_parent_folder.py` ([#1342](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1342), idea 864)
- A local page an app hands over as an address (file://…), as Python's webbrowser and the apps built on it do to open their page through a file that sends you on to localhost (Jupyter's way, Claude Science's), opens and goes on to localhost, as in Safari and Firefox. Search dropped any address from another app that wasn't http, https or mailto, so nothing opened. ([#775](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/775), idea 462)

### Held back

Not in this version, kept on main for a later one:

- AI on pages, off unless you turn it on in Settings › AI: Summarize Page and Ask About This Page… in the View menu, answered where you choose — a model downloaded to this Mac (Apple Silicon: Qwen3 1.7B, 1.1 GB, checked before every use and run by a small program of Search's own that can't reach the network or your files), Ollama or LM Studio on this Mac, your own key for Anthropic, OpenAI, Google Gemini or OpenRouter, or signing in with your OpenRouter account. The answer comes in a panel beside the page, in plain text, marked as possibly wrong and with where it ran; follow-up questions stay in the panel, and nothing is kept once it closes. Only the page's visible text goes, never a password, a form or another tab; the page's words are given to the model as something to read, not orders, and any web address, email or phone number in the answer that isn't on the page is pointed out. Keys stay in this Mac's keychain and go only to their provider; a provider off this Mac is shown what it will be sent before the first request, and a private tab uses only what runs on this Mac.

## 1.0.18 — 22 November 2026

### Added

- News by email and the Search for iPhone beta, offered at the foot of the What's New card after an update, as the welcome's last page and in Settings › About › News by email: an email field and a button, with a box for the iPhone beta. Nothing is sent until you press; then one POST to searchbrowser.app carries only the address, where you pressed and which Mac download you have. Search remembers only that you joined, never the address, and stops offering it on later cards once you have. `swift test --filter OptInTests`.
- A sidebar button, in Settings › Appearance › Tabs: right of the window's own buttons, before back, forward and reload, it hides the sidebar as ⌘S does, where Safari and Arc keep theirs. With the sidebar out over the page at the window's edge, the same click keeps it out. Only with titles in the column; off unless you turn it on. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1351](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1351))
- Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Narrow to icons, off unless you turn it on: pull the sidebar narrower than 200 points, where titles, the window's buttons and the foot started to run into each other, and it becomes a thin strip of the tabs' letters or icons, as you have them, with its buttons — before the pins with Buttons at the top, after the tabs otherwise — in the space's colour. Back, forward and reload stay on their keys. Pull the edge back out, double-click it, or press ⌘S where ⌘S shrinks the column, for titles again. `swift test --filter NarrowStripTests`. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1360](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1360))
- bench-mcp reads a page as an outline too: `outline_page` gives what needs attention (dialogs, alerts, invalid fields, the focused control) and then the page's sections with their size and first words, and opens a section by its id with handles for its controls; `read_section` gives a section's words and `find_in_page` where words are. Wikipedia's Hyderabad article is 510 bytes as an outline and 72 KB from `read_page`. Its handles are a read's like `read_page`'s, it only reads, and a password or card field's value never leaves the page. `python3 Tests/bench_mcp.py`. Thanks [@kalki-kgp](https://github.com/kalki-kgp) ([#1371](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1371), [#1329](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1329), idea 869)

### Changed

- Send Feedback (⇧⌘F, the Help menu, About) and the help links write to hello@searchbrowser.app, Search's own address.
- With the tabs across the top, an open tab group now shows where it starts and ends: a soft band runs behind its name and its tabs, in the group's colour or a faint grey without one. Right-click the group's name and choose Colour… to set it there too. A folded group looks as before. `python3 Tests/group_band.py`
- Sync with iCloud and Handoff are on for a new install, as on the iPhone: the welcome's first page shows both switches, on, with a line on what each sends, and nothing syncs or is handed off until you leave the welcome, so either can be turned off first; quit during the welcome and nothing has gone anywhere. Where iCloud isn't there (not signed in, or a build not signed for it) sync waits quietly and Settings says why. Anyone who had Search before keeps both as they were, and the What's New card now offers Sync with iCloud first, then Handoff, while they're off. Told apart once, at the first launch of this version: a Search folder, or any line an earlier Search wrote, means an install from before. `swift test --filter SyncDefaultTests`, `python3 Tests/sync_default.py`

### Fixed

- ⌘S stutters no more where it makes the column icons only and you have several spaces: since 1.0.16 every space's tabs, kept out of sight for a swipe, were laid out again at each width the column passed through, and the column moved on every other frame at best; they now wait until it rests. `python3 Tests/fold_to_icons_pace.py` (idea 927)
- In full screen, the menu bar and the bar with the window's buttons come down over nothing of Search's again: the strip of tabs, the bookmarks bar, the column and the page move down by exactly what came down, measured from the bar itself, in both layouts. On 1.0.17 that bar came down taller and grey, over the row of tabs too. `python3 Tests/full_screen_reveal.py`, `swift test --filter TopEdgeTests` (idea 926)
- With the tabs in the column, a press-drag in a field at the very top of a page, Amazon's search box, selects its text instead of moving the window, as in Arc. The thin band along the top that moves the window leaves a field, a button, a link or text under it to the page, and still moves the window over empty page and still fills the screen on a double-click. `python3 Tests/top_band_field.py` (idea 928)
- Search no longer asks to save a password nobody typed. A logged-in dashboard's list asked "Save the password for 40" after a click: its page size was taken for the name and a key the page showed masked for the password. A password is offered only once something was typed or filled into its box, and the name only comes from a box that could hold one: not a list's picker, a page size or a search, and with no form around the password, only a box that says it takes a name. `python3 Tests/password_false_offer.py`
- Float the video on its own floats only a video you are watching: one playing with sound, or muted after you heard it, big enough to watch, longer than a loop, and not a preview in a link or a thumbnail. Leaving YouTube's home page during a muted hover preview floated the preview, and the little window went black when it ended. A floated video that ends, loses its source or goes from its page now closes the window. ⇧⌘P still floats any video. `python3 Tests/float_preview.py` (idea 923)
- Closing a window while another stays open stops that window’s background checks for tabs to put to sleep. `swift test --filter KeepAwakeTests`. Thanks [@shawn-t516](https://github.com/shawn-t516) ([#1356](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1356))
- A draft typed into an embedded form keeps its tab awake, including a form hosted by another site. Clearing it lets the tab sleep again. `swift test --filter PageDraftTests`. Thanks [@shawn-t516](https://github.com/shawn-t516) ([#1357](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1357))
- The Downloads card stands by the button you clicked when Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Buttons at the top is on and the column is too narrow for them there: it hung from the top of the column instead of beside the downloads button at its foot. `swift test --filter DownloadsAnchorTests`. Thanks [@Xander-AJ](https://github.com/Xander-AJ) ([#1364](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1364), [#1350](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1350))
- Buttons say their key as it stands in Settings › Shortcuts, and say it again the moment it changes: Back, Forward, Reload, New Tab, Downloads, the find bar's arrows, the site card's zoom, the address, Hide something…, an agent's Stop, and each space's button. The keys were written into their tooltips, so one you changed still showed the old key, and most buttons named none. A command without a key shows its name alone. `swift test --filter ShortcutTests`. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1353](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1353))
- Hide Elements (⇧⌘H) says Hidden only once the thing is gone: a page's own more particular `!important` rule no longer keeps it on screen, a pick the page still shows isn't kept or listed, ↑ and ↓ widen and narrow what is outlined (the words of a banner, then the banner), and pointing stops when the page reloads or goes elsewhere. `python3 Tests/hide_elements.py`. Thanks [@hardope](https://github.com/hardope) ([#1278](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1278), [#1270](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1270))
- ⌘T's floating field and ⌘K's switcher come up over the page again after a click into the address bar (Settings › Window › Always show the address bar): from then on they came up in the bar instead, and with the bar turned off, not at all. `swift test --filter AddressLineLaunchTests`. Reported by [@ctrl-raj](https://github.com/ctrl-raj) ([#1374](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1374))
- Rename on a pinned square, or on any tab with the column as icons only, asks for the name in a small sheet, as a bookmark's Rename does. There was no title to type over, so nothing came and the edit stayed open, unseen, until Escape. `swift test --filter TabRenameTests`. Reported by [@fer7G](https://github.com/fer7G) ([#1354](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1354))
- An extension's popup from the first button at the column's foot no longer points at the button beside it. With the window against the screen's left edge (or the right one, the column on the right), the arrow is moved 29 points away from the edge to stay clear of the popover's corner ([#1188](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1188)), past the 26-point button and onto the middle of the next: Bitwarden's popup opened with its arrow on Similarweb's button, once it opened at all ([#1323](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1323)). No whole arrow can point at a button that near the corner, so there the popup opens above its button without one; every other button keeps its arrow. `swift test --filter PopoverSideTests` Thanks [@realaeeron](https://github.com/realaeeron) ([#1375](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1375))
- Sites at `.local` names and on other devices in your home or office load on macOS 15 and later: Search now carries the sentence macOS shows when it asks for local network access, and without it macOS could block those connections without asking. Reported by [@soldier99](https://github.com/soldier99) ([#1348](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1348))
- A window opened while the app is still bringing back last time's windows is written down once: it no longer comes back twice at the next launch, and the file that keeps your windows no longer grows with every save until they are back.
- With the tabs across the top, a tab too narrow for three letters of its title shows its icon alone, centred: between about 80 and 104 points wide it showed its icon and a lone "…". The tab you're on says its address only where the site's name fits beside its port, and its title otherwise; a pair in Split View does the same with each half. `python3 Tests/tab_titles.py`

## 1.0.17 — 22 November 2026

### Added

- Settings › Privacy › Try HTTPS first, off unless you turn it on: an http:// link, bookmark or history entry tries HTTPS first and loads as asked only where the site has none. Sites WebKit already knew have HTTPS were upgraded before; with it on, any site is. Local addresses (localhost, .test, .local, IP addresses) are left as they are. macOS 15.2 and later. Thanks [@beatsbypedro](https://github.com/beatsbypedro)
- Coming from Zen brings its spaces too, the way Arc's come: each of its spaces a space here with its name, icon and colours, its pinned tabs that space's, its folders kept as folders where pinned rows hold them, and its Essentials as pins in every space. Read from the file Zen keeps them in, a backup of it when that is torn, or the tables older versions of Zen kept; nothing in Zen changes.
- Open tabs and tab groups come over from Firefox and Zen too, as they do from Chrome-family browsers: asleep at the end of the space, a group by its name and folded as it was, Firefox's pinned tabs as pins (Zen's come with its spaces). Off in the import sheet unless you turn it on.
- More browsers to come from: Thorium, Naver Whale, Island and Sidekick, with their passwords; Yandex and ChatGPT Atlas, their bookmarks, history and open tabs (their passwords are locked away where other apps can't read them); Floorp, LibreWolf, Waterfox and Mullvad Browser, as Firefox; and Orion, its bookmarks and history, each profile. A Firefox-family profile is read once, however its folder is spelled.
- Safari comes over directly when macOS lets Search read its files (with Full Disk Access, which Search never asks for): its bookmarks, Reading List and history. Otherwise the import sheet says in a sentence why it comes from a file, and How shows the steps for Safari's File › Export Browsing Data to File….
- The import sheet and the Welcome say, under each browser found on this Mac, what can come over from it: bookmarks, history, passwords, spaces & pins, open tabs. Browsers that aren't here aren't listed.
- Settings › Tabs › Swipe back closes a tab a link opened, off unless you turn it on: on the page a link (or a page's window.open) opened in a new tab, a swipe back shows a cross, closes the tab and goes back to the tab you were on, as a small window's swipe closes it. Pages clicked through in the tab go back as usual first, and a mouse's back button still does nothing there. `swift test --filter SwipeBackClosesTests`. Thanks [@xibrox](https://github.com/xibrox) ([#1337](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1337), idea 565)
- A blank tab can show your Home bookmarks: make a bookmark folder called Home, turn on Settings › Tabs › Show the Home bookmarks on a new tab, and up to 16 of its sites stand as tiles under the address field, in Split View too. `swift test --filter HomeGridTests`. Thanks [@harshith-ashok](https://github.com/harshith-ashok) ([#1316](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1316), [#1308](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1308))
- Settings › Spaces & Pins › ⌘T finds your open tabs, off unless you turn it on, with the new tab in a floating field: as you type in ⌘T's field, the open tabs that match come first, then your pins, then the places a new tab would go, and the best of them is picked as you type, as Arc's ⌘T does. Return switches to that tab instead of opening the page again, and the field keeps what you typed; ↑ lets go of the tab, and Return opens what you typed. `python3 Tests/newtab_arc.py`. Thanks [@xenonwellz](https://github.com/xenonwellz) ([#1288](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1288), [#1284](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1284))
- A split can be pinned as a split: Pin Split View in a page's right-click menu, Pin Tab in the Tabs menu, or the split dragged onto the pinned rows. Every page of it becomes a pin and they stay one split, drawn as one row as among the tabs, so a click on it brings the split back as it was. Unpin on either page leaves both as a Split View among the tabs, Delete closes both, and ⌘W puts the whole split down; dragged, it moves whole. A pinned split is always rows, stays out of folders, and has no Only in This Space or Keep in Every Space. Open 2 Tabs in Split View over two pins makes them a pinned pair rather than copying them into new tabs. `swift test --filter PinSplitTests`, `python3 Tests/pin_split.py`. Thanks [@xenonwellz](https://github.com/xenonwellz) ([#1286](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1286), [#1282](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1282))
- Settings › Spaces & Pins › One button per space, off unless you turn it on: every space beside the bookmarks, a click away, in place of the single space icon and its menu. Each wears its space's icon and colour, the one you are in marked, and a click on that one opens the space's menu. At the foot of the column the row is centred. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1339](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1339))
- ⌘O keeps a peek as a tab, in front, as ⌘↩ and the panel's button do, and as ⌘O already does in the small window for links from other apps. `python3 Tests/peek_keep_keys.py`. Thanks [@danpasecinic](https://github.com/danpasecinic) ([#1340](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1340))
- Settings › Themes paints the blank tab, off unless you choose one: ten painted walls, ten sticker packs, a solid colour, a gradient, or a picture of your own (copied inside Search), with Intensity, Density and Scale to tune, a live preview at the top of the page, and Shuffle for a different wall or pack each day. Each has a light and a dark look. Drawn once and kept, only behind a blank tab, so it costs nothing while you browse. Thanks [@datasciritwik](https://github.com/datasciritwik) ([#1318](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1318), [#1317](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1317))

### Changed

- Right-click one of several tabs picked with ⌘ or ⇧ and the menu lists only what can be done to all of them, and does it to all of them: Move to Group, Pin or Unpin, Move to Space, Open in Split View, Move to Window, Copy Addresses, Mute, Put to Sleep, Close and Close Other Tabs. An action shows only when every picked tab can take it, and moving them to a space with other sign-ins asks once, not once a tab. `swift test --filter TabSelectionTests`. Thanks [@xenonwellz](https://github.com/xenonwellz) ([#1287](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1287), [#1283](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1283))
- Settings › Appearance › Tabs show says what the choice is, "A letter or the site's icon"; where it shows, beside the title and on a pinned square, is behind the (i). The line only said where, and tabs wearing letters were taken for site icons that hadn't loaded. Thanks [@dnhuan](https://github.com/dnhuan) ([#1276](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1276), [#1271](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1271))
- The welcome's two small pictures, Sidebar and Tab strip, show what the tabs wear as Tabs wear is switched: a letter in a faint square, or a patch of colour where a site's icon would be. Thanks [@dnhuan](https://github.com/dnhuan) ([#1275](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1275), [#1271](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1271))
- Profiles can be put in any order, Personal no longer always first. With Settings › Spaces & Pins › Profiles in one window on, Move Left on a profile's first space, or Move Right on its last, moves the whole profile past the one beside it: the space's menu, ⌃1–⌃9, the swipe and the profiles list follow, and the order is kept. `python3 Tests/profile_order.py` (reported by email)
- Spaces brought over from Arc (or Zen) take the place of the space you started with when nothing was ever put in it (no pins, no tab but an empty one), as in Arc, instead of leaving an unused "Personal" beside them. A first space with anything in it stays. `python3 Tests/space_arc_replace.py` ([#1326](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1326))

### Fixed

- Sync with your other Macs brings pinned-row folders too, and no longer takes pins out of theirs: a pin coming from another Mac lost its folder, since a pin's item had no folder in it, and the folders themselves never went. Now each space's folders and each pin's folder travel as items of their own, made, renamed, nested, moved and deleted on either Mac; folding and a live folder's posts stay on each Mac. A pin that comes before its folder shows at the top of the rows until the folder arrives. A Mac on 1.0.16 carries the new items along untouched, and its edits to a pin leave the pin in its folder. `swift test --filter SyncTests`, `python3 Tests/sync_two_macs.py` (idea 909)
- Sync with iCloud brings pinned-row folders to your other Macs: a folder of pins made on one Mac came to the other as loose pins, since the folders themselves never went up. They now travel in a record of their own per space, made, renamed, moved and deleted on either Mac, with each pin in its folder; folding stays on each Mac. A pin that comes before its folder shows at the top of the rows, then goes into the folder when it arrives. A Mac with Folders in pinned rows off keeps them for when it turns the setting on. A Mac on 1.0.16 leaves the new record alone, and takes it in once updated. `swift test --filter ICloudSyncTests`, `python3 Tests/icloud_two_macs.py` (idea 903)
- Floating a song on YouTube Music shows its album cover: the little window showed the player's buttons and its Up next and Lyrics tabs, since the song's video there is hidden or has no picture. With no cover to show, such a page doesn't float. `python3 Tests/float_song.py`
- ⇧⌘T on a page closed out of a Split View puts it back in the split with its Back and Forward again: it came back on the right page but with no history behind it. `python3 Tests/reopen_history.py`
- Escape answers no again to a camera or microphone question in a site's small pop-up window: since 1.0.13 a pop-up opened in front kept its keys from every window, so its question stayed up. `python3 Tests/website_popups.py`
- The floating Downloads card keeps its whole shadow beside the column (its window now leaves 18 points on the button's side, which cut the shadow off flat at 8), and its height changes as one smooth animation of the window when the list grows, shrinks or is cleared, in place of a jump. `swift test --filter DownloadsOpeningTests` (idea 897)
- ⌘S slides the column smoothly again, as in 1.0.15. Since 1.0.16 the page under it turned its own background off while it grew, so WebKit painted the whole page twice and the Mac had to blend it, see-through, all through the slide, and the slide stuttered in WindowServer. ⌘S already covers the room a page grows into, so the page now keeps its background there; a window made wider still gets #1250's fix, without the dark strip. `python3 Tests/fold_pacing.py` (idea 847)
- History from before 1.0.16 shows in your signed-out spaces again. Since 1.0.16 a space with cookies of its own keeps a history of its own (#1166), and those spaces started empty, everything visited before left in the first space. At the first launch now, that history is copied once into each signed-out space there is, as they all saw it before. What a signed-out space visits stays its own, and a space made later starts empty. Spaces signed in with the first keep sharing its history. `python3 Tests/space_history_seed.py` ([#1325](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1325))
- Turning Spaces on no longer seems to make a second space. With only one space, a stroke or a notch over the tabs toward a next one gives a little and comes back, instead of bringing the nameless card for a new space that looked like a space nobody made (New Space… still opens it). And a single Arc space brought over with Spaces off gives the one space there is its name and icon, so turned on, Spaces shows the space you brought rather than "Personal". `python3 Tests/space_turn_on.py` (reported by email)
- Any space can be deleted, the first one included, as long as its profile keeps one: its menu has Delete now. The next space in the order becomes the profile's first, the one a window shows with Spaces off, and keeps the sign-ins it had. A space signed in with the others erases nothing of them; one with cookies of its own still asks before they go. A profile's last space offers to delete the profile instead (Personal keeps its last). `python3 Tests/space_delete_first.py` ([#1326](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1326))
- A bookmarks folder of thousands no longer stalls the window when it opens in the bookmarks list. Every row was built at once: a folder of 5,000 took 17.5 seconds to show. Now only the rows in sight are built, and the rest as you scroll to them (25 ms for the same folder); dragging rows and their menus are as before. `python3 Tests/bookmarks_large.py` checks it. Reported by [@colinaut](https://github.com/colinaut) ([#1306](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1306))
- With the tabs across the top, the last tab is no longer cut off with its close button out of reach. The run of tabs was as wide as Search worked its tabs out to be, and a group's name can be drawn a little wider than it is measured, as on macOS 15: the run came out short, and since the row seemed to fit it didn't scroll either. Now it is as wide as its tabs are laid out, or scrolls; and the profile's badge, once there are several profiles, is counted in the row's room. `python3 Tests/top_row_fit.py` checks it. Reported by [@maxikobi](https://github.com/maxikobi) ([#1295](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1295))
- The bookmarks list off the bookmark button is as tall as the rows it shows, and grows as you open a folder and shrinks as you close one, up to the height it can be before it scrolls. It was sized for every folder open, which left a large empty gap above Add This Page with them closed. `python3 Tests/bookmarks_dropdown.py` checks it. Reported by [@derpyjoystick](https://github.com/derpyjoystick) ([#1309](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1309))
- The site card hangs from its tab after a double-click on a tab you weren't on, instead of coming up near the middle of the screen. The second click opened the tab's address while the row was still settling around the tab now in front; the card now waits until the field has stopped moving, and goes with it while it is up, as when a tab before it closes. An address field going away late no longer ends the edit begun in another tab, which took the card away with it. `python3 Tests/site_card_anchor.py` checks it. Reported by [@ryadhiprasetya](https://github.com/ryadhiprasetya) ([#1310](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1310))
- Extension popups open at their own size with System Settings › Appearance › Show scroll bars set to Always. They came out 17 points too wide and too tall: measured while still a small square, the page overflowed it, macOS's classic scroll bars came up, and their room was counted as the popup's. A scroll bar now counts only when the page asks for one, or when the popup is too tall and will scroll. Reported by [@beatsbypedro](https://github.com/beatsbypedro) ([#1311](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1311))
- Browser games can lock the pointer, as in Safari: a page that asks for it right after your click or key, in the tab in front, gets it, and "Press Esc to show your pointer" shows over the page for a few seconds; Esc gives it back. WebKit could do it, but only once the app said yes, and Search never answered, so the cursor stayed and the camera didn't follow the mouse. Never in a coding agent's tab. `python3 Tests/pointer_lock.py` checks it. Reported by [@Shashwat2907](https://github.com/Shashwat2907) ([#1305](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1305))
- A site that asks for a client certificate, as some company and government sign-ins do, gets one you choose. Search asks over that site's tab, listing your keychain's certificates from the authorities the site accepts, with their issuer and expiry, and remembers the choice for that site until you quit (a private tab asks for itself and forgets when it closes). A certificate you set for the site in Keychain Access is used without asking, as in Safari. Before, nothing was sent and the site answered "No required SSL certificate was sent".
- YouTube videos play again for accounts YouTube walls for using a blocker ("Ad blockers violate YouTube's Terms of Service"). With Shield on, YouTube's own blocker tests failed: static.doubleclick.net's ad_status.js never ran, and the ad id request and ad reports were blocked, so YouTube wrote its wall over an answer that said the video could play. ad_status.js is now let through on YouTube's pages, the ad id and ad reports Shield blocks are answered in the page with an empty reply (nothing is sent), and the wall written over a playable answer is ignored. Ads stay out ([#1320](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1320)) Thanks [@kalki-kgp](https://github.com/kalki-kgp)
- An extension's popup opens from the first button at the column's foot again. With the window against the screen's left edge (or the right one, the column on the right), a click on the pinned extension nearest the edge did nothing from 1.0.16: the popup's page loaded, in a view no window held. The arrow is moved away from the screen's edge to stay clear of the popover's corner ([#1188](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1188)), there by 29 points, past the 26-point button, and `NSPopover` shows nothing, and says nothing, from a rect that lies wholly outside the view it is given. The popover is now shown from the nearest view that holds the moved rect, at the same place. `swift test --filter PopoverSideTests` Thanks [@realaeeron](https://github.com/realaeeron)
- A tab that went to sleep keeps what its page stored for the session (sessionStorage), so a draft or a form kept there is still there when you come back to it. The page's own, up to half a megabyte, held in memory only and never for a private tab; frames inside the page still start afresh. Thanks [@beatsbypedro](https://github.com/beatsbypedro)
- Similarweb's button opens its panel. It slides in from the side of the page, in a frame inside an empty frame of its own; WebKit checks an extension's web accessible pages against the frame just above, there `about:blank`, which no pattern matches, so the panel's page was refused without a word and the button did nothing. Chrome checks against the site. The empty frame is now given the site's address as the extension puts its page in it, what it already held kept as it was; the page is then checked against the site, and one that isn't web accessible is refused as before. A probe builds its frames the way Similarweb does: `python3 Tests/extension_nested_frame.py` Thanks [@realaeeron](https://github.com/realaeeron)
- Brandsearch's panel no longer stays grey. Its worker asks app.brandsearch.co for your account with the extension's token and nothing said about credentials. Chrome sends a site's cookies with such a fetch to a site the extension has been granted, and Brandsearch's site lets a request in on its session cookie alone: Search sent them with a plain fetch from an extension's page but not from its worker, so the site answered with its sign-in page, which the panel took for the account. A worker's plain fetch to a site its extension has been granted now carries the site's cookies as a page's does; other sites, and web pages, get nothing new. A worker's fetches through the browser also stopped waiting a tenth of a second every so often: its first word on the port, when it came back unheard, was only said again at the next turn of a timer. Thanks [@realaeeron](https://github.com/realaeeron)
- Passkey sign-in works in Peek and small link windows, including after navigating to another site. Their committed page address was never recorded, so Search refused the request before the Touch ID sheet could appear (#1263). `python3 Tests/passkey_overlays.py` checks it. Thanks [@guilopeszw](https://github.com/guilopeszw)
- Floccus can pick a bookmark folder to sync: choosing one said "Folder not found". It finds a folder's path by asking `chrome.bookmarks.get` for each parent in turn, and the bookmarks bar ("1") and the root ("0") came back empty, though `getSubTree` and `getChildren` knew them. Then syncing failed with "this.absoluteRoot.id": an id that names no bookmark came back empty instead of an error, as Chrome gives, so Floccus never fell back from Firefox's root id to `getTree`. Thanks [@xibrox](https://github.com/xibrox)
- In full screen, the strip of tabs and the bookmarks bar move down with the menu bar and the bar with the window's buttons when you bring the pointer to the top, instead of lying under them out of reach, as Safari's and Chrome's do.
- Monarch Money | Retail purchase sync no longer shows its options page without any text. Its code reads `chrome.i18n.getMessage` into a variable and calls it from there, and WebKit answers nothing for that; the i18n functions now work however they are called.
- A security key (a YubiKey, say) works with Settings › Passwords › Offer passkeys off. The switch refused every request for one at once, "The operation either timed out or was not allowed.", before any sheet. Off now means what it says, no passkeys from the Mac, iCloud or a phone: a sign-in that names a key over USB, NFC or Bluetooth, or a key being added as one, brings up the Mac's sheet for the security key alone, and everything else still goes straight on to the password. `python3 Tests/security_key_passkeys_off.py` (idea 846)
- The rows the column keeps out of sight for a swipe between spaces are hidden until a swipe brings them, not only moved aside. Since 1.0.16 they lay under the page still drawn, so whatever moved in them went on moving there too: a tab loading turned a second ring the whole time it loaded, and the window was drawn again for it at every frame. `python3 Tests/idle_animations.py`
- Reddit's promoted posts, in the feed, under a post before its comments and in the sidebar, are hidden by the ad blocker, on reddit.com only; switching the blocker off, or off for reddit.com, brings them back.
- A click on the tab you're on in the column opens its address to type in again, and a double-click on a pin (or Change Letter) changes its letter: in 1.0.16 both closed the moment they opened. The copy of the column kept out of sight for switching spaces drew a second field for the same tab, which took the keys from the one on screen; a group's name being changed could lose them the same way. `swift test --filter SidebarPressTests`
- Stylus's button opens its popup, and the popup opens its style manager. In 1.0.15 the popup stayed at its first frame ("Toggle styles" and ⚙), so the click seemed to do nothing: Stylus reaches its worker over a port handed to `navigator.serviceWorker`, which got through from 1.0.16 on ([#1258](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1258)). A probe now checks it with Stylus from the Chrome Web Store: `python3 Tests/extension_stylus.py`. Reported by email
- Settings (and History and Search's other pages) in Split View stays in its own pane. In a pane narrower than Settings needs, it was drawn past both edges of its half: over the page beside it, and over the tabs column, whose titles showed through with Glass on. A probe checks it from pictures of a hidden window: `python3 Tests/split_settings_pane.py`. (idea 833)
- A two-finger swipe between spaces slides once. With the trackpad set to swipe between pages with two fingers, the same stroke can also come as a swipe between pages, told as its glide ends; from 1.0.16 it was ignored only within 0.6 s of the fingers' last move, so after a quick stroke's long glide it slid on to the space after (or the card for a new one). The glide now counts as the stroke. A probe makes the trackpad's events up, glide included, in a hidden window: `python3 Tests/space_swipe_once.py`. (idea 834)
- Typing in a page no longer offers the grey rest of a word at the caret ("would you m" → "ind") with Correct Spelling Automatically off. That was macOS's inline predictive text, not autocorrect: WebKit shows it in every page whenever System Settings › Keyboard › Text Input › Show inline predictive text is on, as it is unless turned off, while Chrome and Arc never do. It now has its own switch, Edit › Spelling and Grammar › Show Inline Predictions, off unless turned on, and counting over the Mac's setting in every page and after a relaunch. `python3 Tests/autocorrect.py`
- YouTube videos start at once on a return visit with Shield on. From the second visit YouTube schedules an ad before the video, and once Shield had taken it out the player held the video at 0:00 with its spinner for 5 to 30 seconds; Search now starts that first video again through YouTube's own player, which plays it in about two seconds, ads still blocked. `python3 Tests/youtube_hold.py` loads videos again and again in a hidden window. (idea 852)
- With the tabs across the top, the window moves from above the tabs too, and a double-click there does what a title bar's does. The row's scroll view reached up into the title bar and took every press over the tabs' run, so with a full row only its two ends were left to take hold of. Reported by [@zhitongwu](https://github.com/zhitongwu) ([#1321](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1321))
- A download into a folder Search can't write to asks where to save it, as Ask where to save does, instead of stopping at zero KB or failing with "Cannot create file": a downloads folder that's read-only, gone, or one macOS keeps Search out of in System Settings › Privacy & Security › Files and Folders. Reported by [@jolly-0806](https://github.com/jolly-0806) ([#1291](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1291))
- Hide Elements (⇧⌘H) hides on sites whose security policy limits styles, as many large sites' does. It said the element was hidden and the page kept showing it: what was taken off went into a stylesheet such a site turns away. `python3 Tests/hide_elements_csp.py` checks it. Reported by [@yuri-urban](https://github.com/yuri-urban) ([#1270](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1270))
- The extensions button at the foot of the column sits centred between its neighbours: the puzzle's drawing leans almost a point left in its own box, and looked pulled toward the space's icon. Reported by [@xenonwellz](https://github.com/xenonwellz) ([#1281](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1281))
- A double-click along the top of the window fills the screen and stays there, instead of snapping straight back, and a double-click in the empty run beside the tabs opens a new tab without also filling the window. Since 1.0.16 kept the window movable over the top for window managers, macOS answered the title bar's double-click as well as Search; the strip now takes the double-click itself, so only one answers. `swift test --filter DragStripDoubleClickTests`. Thanks [@ricardo-rp](https://github.com/ricardo-rp) ([#1338](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1338))
- Switching spaces with ⌃1–⌃9 or the space's menu changes the window's colour at once. It waited for the next space's tabs to slide in and settle, up to most of a second, before the colour began to change. Thanks [@petty](https://github.com/petty) ([#1339](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1339))

## 1.0.16 — 20 November 2026

### Fixed

- The window moves from more of its top: below the tabs and the buttons across the top, not only past the last tab, and above and below back, forward and reload in the column; a double-click there does what a title bar's does. With the tabs down a side, the band along the top of the page that moves the window is half again as tall. Thanks [@BaraaAwwad](https://github.com/BaraaAwwad) ([#1257](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1257))
- Resizing pages no longer flashes a dark strip at a page's edge in dark mode, with the divider between split pages, the window, or the column sliding in or out. WebKit draws a page at its new size a frame or more after its view is resized, and until then the new room showed the web view's own background, dark grey in dark mode, chasing the hand at a white page's edge. While the pages change size, and for half a second after, that room shows the page's own colour instead, as in Safari. Captured frame by frame dragging the divider between two white pages, 28 of 38 frames showed the strip before and none after; resizing the window, 57 of 78 before and none after. The main thread's work per divider move is about the same (0.46 ms median before, 0.51 ms after). Thanks [@jakobbingham](https://github.com/jakobbingham) ([#1250](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1250))
- Settings, History or another of Search's own pages beside a web page follows the divider as it is dragged. It kept its old width until the button came up, leaving the window's ground between it and the divider, or was cut off by it. The stage now tells such a page where it is as it lays out, and only when one is in the pair: two web pages work as they did. Thanks [@jakobbingham](https://github.com/jakobbingham) ([#1251](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1251))
- Zotero Connector starts: its button saves the page to Zotero and its settings show, where the button did nothing and Settings was empty ([#1236](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1236)). An extension's page that hands its worker a port, through `navigator.serviceWorker.ready` as Chrome's documentation shows or through `controller`, now reaches the worker, and the worker hears what the page says on that port, where only the worker's side got through. Zotero still saves a page as a web page rather than reading it with its translators. `python3 Tests/extension_worker_ports.py`. Thanks [@fuoar](https://github.com/fuoar) ([#1258](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1258))
- The ad blocker is on from a launch's first request. The list is prepared in the background as Search opens, and the tab brought back in front started loading 50–150 ms before it was ready, so that page's first ad and tracker requests went out unblocked. The first pages after a launch now wait for the list, at most a second, once; after that nothing waits. Thanks [@jakobbingham](https://github.com/jakobbingham) ([#1248](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1248))
- A middle-click on a Google Drive folder opens it in a tab behind the one you're on, as a middle-clicked link does. Drive's folders aren't links: the page opens the folder itself, and that tab came to the front. A window a page opens for a press of the middle button now goes behind, as in Chrome, and with ⇧ it comes forward. `python3 Tests/middle_window.py`. Thanks [@ryadhiprasetya](https://github.com/ryadhiprasetya) for reporting ([#1243](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1243))
- A tab dragged into the pinned rows, with folders in pinned rows on, no longer leaves the blue line where it was let go standing until the next drag. Thanks [@quantum-strike](https://github.com/quantum-strike) for reporting ([#1245](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1245))
- Dragging the column's edge keeps up with the pointer again. Every space's tabs, kept out of sight for a swipe between spaces, were laid out again at each width the edge passed through; they now wait until it is let go. The page slides with the edge instead of being laid out again at every width, and takes its new size once you let go. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1268](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1268))
- Settings › Privacy › Clear › Cache lets a site's new version in when its service worker kept the old one. It cleared the disk and memory caches but not Cache Storage, where a worker keeps its own copy of the site's pages, so the worker went on answering with the old version through any number of clears. Cache Storage now goes with the rest, and a tab left open gets the new version on its next reload. The workers stay, with the notifications and offline data they hold; Sign out of everything still removes those. `python3 Tests/clear_cache_workers.py`
- uBlock Origin's Manifest V2 build says in Settings › Extensions, and as it installs, that it can't block requests here: WebKit doesn't let extensions block them with `webRequestBlocking`, so only its filters inside pages work, and uBlock Origin Lite is the one that blocks. Its timers now run, where every one was refused: an extension's `alarms.create` takes a callback last, as Chrome's does. And a menu entry with a link pattern WebKit can't read (uBlock Origin's `abp:*`) is made with the patterns it can, where it failed and the rest of the menu was never made. `python3 Tests/extension_load_failure.py`. Thanks [@djenttleman](https://github.com/djenttleman) for reporting ([#1208](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1208))
- An extension's request rules load more as Chrome takes them: an empty list of sites to leave out leaves out none, `\d`, `\w` and counts in a pattern are written out the way WebKit reads them, and a pattern beside a list of sites is pinned to the site it opens on. `python3 Tests/extension_dnr_shapes.py`. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1168](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1168))
- A long list of accounts under a sign-in box scrolls: with many accounts for one site it ran past the bottom of the window and the last ones couldn't be reached. It now stops at the window's edge and scrolls under its footer, goes above the box when there is more room there, and follows ↓ and ↑ down a long list. `swift test --filter AccountListRoomTests`. Reported by [@motionless-isdev](https://github.com/motionless-isdev) ([#1215](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1215))
- Switching spaces is quick and smooth with many tabs, with two fingers, ⌃1–⌃9 or the space's menu: the tabs slide in already scrolled to the tab you were on, and the page comes in with them, where the column stalled, came in at the top and jumped down. Quick runs of strokes, short flicks and a quick stroke back all count, and the card for a new space takes a longer stroke. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1213](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1213))
- A popover's arrow is drawn whole near the edge of the screen. With the window down to the foot of the screen, the bookmarks and extensions lists at the column's foot had their arrow bent into the popover's rounded corner, and the same happened to a popover above or under a button near the screen's left or right edge. The arrow now moves along the popover's side, just enough to stay clear of the corner, and a list beside a button too near the foot of the screen to point at it opens above it instead. The bookmarks list's arrow is the colour of the list too, where it was lighter. With the column on the right, its lists open toward the page. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1188](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1188)), and [@egegokdenizduman](https://github.com/egegokdenizduman) for reporting ([#1167](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1167))
- Signed in to Affinity Pathfinder once, you are signed in on every company's site and in Gmail, as in Chrome, instead of signing in again on each site. Pathfinder keeps its sign-in in its panel's localStorage, and WebKit keeps a framed page's storage per website. An extension's pages framed in websites now share one localStorage: what one writes the others read as they open (opening once more if they had already read an older value) and hear about at once, with a `storage` event, as Chrome's would. The copy they share sits in the extension's session storage, which its content scripts, in the website's process, can't read, and which never shows it (not in `get`, `getKeys` or `getBytesInUse`); it is written in batches, and gone when Search quits until a panel writes again. A page in a private tab keeps its own localStorage, as before. `python3 Tests/framed_storage.py`. Thanks [@etienne-asc](https://github.com/etienne-asc) ([#1063](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1063), [#1086](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1086))
- ⌘1–⌘9 pass over a folded group whose tabs all hold no page, closed or not opened since launch, as they already passed over an open group's closed tabs; its heading shows no number while you hold ⌘. A folded group with one open tab still counts as one place. `swift test --filter TabShortcutHintTests`. Thanks [@Karthikxp](https://github.com/Karthikxp) for reporting ([#1186](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1186))
- An extension can read a blob URL it created from its own content script, where WebKit would not fetch that address from the page. The bytes stay inside the extension: a website or another extension still cannot read them. `node Tests/ExtensionBinary/run.cjs`, `python3 Tests/ExtensionBinary/live.py`. Thanks [@rixct](https://github.com/rixct) ([#1058](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1058), [#1148](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1148))
- An extension that is switched on but couldn't start says so in Settings › Extensions, with WebKit's reason under its name, and installing or reloading one that doesn't start says why. It looked like any other, with nothing behind the puzzle button. A Manifest V2 background page that stays open, uBlock Origin's for one, isn't the reason on this WebKit: it starts. `python3 Tests/extension_load_failure.py`. Thanks [@djenttleman](https://github.com/djenttleman) for reporting ([#1208](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1208))
- ⌃⌘F puts the window in full screen and takes it out again, as on every Mac. The View menu Search builds had lost the system's Enter Full Screen item, and the key with it; it's back at the foot of View. `python3 Tests/full_screen_key.py`. Thanks [@jaylotw](https://github.com/jaylotw) for reporting ([#1176](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1176))
- An extension's message to one of its own pages keeps its reply when a relayed copy arrives first, where Search dropped the native message and the sender waited until it timed out. When every other open page answers empty, that message comes back at once. `node Tests/ExtensionBinary/relay.cjs`, `node Tests/ExtensionBinary/replies.cjs`. Thanks [@rixct](https://github.com/rixct) ([#1058](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1058), [#1147](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1147))
- Downloads, wherever it opens, draws the lines between its files from one side of their box to the other, and a list longer than its room fades out at the foot instead of stopping halfway through a file. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1190](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1190))
- In a small window, Back and Forward go back and forward in its page, and Previous Tab and Next Tab go to the other small windows. They changed the tab in the browser's window behind it. ⌘[ and ⌘] reach the page first, so an editor in it can outdent and indent; and with a command moved to an arrow key (Back on ⌘←), that key moves the caret while you type. Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1119](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1119))
- Extensions that set themselves up when they're installed now do. WebKit told a fresh install as a browser start, or handed the event over while the extension was still loading the file that listens for it, so the extension never heard it; the first extension installed in each launch always missed it. `python3 Tests/extension_install_event.py`. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1165](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1165))
- An extension's new tab page, with Settings › Tabs › Show the address on the tab you're on, shows its own title on the tab, as a new tab does, where it read "Extension page". Its other pages still say they aren't a website. Thanks [@fl0wstate](https://github.com/fl0wstate) for reporting ([#1214](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1214)) `python3 Tests/newtab_extension_title.py`
- With the column hidden until the pointer reaches the edge and the window kept off the screen's edge, by a tiling window manager's gaps for one, a pointer flung against the screen's edge brings the column out at once. It waited for the pointer to stop, and started the wait again with every move as the hand settled. A pointer passing through to a display on that side, or bringing out a Dock hidden on that side, still stops first. Thanks [@lars-1987](https://github.com/lars-1987) ([#1170](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1170))
- A space signed out of the others keeps its own history. A page visited there showed up in every other space's history and address suggestions; it now stays in that space's, and goes when the space is deleted. Spaces signed in with the first one still share its history. Clear History clears them all; History's Clear… can clear one space's instead, chosen beside Clear as Safari chooses a profile, shown only while a space keeps a history of its own. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) for reporting ([#758](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/758)) and [@aaravmaloo](https://github.com/aaravmaloo) ([#1166](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1166))
- Search no longer crashes on macOS 27.2 (26B5091g) when opening a menu, closing or reopening tabs, or on its own a little after launch. Built with the macOS 27.2 SDK, comparing two dates called into a part of Foundation that this build of macOS says it has but doesn't; Search now compares dates itself. Thanks [@sailybaev](https://github.com/sailybaev) ([#1201](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1201))
- Extensions can ask for one site covered by the optional host permissions in their manifest after a click, even when async work loses WebKit's user gesture: Search compared the site to the manifest's wildcard as a literal string and refused it as undeclared (adding a site in Timvis, for one). Sites outside the declared hosts are still refused, and access still asks you first. `swift test --filter ExtensionSiteGrantTests`. Thanks [@isaacbraun](https://github.com/isaacbraun) ([#1217](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1217))
- ⌘+ and the site card's + zoom a PDF as far as the bar over it does, six times its actual size, where they stopped at three: on a wide window that left the page not much wider than the window. ⌘0 brings a PDF back to its actual size, and zooming a PDF no longer changes the size its site's pages open at.
- The floating Downloads card, by the downloads button, is as tall as what it lists, up to half the window: an empty list is a short card instead of a quarter of the window of nothing, and a download coming in or the list cleared changes its height as it stays open. Beside the column, its left edge is no longer cut off flat: its window now leaves its shadow room on the button's side too, up to the button and never over it.
- The column, or the tab bar across the top, hidden with ⌘S stays hidden after you quit and open Search again: it came back shown at every launch. Each window keeps its own, a window brought back with ⇧⌘T comes back as it was, and a new window (⌘N) hides its tabs if the window it came from had them hidden. Switching between the column and the tab bar still brings the tabs back out, and Settings' hide-the-column-until-the-pointer-reaches-the-edge works as before. `python3 Tests/fold_keep.py`
- Hiding the column with ⌘S no longer flashes a dark block on the far side of the window on a page that takes a while to draw at its new size (a large web app, a page that works when the window changes size). The column waits for the page to be drawn wider, but only for a moment; past that it slid away with the room the page grew into still undrawn, shown in the page view's own ground (black in the dark theme, white in the light) until the page caught up. That room is now covered in the page's own colour until it has been drawn, with the column on either side, edge to edge or as a card, the strip across the top and Reduce animations alike. And ⌘S with the column out over the page (the pointer at the window's edge brought it out) keeps it where it is: it went away for a frame, the page bare with the window's buttons over it, and slid out again. `python3 Tests/fold_cover.py`
- Settings › Appearance › Slide the column in and out does something with Reduce Motion on in System Settings › Accessibility › Display: the column fades in and out where it stands, as Apple's own sidebars do, for ⌘S and for the column brought out at the window's edge. Under Reduce Motion it came and went at once whether the switch was on or off, so the switch seemed to do nothing; the setting now says so. Search's own Reduce animations still makes it immediate, and without Reduce Motion the slide is as it was (0.26 s out, 0.22 s back, against about 0.1 s with the switch off). `python3 Tests/slide_column.py`
- A long query keeps the caret and its last letter in sight at the end of the field: in a new tab's field in the column, in the field ⌘T floats over a page and in ⌘L's. Scrolled to the end, the text stopped a fraction of a point from the field's edge, and on macOS 27 the caret and part of the last letter were cut off there; the fields now keep a few points clear past the caret. `python3 Tests/field_caret.py`
- Edit › Spelling and Grammar › Correct Spelling Automatically switched off stays off in every page, open or new, and after a relaunch. Search switched WebKit by flipping it whenever its saved default disagreed with the choice, and once the two had come apart every flip went the wrong way: the menu said off while pages went on correcting. It now sets where WebKit stands, whichever tab is in front.
- Window managers can move and size the window from its title bar again: Swish played its snap on a swipe across the top of the window and nothing moved. The window told macOS it couldn't be moved whenever the pointer was over the strip of tabs or anywhere over the column, so that a tab dragged along the row wouldn't take the window with it; now it does so only over something in the title bar a press would pick up — a tab, a button in the row — and is movable over the strip's empty run, the lights, the band along the top and the column. `python3 Tests/window_managers.py`
- Teams on the web shows a channel's Shared tab: it said "We can't show this content here, your browser enforces security restrictions". The tab is SharePoint's page in a frame, which with Prevent cross-site tracking on has to ask for its own sign-in there, and Search never answered: the request was left hanging. Now a site framed in another that asks for its cookies, after a click in it, gets a question over the page, as in Safari — "sharepoint.com wants to use its cookies and website data on microsoft.com" — and has them only if you allow it. Prevent cross-site tracking is unchanged for every other site and frame. `python3 Tests/storage_access.py`
- With Search hidden (⌘H), a link from another app opens in its small window alone, as it does after a launch: the browser's windows came up beside it. macOS shows a hidden app, and brings it in front, a moment before it hands it the link, so Search no longer knew it had been hidden; a link now counts as having come while hidden when the app was shown less than a second before. ([#1175](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1175)) Thanks [@AsafDafna](https://github.com/AsafDafna) ([#1255](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1255))

### Added
- Right-click a tab group's heading in the column and choose Colour… to give the heading and its tabs a faint wash. Choose None to clear it. Thanks [@Shreyasdbz](https://github.com/Shreyasdbz) ([#1238](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1238), [#1237](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1237))
- Sync with iCloud, off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Your browser: your open tabs, pins, spaces, bookmarks and a few settings, with Search on your iPhone (in beta on TestFlight) and your other Macs. Through your own iCloud, end to end encrypted; private tabs, history, passwords, cookies and sign-ins never go, and nothing leaves the Mac until you turn it on. Another Mac's open tabs show under History and in the address field.

- Handoff, off unless you turn it on in Settings › General: the page in front comes up in your iPhone's and iPad's app switcher, and at the end of the Dock on your other Macs, as Safari's does, and opens there in the browser. macOS carries the address between devices signed in to your Apple Account; Search sends nothing itself. Never a private tab or a space signed out of the others, and only web pages. The other way, a web page picked up from your iPhone or another Mac opens in Search when it is your default browser, where macOS opened Safari; only http and https addresses are taken. `swift test --filter HandoffTests`. Thanks [@francescovaglia](https://github.com/francescovaglia) ([#1256](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1256)) and [@battlemountain2](https://github.com/battlemountain2) ([#1235](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1235))
- The page's full address can stay in sight, to type in: Settings › Appearance › Window › Always show the address bar, off unless turned on. It sits at the top of the column, or before the tabs across the top, Small or Medium, with a button for the site card at its end. A click there types an address or a search in place, with ⌘L's suggestions under it; ⌘L still opens the field over the page. Search's own pages show theirs. ([#1227](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1227), [#1240](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1240)) Thanks [@BaraaAwwad](https://github.com/BaraaAwwad) ([#1254](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1254))
- Glass, as in Arc and Zen: the tab column or the row across the top, the bookmarks bar and the frame around the page can let what is behind the window through, blurred, while the page stays as it is. Settings › Appearance › Window › Glass, off unless turned on, frosted or, from macOS 26, Liquid Glass. The glass takes the space's colour or gradient; how see-through it is, from richly coloured to clear glass with no colour, and how much grain are two sliders in the space's menu › Colour…. Thanks [@BaraaAwwad](https://github.com/BaraaAwwad) ([#1253](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1253))
- Liquid Glass tab, from macOS 26: the tab you're on under a pane of Liquid Glass that glides to the next one, bending the tabs it passes over, stretching with its speed and settling with a slight overshoot (straight onto the tab under Reduce Motion). Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Liquid Glass tab, off unless turned on. Thanks [@BaraaAwwad](https://github.com/BaraaAwwad) ([#1253](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1253))
- Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Buttons at the top puts the column's bottom buttons (spaces, profile, bookmarks, downloads and extensions) beside back, forward and reload, wherever they fit. In a column too narrow for them they go back to the bottom as you drag its edge, and come up again once there is room. After a space switch, its name shows at the bottom of the page. Off by default. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1268](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1268))
- Open in Small Window, in a tab's menu and the Tabs menu, puts the tab in the small window that links from other apps use, beside the browser's window. Open in Search (⌘O) puts it back in the row. Closing the small window closes the tab, and ⇧⌘T brings it back; after a quit it is back in its space's row. Not for a pin or a private tab. Only with Settings › General › Open links from other apps in a small window on. ([#874](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/874)) Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1119](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1119))
- Dim sleeping tabs, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Memory: a tab put to sleep after time away or with Put to Sleep fades its title and icon until you go to it, or the pointer is on it. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#1131](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1131))
- High contrast text, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Tabs: tab titles, pin letters and the headings of groups and pin folders in full black or white instead of soft grey, in the column, across the top and on pinned squares. Thanks [@elia-orsini](https://github.com/elia-orsini) ([#1218](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1218))
- The accounts under a sign-in box answer the keyboard, as in Safari and Chrome: ↓ lights the first and moves down, ↑ moves up and from the first goes back to the box, and Return fills the one lit, as a click would (⇧Return puts one of your card's rows into the box alone). Until an arrow lights a row, Return sends the form and ↑ moves the caret as before. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1199](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1199))
- Asking which space a link from another app opens in, off unless you turn on Settings › Spaces & Pins › Ask which space for links from other apps: a link that no Send links to a space rule matches shows a small panel listing every space by profile, and nothing loads until you choose. 1–9 and 0 open it at once in that space with its sign-ins, Return opens the marked one (the space you chose last time for that site, otherwise the space in front), Space turns on Always open that site's links here, which saves an ordinary Send links to a space rule, and Esc cancels and goes back to the app the link came from. The page then opens in the small window or in a tab, as Settings says. It covers email links once a webmail is chosen, a link that launches Search, and Search hidden (⌘H), where only the panel shows and Esc hides Search again. `python3 Tests/link_chooser.py` ([#1175](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1175)) Thanks [@AsafDafna](https://github.com/AsafDafna) ([#1255](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1255))

### Changed

- View › Actual Size (⌘0) still goes to the page zoom set in Settings › Browsing › Pages › Page zoom, as in Safari, and now says so when that isn't 100%: "Actual Size (95%)". At 100% it reads Actual Size, and the title follows the setting as you change it. `python3 Tests/actual_size_title.py`.
