Thanks [@flow-digital-ny](https://github.com/flow-digital-ny) for reporting ([#1210](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1210))
- The highlight in the Settings sidebar slides to the section you pick, as the live tab's slides in the tab column, with a little give at the end. It jumps as before with Reduce Motion on. Thanks [@aaravmaloo](https://github.com/aaravmaloo) ([#1169](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1169))
- A two-finger scroll sideways in Google Sheets, or over anything else on a page that scrolls sideways, scrolls it instead of going back or forward a page, as in Safari. A swipe means Back or Forward only when nothing under the pointer can go further that way and it was already resting at its edge when the fingers set off: a box carried to its edge by the same swipe, or by its glide, is not a swipe, and neither is a quick flick across a busy page's grid, which used to go before the page had said the scroll was its own. A page with nothing to scroll sideways swipes as before. ([#1219](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1219), [#1184](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1184)) `python3 Tests/swipe_scrollers.py`

## 1.0.15 — 18 November 2026

### Fixed

- A page you bookmark (Add This Page, ⇧⌘B) goes into the folder you picked last on a bookmark's card, as in Safari and Chrome, instead of the top level every time. Each profile remembers its own; a page already kept stays where it is, and with the folder gone it's the top level again.
- With Profiles in one window on, switching to another profile keeps you on the Mac desktop you're on. A profile's window came back on the desktop (macOS Space) where it was last shown, so after moving the window to another desktop each switch threw you back and forth between the two, unless every profile's window had been dragged to the same one. The window coming in now joins the desktop of the one it replaces, at its size and place; afterwards it stays there like any window.
- An extension's popup opens from its keyboard shortcut with the column or the strip hidden (⌘S), as Raindrop.io's does. Folded away, the tabs keep the extensions' buttons in the window, slid out past its edge, and the popup hung from its button there opened off the window, where you never saw it; it now hangs from the button only when the button is in sight, and otherwise opens where the buttons would be. `python3 Tests/extension_folded_popup.py`
- Extensions no longer open their welcome or setup tab again when Search is relaunched (Raindrop.io, Dark Reader, Grammarly…). Safari's engine tells an extension it has just been installed whenever it loads more than five seconds after the browser started, as one does at launch with several installed, or switched on in Settings; Search now says "install" only once, when you install it, "update" for a new version or a Reload, and nothing on a launch, as Chrome does. One installed in the first seconds after launch is now told it was installed, where it heard nothing. `python3 Tests/extension_installed_reason.py`
- Capture… takes the element you click. The click that picked it still reached the page once the layer was gone, so a link or a button clicked to be captured was followed or pressed as the picture was taken, and a link or an image pressed could be picked up by WebKit to be dragged, which ended the press with no capture at all. Both are now kept from the page. The outline under the pointer is drawn in your accent colour with a light and a dark ring, and filled a little more in dark mode, where it was near-black and hard to see on a dark page. `python3 Tests/capture.py` checks it.
- Signing in to X works with Settings › Passwords › Offer passkeys off. Search took the passkey object away from pages but left its name on the window, so X's sign-in, which looks for a method on it after you enter your name, stopped there with an error instead of going on to the password. The object now stays and says there are no passkeys here, as Safari's does on a Mac with nothing to sign in with: sites asking for one are refused at once and go on to the password, and a request under the name field no longer waits for good. `python3 Tests/passkeys_off_signin.py` checks it.
- SoundCloud's Sign in works with Privacy Badger on. Privacy Badger blocks soundcloud.com everywhere but on SoundCloud's own pages; Safari's engine judged a frame by the frame itself, still empty when it starts loading, rather than by the page holding it, as Chrome does, so SoundCloud's own sign-in frame was blocked on soundcloud.com and the button did nothing. An extension's rule for some sites' requests now treats frames as Chrome does, which also blocks frames a rule names that got through before. `python3 Tests/extension_initiator_frames.py` checks it.
- Signing in with a passkey from 1Password no longer leaves the site on its sign-in page when you take more than a few seconds to pick it. While 1Password showed its menu or a prompt in another tab, that tab answered for 1Password after ten seconds, with nothing; the passkey you then picked never reached the site. Any extension's long answer, a password manager's or another's, now waits for the extension itself.
- Giving an extension a permission it asks for, or changing where it runs, no longer runs its scripts a second time in the pages you have open (doubled buttons and listeners). An extension that gives back access to every website now really stops running on the sites it no longer has, and keeps the ones its manifest names.
- Claude in Chrome's side panel is no longer blank once you're signed in with a paid plan. Its panel is now claude.ai itself in a frame, and WebKit sends a frame inside an extension's page none of claude.ai's session cookies, so the frame stayed signed out and empty while the panel thought you were signed in. Search now gives the panel the message claude.ai sends when it can't be shown there, and the panel shows its own chat instead, signed in as the extension is. `python3 Tests/claude_panel_frame.py` checks it.
- Voice-over-Translation's requests to its translation service go out with the headers it sets for them, as in Chrome; Search sent them without, and the service turned them away. More generally, an extension's own header rules now apply to its requests that Search sends for it, and a rule naming a header Safari's engine doesn't know loses only that header instead of being dropped.
- Language Reactor and Ambient light show on YouTube again. They add a script of their own to the page, which YouTube's security rules refused in Search; as in Chrome, Search now runs an extension's own script there, when the extension's manifest lets that page load it.
- Language Reactor, and any extension whose background is a page rather than a worker, is no longer restarted every few seconds for no reason.
- An extension that downloads a file into a folder, as "photos/2026/a.jpg", gets that folder inside Downloads, as in Chrome, where only the file's name was kept.
- Cosmos's window now opens, from its button in the toolbar and from the one on an image. Its background loads a common add-on (webextension-polyfill) that, in Search, cut it off from your clicks.
- Eagle's extension saves images to the Eagle app again, and 好采助手 saves Xiaohongshu images to it: an extension's background can now talk to an app on your Mac that its manifest names (Eagle's, on localhost), as in Chrome. Eagle's extension also stopped half-way through starting, on a check that only works in Chrome, so it never even asked the app.
- Pages load faster with extensions on. Every frame of a page read Search's whole 230 KB compatibility layer once for each content script an extension runs there, and once more in the page's own world, where it does nothing. Content scripts now carry only the part they run (70 KB), the page's own world none of it, and a second copy in the same frame is skipped. Measured on a page with 20 frames: the time an extension shaped like uBlock Origin Lite adds to each load fell from 39 ms to 17 ms, and with the real uBlock Origin Lite the page loads in 79 ms instead of 90.
- On a space with a colour or a gradient, the open tab, the tab under the pointer and the pins' squares take the space's tint, where they sat on it as grey patches.
- Passwords from Apple's Passwords app should ask for a new six-digit code far less often: Search now keeps Apple's helper running for as long as you're connected, telling it now and then (and when the Mac wakes) that a tab came to the front, as Apple's own extension does, so a sleep or a quiet hour no longer lets the pairing go. If it still asks, the reason is noted in the Mac's log for us to look at. Reported by [@mehdiillala](https://github.com/mehdiillala) ([#1122](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1122))
- An extension's box opened over a page from a key, as Vimium's Vomnibar (T, o) is, takes what you type straight away, where it opened unfocused and the keys went nowhere until it was clicked. WebKit refuses a frame from another site focus until it has had a key of its own, so the first key now puts the focus where the extension asked before it lands. Thanks to the person who reported it with a full diagnosis.
- Split View no longer draws a frame round the page the keys go to, as a card or edge to edge: it read as a stray border on one page of two.
- The line under the pins, with Clear at its end, waits for a tab with a page under it: over nothing but the empty tab a window starts with, it was a line with nothing to keep apart. New Live Folder… is in a square pin's menu too.
- View › Edit Site Style… can be made larger: drag its bottom corner and the CSS editor takes the room, down to a sensible least size and never past the window. It opens at the size you left it; a double-click on the corner puts it back. `python3 Tests/site_styles.py` checks it.
- A pin closed, put down or put to sleep while its page was still loading no longer spins on its row for good. The page's view was torn down after what listened to it, so its last "done loading" was never heard.
- Folders in pinned rows are as tall as the tab rows beside them, their folder icon as large as a tab's icon and their name as large as a tab's title, and they follow Settings › Appearance › Roomier tabs. They were a smaller group heading's size. `python3 Tests/pin_folders.py` checks it.
- Opening a pin folder opens it in place. When it held more pins than the column had room for, the column started scrolling and jumped down to the tab you were on, and the folder's own row had to be scrolled back to. `python3 Tests/pin_folder_scroll.py` checks it.
- A folder in the pinned rows can be moved by dragging its row, as a pinned row is: let go on the top of another folder's row to put it before that folder, on the rest of it to put it inside, or among the pins of a folder (or at the top) to move it there. Nested folders move the same way, and a folder never goes inside itself or deeper than three levels. Before, only the right-click menu could move one. `python3 Tests/pin_folders.py` checks it.
- Hiding the tab column with ⌘S no longer flashes a black or white bar along the far side of the window. The page was given the column's room as the column started to slide, and the part of it the web view had not drawn yet at its new size came in from the far edge. The page now takes its room first, with the column still over it, and the column slides once the page is drawn, a few hundredths of a second later. ⌘S pressed again in that moment leaves the column where it is. `python3 Tests/fold_flash.py` checks it.

### Added

- Settings › General › Sync with iCloud, off by default: Search on your iPhone and your other Macs share this Mac's pins, spaces, bookmarks, search engine and look through your own iCloud, end to end encrypted, and see each other's open tabs under History › Tabs on Your Other Devices and in the address field, as Safari's iCloud Tabs, opened only when you pick one. Private tabs, history, passwords, cookies and sign-ins never go, and nothing leaves the Mac until it's on. Not together with Sync with your other Macs. It needs a build signed for iCloud; until then Settings says so. `python3 Tests/icloud_sync_off.py` checks that it stays off and idle.
- Settings › Spaces & Pins › ⌘1–⌘9 switch spaces: the space keys and the tab keys swap places, as in Arc, so ⌘1–⌘9 go to your spaces and ⌃1–⌃9 to your tabs. The tab numbers shown while the key is held, the Shortcuts page and the hints follow. Off unless you turn it on. Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › Shortcuts now lists the tab keys too, and pressing ⌘1–⌘9 for another command there says why it can't have them instead of only “Can’t be changed”. `python3 Tests/space_command_numbers.py` checks it.
- Settings › General › Email links open in: Gmail, Outlook.com, Fastmail or Proton Mail instead of your mail app. An email (mailto:) link clicked in a page opens the webmail's new message in a tab beside it, with the addresses, subject and text filled in, and from a private tab in an ordinary one, where you're signed in; Send Feedback goes there too. With a webmail chosen, Make Search open email links (macOS asks you to confirm) sends links from other apps there as well, in a tab or the small window for links. Off unless you choose one; a link that reaches Search with none chosen goes on to your mail app. `python3 Tests/webmail.py` checks it.
- Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › From any app › Open in a small window: Return in the address field raised from any app opens the page in a small window over the app you're in, as Arc's ⌥⌘N opens Little Arc, with Open in Search to keep it. Off unless you turn it on; a page already open, picked from the list, still goes to its tab. `python3 Tests/launcher.py` checks it.
- A right-click anywhere in the tab column has a menu, as in Arc: the empty space under the tabs, the gaps between groups, the New tab row, the band at the top and the foot round its buttons offer New Tab, New Folder and New Live Folder… (with folders in pinned rows on), Paste and Go when the clipboard holds an address, Hide Sidebar and Sidebar Settings…. Before, only a tab, a pin or a group had one. Tabs, pins, groups and folders keep their own menus. `python3 Tests/column_menu.py` checks it.
- Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › ⌥-click a link opens it in Split View: the link's page opens beside the one you're reading, as the link's right-click Open Link in Split View does. Off until you turn it on, since ⌥-click downloads a link in Safari; off, a ⌥-click follows the link as before, and ⌥ with ⇧ or ⌘ keeps its peek or ⌘-click. `python3 Tests/option_click_split.py` checks it.
- Choose where each extension runs, as Chrome's Site access: Settings › Extensions › an extension's Runs on menu picks All sites (as before, and the default), Some sites (the ones you list, with their subdomains) or On click (only once you press its button, on that tab's site until it leaves it). An extension kept off a site isn't put into its pages at all, so they stay as light as without it. Right-click an extension's button to run it on this site once or always.
- Settings › Tabs › Same tabs in every window: every window lists the same open tabs in each space, as Arc's sidebar does and as the pins already were. A tab opened, closed, moved, grouped or renamed in one window is so in the others; each window shows the page on its own, so one tab can be on screen in two windows, and a tab picked in one window wakes where another window left it. Private tabs stay in their window, and closing a window leaves its tabs in the others. Off unless you turn it on.
- Settings › About › Developer › Let coding agents use Search: Claude Code, Codex or another agent on your Mac can ask to browse in the window you have open. Each time one connects, the window asks you first, unless you ticked “Always allow” for it. Nothing is drawn over the page while it works: the tab it is working in wears a small dot on its icon, moving with it and gone once it is idle or done, and Stop is on that row, on a quiet line at the foot of the column (or at the end of the tab strip) and in File › Stop Agent for as long as it is connected. One that stops asking is shown idle, and let go after five minutes. It works in tabs of its own; your tabs it can read only if you tick “Let it see my open tabs”, and never uses, and private tabs it never sees. Password, card and one-time-code values never reach it, those Search fills included, and pictures black them out along with every frame; it can't download, choose files, use the camera, microphone, screen or location, or open Settings or Search's own pages, and Allow takes a click of yours in the window. It connects through a file only you can open, never the network: `claude mcp add search -- /path/to/Search/search-mcp`. “Always allow” is offered only when the program that started the agent is Claude Code's own `claude`, signed by Anthropic; Search remembers that program's code signature, not the name the agent gives, checks it again on every connection, and keeps it where only Search can write. Let in that way, the window says so, it never sees your tabs, and Stop means it is asked again from then on, until you tick “Always allow” for it again; it runs out 30 days after you granted it or 7 days after it was last used. Forget one in Settings › About › Developer, where each shows when it runs out; turning the setting off forgets them all. File › Stop Agent is ⌃⌥⌘., and a tab an agent opened keeps a faint ring on its icon. Off unless you turn it on.
- Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › Bounce at the top and bottom: pages stretch past their ends and spring back, as in Safari. Off unless you turn it on.
- A space can wear a gradient instead of one colour, if you like. The space's menu › Colour… opens one small panel with everything a space can wear: its icon on its look at the top, then the colours and None, a few ready-made gradients, and a gradient of your own (two or three colours, which way they run, a faint grain). The window changes as you choose; click elsewhere to keep it, or press Escape to put back what was there. A gradient washes the tabs, the bookmarks bar and the ground around the page as faintly as a colour does, and goes with the space when you switch or swipe. If that is too quiet for you, the panel's Strength makes a colour or a gradient Rich or Vivid: a deeper tint in light mode and a deep, saturated colour in dark mode, closer to Arc's themes, with the tab titles, the open tab and the pins kept as readable as on Subtle, which stays the default. Spaces without one look exactly as before, and it syncs with your other Macs.
- Spread two fingers on a peek to open it as a tab, as zooming into it: the panel grows a little with your fingers, the trackpad taps once when letting go will open it, and a lighter tap when your fingers come back under that. Let go past it and the panel grows to the whole page and simply is the tab, with no flash. A page already zoomed in keeps its pinch.
- Settings › Appearance › Slide the column in and out: the tab column comes out on a quarter-second ease-out and goes back on a slightly shorter ease-in, as Arc's does, for ⌘S and for a column that hides until the pointer reaches the edge alike, the window's buttons riding with it. The page slides with it and is laid out again only once, as before. Off by default: the column keeps its short, quick slide. `python3 Tests/slide_column.py` checks it.

## 1.0.14 — 17 November 2026

### Fixed

- ⌘1–⌘9 count only the open tabs of an open group: a grouped tab holding no page, drawn dimmed as closed, is passed over as ⌃Tab passes it, where ⌘1 opened a group's closed tabs before the open ones below. A folded group is still one place. Thanks [@Karthikxp](https://github.com/Karthikxp) for reporting ([#1159](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1159))
- A content script an extension registers while it runs gets the same compatibility shim as one named in its manifest, before that script captures fetch or runtime. The page's own world still gets only the passkey patch. ([#1156](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1156))
- An extension that opens its popup itself when its button is clicked shows it, as Inoreader does in its Extended mode. Inoreader sets the popup, opens it and sets it back to none in one go, and Search, asked to show it a moment later, found none and showed nothing: the click only seemed to turn the button on and off. The popup is now taken as it is when the extension asks. Thanks [@varyform](https://github.com/varyform) ([#1155](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1155))
- ⌫ no longer goes back a page, as in Safari: pressed outside a text box, or in a search box a page built its own way, it left the page and what you had typed. Back is ⌘[, or the key you chose in Settings. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1161](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1161))

### Added

- Keep the card in full screen, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Window: the page stays a card with room around it when the window fills the screen, as in Arc and Zen, where it goes edge to edge. Asked for by [@ctrl-raj](https://github.com/ctrl-raj) ([#1152](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1152))
- Live folders in pinned rows, as Arc has them: right-click a pinned row and choose New Live Folder…, give it a feed's address (RSS or Atom, or a site's address that points to one — a GitHub repository's releases.atom works too), and the folder fills itself with the twelve latest posts, each opening in a new tab. It refreshes every half hour while Search runs, or with Refresh in its menu, fetched without any of your cookies or sign-ins. Part of Folders in pinned rows (Settings › Spaces & Pins).
- Your own style for a site, as Arc's Boosts: Edit Site Style… in the View menu opens a box for CSS of yours, worn by every page of the site you're on, as you type it and from the first frame of each page after. Done closes it, Clear takes it off. CSS only: Search never runs script you write in a page. Not written from a private tab, though one wears a style written elsewhere, as it does what you've hidden. The sites with a style are listed beside the setting, each to remove. No key until you give it one in Settings › Keyboard & Mouse. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing › Pages › Your own style for a site; turned off, every style is kept but none is worn. Asked for on [#1127](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1127)
- A space with a colour now washes the window in it too, faintly, as Arc's space themes do: the tabs' column or the tab bar, the bookmarks bar and the ground around the page take on a hint of it, a little more in dark mode, and change with the space you're in. Never the page itself. A space without a colour looks exactly as before.
- ⌘-click on a pinned tab moves a wandered page into the loose tabs and opens a fresh tab for the pin at its pinned address, or opens a new tab at that address when already at home. Off unless turned on in Settings › Spaces & Pins › ⌘-click on a pin opens a new tab ([#1142](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1142)). Thanks [@DHIRENSACHER](https://github.com/DHIRENSACHER) ([#1143](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1143))

## 1.0.13 — 17 November 2026

### Fixed

- ⌘T's tab is in no group, whatever empty tab is lying around: ⌘T took an empty tab left in a group for its own, so the page you typed next opened in that group, from a tab in none. Thanks [@kurinawa](https://github.com/kurinawa) for reporting ([#1151](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1151))
- A window just opened with ⌘N is the one the menus act on: a bookmark chosen from the Bookmarks menu opened in the window behind it until you clicked away and back. Thanks [@shubhammore121](https://github.com/shubhammore121) for reporting ([#1146](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1146))
- A link from another app that opens in a small window while Search is hidden (⌘H) comes alone. Shown again for it, Search brought back every window it had, so the browser's window came up beside the small one as if the link had opened twice; it now stays away until Open in Search, the Dock or ⌘N asks for it, as after a launch made by a link. A browser window brought forward by anything else while they are kept away goes back off screen.
- Save to Notion's popup sees that you are signed in to Notion, where its "Log in" only took you to your Notion workspace. An extension's own page asking a site it has been granted, without asking for credentials, now sends that site's cookies, as Chrome does; Search sent them only when the extension asked for credentials outright.
- The downloads card floating by its button keeps Resume, Retry and Remove on one line each, where a failed download's buttons broke in the middle of the word, and its shadow is lighter.
- Profiles in one window stays one window in full screen. Each profile's window kept a full-screen space of its own once it had been on screen, black in Mission Control, and a swipe went from one to the other as separate windows; the window you leave now comes out of full screen and the next one takes its place in it.
- The floating video closed from another tab comes back at its full size in its own tab. Back on its tab, YouTube showed the video at the little window's size in the corner of the player until it went full screen or the page was reloaded. Thanks [@gxlactuss](https://github.com/gxlactuss) ([#1140](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1140), [#1141](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1141))
- Control Panel for YouTube saves what you change in its popup. Its page starts with a `browser` of its own, and Search had fixed that name in place for every extension page, so the page stopped before any of it ran and its switches did nothing. Thanks [@ahmedtarekxo](https://github.com/ahmedtarekxo) for reporting ([#1124](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1124))
- Closing or moving the tab a peek was opened from closes the peek. When that tab was the only one, or moved to another window or space, the peek stayed over the next tab. Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1120](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1120))
- The Bookmarks menu stays still and keeps your bookmarks while a tab's title changes many times a second, as a live trading chart's price does. Its title flickered at each change, and opened at the wrong moment it could show only its first three items. Thanks [@shubhammore121](https://github.com/shubhammore121) for reporting ([#1137](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1137))
- With New tab in a floating field on, ⌘T held down leaves the field as the press left it, up or put away. Each of the key's repeats put the field away or raised it again, so it flickered and stayed wherever the count left it.
- With the window on a display other than the Mac's first one, extensions find the tab they name again: a private tab reads `incognito: true` to an extension let into private tabs, and tabs.move, tabs.discard and the other calls that find a tab by its place work. WebKit gives a window's frame turned over by the height of the window's own screen, and Search turned it back by the first screen's.
- A side panel's page goes with its window when the window closes for good, instead of staying in memory unseen.
- Site icons no longer keep growing in memory as you browse, and an unusually large icon stops downloading rather than filling memory before it is turned away. Clearing History lets their cached pictures go too; open tabs keep theirs.
- A session that runs for days keeps about the 2,000 places in memory that the history file keeps, the latest ones always among them, where memory held every page it had seen and the address field searched all of them on every key.
- A peek waiting for its page no longer keeps the window busy drawing its loading light, even if the page never finishes loading.
- Copy Address and Print in the card under the address, and Settings' own text, show the keys you set in Settings › Keyboard & Mouse, where they showed the default ones.
- A saved site shortcut can be edited: click it in Settings to change it, where it had to be deleted and added again.
- A custom search engine copied from another browser with {searchTerms}, or typed without https://, is used instead of falling back to Google.
- The pointer is an arrow over the strip you drag the window by, where it could stay a text cursor from the page (#1094).
- The ⌃Tab switcher no longer offers pins you put down or grouped tabs you put away (#1092).
- In a dark window, the first visit to a tab after launch no longer flashes white before the page shows. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1114](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1114))
- A tab in a group whose page is put away, with the minus at the end of its row or ⌘W, dims, as a pinned tab put down does. The row looked just as it had, so the minus seemed to do nothing, and a second click closed the tab.
- A shortcut set to an arrow moves the caret while one is in a text field, and runs the command only when nothing is being typed. Back on ⌘← and Forward on ⌘→ went back and forward in the address field, a panel, and a box on the page; so did any command on ⌘ or ⌥ with an arrow, ⇧ included for selecting. ⌘T, ⌘W and the other letter shortcuts are unchanged. ([#1064](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1064))
- Dusk no longer holds ever more memory on a page that keeps replacing its style sheets, where a page's process grew to several gigabytes and restarted in a loop. Dusk's dark copy of a sheet goes as soon as the page takes the sheet out, where every copy was kept until the page went quiet, which such a page never does; and a page that still swaps sheets past a generous budget is darkened by the filter instead, until it is reloaded. Reported by [@2cristo7](https://github.com/2cristo7) ([#740](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/740))
- Signing in to Affinity Pathfinder works again, in its panel on a website and in Gmail. Since 2.88 its panel and the script on the page talk through its worker, which sends the panel a tab's message by its frame. WebKit brings a tab's message to content scripts only, and Search handed it to the extension's own frames too only for an extension that asked for webNavigation, which Pathfinder doesn't: the panel never heard back, and a finished sign-in went nowhere. The worker now knows those frames by hearing from them: each says it is there as it opens and that it is going as it closes, so a tab whose panel is gone answers at once. `python3 Tests/framed_messages.py` checks it.
- An extension's page in a website's frame can open a window again, as Affinity Pathfinder's sign-in does from its panel: since #872 the stand-in for windows.create made for Claude in Chrome took the place of the one a website's frame gets, which asks the extension's worker, so the panel called WebKit's own and WebKit ended the website's process. A website's frame keeps its own. `python3 Tests/embedded_windows.py` checks it.
- A pin you put down shows its icon in the ⌃Tab switcher, not the page it had before, and its row no longer offers a − (#1050).
- With Profiles in one window on, switching to another profile and quitting no longer opens a second, empty window at the next launch (#1060).
- A long address that history completes in the address field keeps the start in view, so what you typed and the site stay visible; it used to scroll to the end (#1055).
- Settings, History and the other pages of Search draw their symbols in English on a Mac set to another language: the Tabs heading's icon read 大小 in Chinese (#1049).
- Move to Group in a tab's right-click menu moves every tab picked with it, as dragging them onto a group does, where it moved only the tab clicked.
- An extension's right-click items for a picture, Are.na's Add to Are.na for one, show on pictures that aren't links: with an extension that adds such items, the page keeps WebKit's menu there, whose Copy Image and Download Image are Search's own.
- With more than one window open, a click in the puzzle button's list of extensions opens the extension or pins it, where it only closed the list: every window's list followed one shared switch, and the click closed them all.
- Waking an extension's worker no longer starts WebKit afresh on the main thread, which could hold the window still for seconds. Reported by [@aminezar](https://github.com/aminezar) ([#1037](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1037))
- Native helpers an extension starts, 1Password's for one, are stopped when the extension's worker starts again, where each new worker left the last one's running: dozens in an afternoon. Reported by [@aminezar](https://github.com/aminezar) ([#1036](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1036))
- With the column hidden until the pointer reaches the edge, swiping to another profile's space keeps the column out while the pointer is on it.
- ⌃Tab, ⌘T and ⌘1–⌘9 on a video playing full screen leave full screen and do what they say, where before they acted on the window behind it unseen. Reported by [@hmehrafarin](https://github.com/hmehrafarin) ([#1042](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1042))
- A link that opens a new window from a page in a small window (X's links out, for one) opens in a small window of its own, not as a tab in the main window. Reported by [@hmehrafarin](https://github.com/hmehrafarin) ([#1041](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1041))
- Swiping to a space with more tabs than the column shows keeps its pins and space buttons in place: the column no longer grows past the window for the length of the swipe and jumps. Reported by [@lightsound](https://github.com/lightsound) ([#1024](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1024))
- ⌃Tab finds a link you opened behind the page, with ⌘-click, the middle button or Open Link in New Tab: it has its card as a tab you just used, where before it came after every tab you had been on and, past nine, had none. Turn on Settings › Tabs › ⌃Tab goes to a link opened behind to have it right after the page. Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1027](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1027), [#1028](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1028))
- A tab dragged out of its group and let go among the tabs in no group leaves the group and lands where it was let go, down the column and across the top; the tabs picked with it and a Split View pair go with it, and a group left empty goes. Asked for by [@vincent-caetano](https://github.com/vincent-caetano) ([#1009](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1009)). Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#1021](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1021))
- A link ⌘-clicked inside a peek of a private tab opens in a private tab, as one from the private tab itself does. An extension reads your installed fonts, or your Mac's processor, memory and displays, only if it asked for `fontSettings` or that `system.*` permission, as in Chrome. Thanks [@D3OXY](https://github.com/D3OXY) ([#1045](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1045))
- Closing a tab or a window no longer crashes Search on some versions of macOS 14 and 15: WebKit called an empty callback as it put the page's media away. Reported by [@wagonza](https://github.com/wagonza) and [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#1031](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1031))
- With New tab in a floating field on, ⌘T over a tab in a group opens its new tab outside the group, where ⌘T's tab goes with the setting off. It joined the group of the tab the field was raised over. The same for an address typed into ⌘K over a grouped tab; ⌘Return from a grouped tab's address still opens in its group. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#1014](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1014))

### Added

- Folders in pinned rows, as in Arc's pinned list: a folder holds pinned rows and other folders, up to three deep, folds and opens with a click, and is the space's own, the same in every window. Right-click a pinned row for New Folder or Move to Folder, or drag it onto a folder; a folder's own menu renames it, makes one inside, moves it into another, or deletes it, handing what it held to the folder above. Bringing Arc over keeps its folders nested, as folders of rows rather than tab groups, and the import sheet offers the switch. Off unless you turn on Settings › Spaces & Pins › Folders in pinned rows, beside Pinned rows; turned off, the rows come back flat and their folders wait. A pins file written with folders still reads in earlier versions, which keep the pins and drop their folders.
- Address bar commands (Settings › Spaces & Pins › Opening and starting, off unless turned on) work as Arc's Command Bar: besides open tabs, history and bookmarks, the field offers every menu command by the words of its name — "hide el" for Hide Elements…, "bookm bar" for Show Bookmarks Bar, "split" for Split Current Page — and every Settings page by its name, each row with its key as the menu shows it. They sit under the search, so Return still goes where it went; one whose feature is off, or that the menu greys out on this page, isn't offered.
- Split View holds up to four pages side by side, as Arc does. A tab dragged onto the edge of a page that is already split, Open in Split View (Add to Split View on a split), Open Link in Split View, a peek kept beside and ⌥-click each add the page to that split beside the page it went to, and the pages are made even, where a third page used to take the other one's place and leave it a tab of its own; Tabs › Add Page to Split View puts an empty page beside the one you're in. Each divider drags on its own, between the two pages either side of it, and a double-click evens those two. ⌘W closes only the page you're in and the others stay together; a split left with one page is a tab again, and ⇧⌘T puts a closed page back where it was. ⌃⌘← and ⌃⌘→ go one page at a time, Remove from Split View in a page's menu takes that page alone out, and Separate Split Tabs and Close All Pages act on the whole split. Three or four tabs picked in the column go side by side with Open 3 (or 4) Tabs in Split View. In the column a split is a line for each page, across the top one item a little wider for each, and each space keeps its splits after a restart. A split of four takes no more pages. Sessions written before open as they were
- Sync with your other Macs, in Settings › General: choose a folder your Macs share, in iCloud Drive or Dropbox for example, turn it on with the same folder on each Mac, and your spaces, pins, bookmarks and a handful of settings (look, search engine, site shortcuts, tab and privacy choices) follow you. Each profile syncs on its own, and a space with sign-ins of its own brings only its name, icon and colour, never its sign-ins. Never your history, passwords, cookies or open tabs. No account and no server: each Mac writes a small file of its own into that folder. Off until you turn it on.
- History › Archived Tabs…, as Arc's archive: the tabs Search closed by itself (Settings › Tabs) stay together for 30 days, up to the last 500, newest first under each day, with a search over their titles and addresses. A click opens one again in the space on screen; Remove and Clear take them out. Only what Search closed, never a tab you closed yourself or a private one, and each profile has its own, emptied with its history. With tabs never closing, the menu doesn't offer it.
- Capture… in the View menu takes a picture of part of a page, as Arc's Capture does: drag across an area, click an element, outlined under the pointer as Hide Elements outlines it, or choose Whole Page for the page from top to bottom. Escape puts it away. The picture is copied, on this Mac's clipboard only, and saved as a PNG in your downloads folder; nothing goes anywhere else, and a private tab's copy is kept out of clipboard managers' history. No key until you give it one in Settings › Keyboard & Mouse. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing › Pages › Capture pages.
- The address field from any app, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › From any app: a key of your own raises it over the app you're in, with the suggestions ⌘L has, and Return opens what you typed in Search or goes to the tab that already has it. No key until you give one. Asked for in [#783](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/783) Thanks [@itallovidal](https://github.com/itallovidal) ([#783](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/783), [#1123](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1123))
- Pin Tab and Pin Tab as Row can have keys of their own, in Settings › Keyboard & Mouse. On a pinned tab, either key unpins it; with pins as rows off, Pin Tab as Row pins a square. No key until you give one. Thanks [@ViktorBejtic](https://github.com/ViktorBejtic) ([#1126](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1126))
- A colour for each space, from the space's menu › Colour: blue, green, orange, red or violet. The space's icon wears it, on a faint wash of it, and so does its entry in the menu, so you can tell at a glance which space you're in. None until you pick one. Thanks [@pPeterle](https://github.com/pPeterle) ([#1115](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1115), [#1116](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1116))
- Chrome's colors in the Web Inspector, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Light and dark: a flat tab strip with the open tab underlined, and DevTools' colors for the selected row, the type and the code. The panels, the icons and the tools stay Web Inspector's. Keep the Web Inspector dark, beside it and off unless asked for, keeps the tools dark while Search is light; the row is there only while Search isn't set to Dark. Either one reaches an inspector already open, from the View menu or from Inspect Element in the page's own menu. Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1129](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1129), [#1130](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1130))
- A peek can stay with the tab it was opened from: another tab hides it, and coming back shows it. Off unless you turn on Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › Peek stays with its tab. While it is hidden, its video, sound, camera and microphone wait, and so does any question it asks. Escape, its cross, or closing, moving or putting away its tab closes it, as does going to another space. Only one peek is open at a time: a new one closes a hidden one. Thanks [@heychintan](https://github.com/heychintan) ([#1120](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1120))
- Leave pinned tabs out of the ⌃Tab switcher, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs: the switcher then shows your other tabs, and the pinned tab you're on. Thanks [@DHIRENSACHER](https://github.com/DHIRENSACHER) ([#1110](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1110), [#1112](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1112))
- Shorter tab bar, in Settings › Appearance › Window: with the tabs across the top, the bar is only a few points taller than its tabs, 36 points where it is 52, and leaves the rest to the page. The window's three buttons stay on the row's middle. Off unless turned on.
- Minimize the floating video, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing. A button tucks the window into a corner, and a click on that button brings it back. Close, and the arrow back to the tab, stay as they are.
- Reduce animations makes everything in Search's interface happen at once: the tab ⌘1–⌘9 picks is lit and shown with no glide, a space, a panel or a page's first frame comes without sliding or fading, for Search alone and whatever Reduce Motion in System Settings says. Off unless you turn on Settings › Appearance › Motion › Reduce animations.
- ⌘Z right after closing a tab brings it back, as ⇧⌘T does and as Safari undoes a close. Only while closing it is the last thing you did: type or click anything else, or close a window, and ⌘Z undoes as before; a page that uses ⌘Z itself, such as a document or a drawing, keeps it. Off unless you turn on Settings › Tabs › ⌘Z reopens a closed tab. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1134](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1134))
- ⌘S can make the column icons only instead of hiding it, so the tabs stay in sight; ⌘S again brings the titles back. Off unless you turn on Settings › Appearance › Tabs › ⌘S makes the column icons only. Not with the tabs across the top, nor with the column hidden until the pointer reaches the edge. Asked for by [@vincent-caetano](https://github.com/vincent-caetano) ([#1008](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1008)). Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#1015](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1015))

### Changed

- A new Search starts with its tabs down the left, as Arc has them; ⇧⌘S or the welcome puts them across the top. If you already use Search, your tabs stay where they are.
- Links from other apps open in a small window on a new install (Settings › General › Open links from other apps in a small window). Anyone who already has Search keeps them in the browser's window, as before, until they turn it on.
- Shield keeps its compiled block list between launches instead of compiling it again each time.
- The floating video's window asks the page how far through the video is only while the pointer is over it, where it asked twice a second behind your back, and the page's check that the video is still the one floating runs when the page changes or a video starts playing, and every two seconds, where it ran four times a second.
- An extension listening for tab group changes, or for the Mac going idle, is told when it happens, where Search had it ask for the groups every 3 seconds and for idleness every 15, for as long as it listened, each ask waking its worker. With nothing listening, nothing runs.
- History reads and prepares its file away from the window, so a long history no longer holds up the first window opening.
- Switching tabs shows the page sooner: the page goes into the window as the tab is picked, where it waited for the rest of the window to be redrawn first. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#1113](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1113))
- Waking a tab that slept, with ⌘1–⌘9 or a click, brings its page up sooner: the page is asked for as its view reaches the window, not when a timer next looks, and the picture of the page as it was left comes off a moment after the new one has drawn, where it stayed up for half a second after the page arrived whatever the page had done. A page that draws late still has it off at half a second.
- A page whose title keeps changing, such as an inbox counting its unread mail, no longer rewrites the whole history file each time it ticks over. History writes a page's first title at once, and a later one with the next visit or when Search quits. Thanks [@D3OXY](https://github.com/D3OXY) ([#1043](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1043), [#1044](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1044))
- Pulling the column's edge, or the side panel's, saves the width once, when the edge is let go, instead of at every step of the pull, which every open window redrew for. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1035](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1035))
- Leaving a tab with a paused video no longer floats it: the float is for a video that is playing. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1034](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1034))
- A tab's row in the column, a pin and a tab across the top redraw only when something of their own changes, not with every change to the window. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1033](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1033))
- Typing into Find redraws only the find bar, not every tab in the column. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#1032](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1032))

## 1.0.12 — 9 November 2026

### Fixed

- A tab across the top can be dragged again with Tabs give when pressed on, in Settings › Appearance › Tabs: its press held on to the mouse however far the hand went, so the drag never began and the window moved instead. A tab carried past either end of that row stays in sight instead of being cut off, and one carried off the row or the column goes with the pointer, as its icon and title. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1011](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1011))

### Changed

- Pages with many frames load quicker with an extension that declares several content scripts: the small script Search gives each extension is read once per frame, not once per script. With 1Password, a page with 20 frames read it 63 times. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#1013](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1013))

### Added

- Your contact details under a form's boxes, from your own card in Contacts, as Safari fills them: under a box for your name, email, phone or address, a row per address, email or number on your card. A click fills that box and the form's other empty boxes you can see, keeping a shipping address and a billing one apart; a shift-click fills that box alone, and the boxes filled flash in your accent colour unless Reduce Motion is on. Boxes a page hides away get nothing. Only your card is read, held in memory and never saved, and nothing reaches the page before you pick. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Passwords › Fill in your contact details, which is when macOS asks whether Search may read Contacts. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1010](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1010))
- Open All in New Tabs at the foot of each folder in the Bookmarks menu, as in Safari, and in a folder opened from the bookmarks bar: the folder's bookmarks, each in a tab of its own. Right-clicking a folder in the bar already offered it.

## 1.0.11 — 9 November 2026

### Fixed

- A window opens with the keys on the page in front, so typing and the arrow keys reach it without a click. With Keyboard navigation on in the Mac's Keyboard settings, Reload took them, with a focus ring around it. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1001](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1001))
- ⌘-click on a link opens it in a new tab even on sites that handle clicks themselves. Some shops, Zara Home's for one, opened the product in the same tab, whatever key was held.
- The floating video no longer flickers as it pops out. Its little window opened on a picture of the video with whatever the page drew over it, the player's controls, its watermark, captions or a dialog, and those vanished a moment later as the live video took over; the picture is now of the video alone, as the window then shows it. Reported by [@Rahuletto](https://github.com/Rahuletto) ([#543](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/543))
- Closing a window straight after using it, with ⇧⌘W or its red button, no longer takes away the pins of its space in every other window. The window waited a moment before saving its tabs, and a save that came after it had closed found no tabs and told the other windows the space had no pins. `python3 Tests/close_window_pins.py` checks it. Reported in [#750](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/750). Thanks [@tobyL05](https://github.com/tobyL05) ([#983](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/983))
- A passkey is offered only to the site the tab has open: a page's own request is checked against the address Search shows, as a frame's already was, not only against what the page says it is. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#988](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/988))
- The Mac's system log no longer records which sites you sign in to with a passkey, private tabs included. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#989](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/989))
- A PDF saved from the PDF bar, or a page saved with Save Page As…, is marked as downloaded from the internet, like any other download, so macOS checks it when first opened. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#990](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/990))
- Saved names, passwords and codes are handed to the page's form filler as values, never written into the script that fills the form. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#992](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/992))
- Extensions' WebSockets keep no cookies, so one extension's socket no longer sends what another's was given. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#993](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/993))

### Changed

- Search keeps up when many tabs are open. Typing in the address field, swiping between spaces and a background tab changing its title no longer redraw every tab and the menu bar: with 60 tabs a key takes about 7 ms instead of 89, and a swipe step under 5 ms instead of 70. Thanks [@kemuri](https://github.com/kemuri) ([#1000](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1000))
- A site shortcut from Settings › Browsing › Searching is offered as a site chip while you type its keyword, like the built-in sites: Tab puts it in the field, and what you type next searches it. Asked for in [#1004](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1004). Thanks [@ugozlave](https://github.com/ugozlave) ([#1005](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1005))
- Show how far you've read is off unless you turn it on, in Settings › Appearance › Tabs: a tab half filled with grey read like a page stuck loading. It stays on for anyone who turned it on themselves. Asked for by [@aminezar](https://github.com/aminezar) ([#994](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/994))
- The tab column draws no scroll bar when its tabs run past the window, as the tabs across the top already don't, even with macOS set to always show scroll bars. The wheel, the trackpad and the keyboard scroll it as before. Thanks [@TheOmkarBirje](https://github.com/TheOmkarBirje) ([#984](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/984))

### Added

- An HTTP or SOCKS5 proxy for the whole browser, in Settings › Privacy › Proxy, off unless you turn it on. Pages go through it, and so does what Search fetches itself: favicons, suggestions, extensions and the Web Store, the AI add-on and updates, in every space and in private tabs. A video call is the exception: WebRTC sends its own traffic over UDP, which a proxy tunnel doesn't carry. This Mac and .local names stay direct. A proxy that can't be reached fails rather than going direct, and its password is kept in the keychain. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#1007](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1007)), built on [@standby2025](https://github.com/standby2025)'s [#510](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/510)
- ⌥-click a tab to put it beside the page you're on in Split View, as Open in Split View does: in the column, across the top or on a pin. On the tab you're on, an empty page comes beside it; on a half of the pair on screen, that half leaves. Asked for in [#939](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/939). Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#1006](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1006))
- A pin's page can be changed to the one it is showing: right-click the pin and choose Replace Pinned Page with Current URL. Going back to the pinned page then goes there, in every window. Asked for in [#1002](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/1002). Thanks [@tobyL05](https://github.com/tobyL05) ([#1003](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/1003))
- Bring Things Over can bring the tabs a Chromium browser has open, Helium, Chrome, Brave, Edge, Arc and the others it lists: turn on Open tabs & tab groups for that browser. They come in asleep at the end of the space you're in, at most 300, its pinned tabs as pins and its tab groups as groups of the same names, folded as they were; tabs already here aren't added twice. Off unless you turn it on. Thanks [@timwuhaotian](https://github.com/timwuhaotian) ([#841](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/841))
- Send links to a space, as Arc's Air Traffic Control does: a site, its subdomains with it, opens in the space you choose, with that space's sign-ins, when a link to it comes from another app or a page in another space opens it in a new tab; Search switches to that space. Typed addresses, links followed in their own tab and private tabs stay where they are, and a rule whose space has gone is passed over. Off unless you turn it on, in Settings › Spaces & Pins, with Spaces on; add sites there, or right-click a tab for Always Open in this space. `python3 Tests/space_links.py` checks it. Asked for in [#595](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/595) and [#721](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/721). Thanks [@AdityaAmitK](https://github.com/AdityaAmitK), whose [#596](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/596) and [#722](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/722) it is built on

## 1.0.10 — 8 November 2026

### Fixed

- A space's menu says where its downloads go in one Downloads submenu: the folder in Settings or the space's own, ticked, and Choose Folder…. It showed two lines that read alike.

- A muted video now floats when you go to another desktop, as it does when you go to another app; one you paused yourself still stays in its tab. Thanks [@Comradevergreen](https://github.com/Comradevergreen) ([#791](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/791))

- A site's SVG icon with a dark look of its own, like ChatGPT's, wears that look when Search is dark. It was drawn in its light look, black on the dark tab bar and column. An icon kept before this update keeps its light look until it is a week old. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#917](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/917))

- Page translation stays quick on a Mac with Apple Intelligence: Search prefers the faster on-device translation model, as it did before macOS 26.4's SDK changed the default. Language pairs only the newer model supports still use it. Thanks [@2470046](https://github.com/2470046) ([#821](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/821))

- Keeping a bookmark or letting one go no longer stalls the window with many tabs open. Each one built every tab's row in the column again, though no row shows a bookmark: with 200 tabs it held the window for 146 to 188 ms, now 12 ms. The bookmarks bar still follows at once. `python3 Tests/bookmark_redraws.py` checks it Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#982](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/982))

- A tab carried past another stays under the pointer, in the column, the icon column and across the top. At every tab it passed it was thrown a place behind and glided back. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#979](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/979))

- ⌘S hides and shows the column as soon as it's pressed. Each press built every row of the column four times over before the slide could start, about 150ms with 30 tabs, as long as the slide itself; it now takes about 38ms, and the column that slides away is the same one that comes back. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#948](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/948))

- Dragging one of several tabs picked with ⌘- or ⇧-click carries them all: they land together where they're let go, in the order they had, Split View pairs whole, and dropped on a group they all go in. Only the tab under the pointer moved, since pressing it in the column brought it forward, which let go of the rest. A click on one of them still brings that one forward and ends the selection, now as the button comes up. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#947](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/947))

- An extension's popup opens in the window where its button was pressed, not another open window: button anchors are kept per window instead of in a single global map, and pressing an extension's button acts in its window. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#839](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/839))

- Disconnect Apple Passwords while a typed code is still being checked, then Connect again, and the new pairing waits for its own code. The old code's answer used to drop it, with "Apple's Passwords helper answered in a way Search doesn't know". Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#973](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/973))

- Pages that load their own `.bundle.js` files, GitLab's among them, are no longer left half-drawn with uBlock Origin Lite installed. One of its rules blocks a site's bundles only when the server answers with an anti-adblock header; WebKit has no way to read that header, skipped it, and blocked every site's bundles. An extension's rules that depend on a response's headers are now left out. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#858](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/858))

- A page visited, retitled or given its icon in the last second or two before quitting is still in History and keeps its icon. Search waited a moment before writing either, and quitting inside that moment threw the write away. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#971](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/971))

- A tab closed while it played video or music (YouTube, Twitch) went quiet only on screen: its sound could carry on in the background, and a stream could keep filling memory with nothing to show it in. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#943](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/943))

- A WebSocket an extension's worker closes before it has connected stays closed. It could open anyway a moment later, and say so to the extension, if the browser hadn't yet heard it was asked to open when the close came; a real WebSocket never opens once it is closing. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#969](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/969))

- Two extensions added from the store at once each keep showing "Adding…" until they are in. The first to finish took the spinner in Settings and the store page's "Adding…" away from the other, which looked done while it was still on its way, and one that turned out already installed could leave the spinner turning for good. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#968](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/968))

- Under a sign-in box, the accounts saved for that exact host come first, and the site's other accounts follow under a thin line naming the site, so a login for admin.example.com never passes for example.com's. Nothing is hidden, only ordered. Thanks [@tomaswarynyca](https://github.com/tomaswarynyca) ([#723](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/723))

- Going to another tab while picking things to hide puts the picker away. Clicking a tab in the column or across the top left the page before still picking, out of sight, and Escape then tried to put away a picker on the tab in front, which had none, so the one left behind kept going. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#966](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/966))

- ⌘K puts the tab switcher away after Tabs › Search Tabs… was clicked in the menu. It stepped down the list instead, and letting go of ⌘ then switched to the second tab in it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#959](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/959))

- When the page in the half of a Split View pair you aren't typing in crashes or is ended by the system, it loads again straight away, as the half you are in does. It stayed white, though on screen, until you clicked into it or went to another tab and back. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#955](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/955))

- A page's camera, microphone, location or notifications card goes away with its page: going to another tab, or the page going elsewhere or closing, refuses it, as the screen-sharing card already did. It stayed up over the next tab, and Allow gave the page you had left what it asked for. A screen-sharing question waiting for a tab behind no longer refuses every other page's camera, location and notifications until you go to that tab. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#954](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/954))

- A sign-in the page sends from its own script is offered a place in the keychain too: a "Sign in" link that posts the form, a box styled as a button, or a page that sends itself after a moment. Still only once the next page shows it worked. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#705](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/705))

- Translate Page left by going to another page before it had read the first one leaves nothing behind. The sign came back saying the page was still being translated, with nothing translating it, and stayed until the next page; if the language still had to be downloaded, the download sheet could come up over the page you went to. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#953](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/953))

- A tab dragged from the column onto the page splits it again, as Welcome and Settings say. Since tabs in the column open as the button goes down (#625), the page under the hand was the dragged tab itself, so nothing was shown at the page's edges and letting go only changed the page. Once the hand is over the page, the page that was there before comes back, and the split is shown and made with it. Letting go anywhere else leaves that page on screen, as before #625. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#928](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/928))

- With Split View on, a tab carried in the column or across the top and let go of with Escape stays put down until the button comes up. The next move of the hand picked it up again, shadow and all, under the pointer until the button came up. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#952](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/952))

- Going to another space with ⌃1–⌃9 or the space menu while the card for a new space is up puts the card away, as Escape does. The row changed but the card and its plus stayed, and if the card came from a tab's Move to Space › New Space…, a later Create carried that tab into the new space too. Typing "new space" in the address field now opens the card the way New Space… does. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#951](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/951))

- An extension that names nativeMessaging among its optional permissions, as Bitwarden does, can talk to its app on this Mac again: it was refused every call, so its unlock with Touch ID never reached its app. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#751](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/751)), and [@shahrushi2003](https://github.com/shahrushi2003) and [@2cristo7](https://github.com/2cristo7) for reporting ([#741](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/741))

- Close Both Pages is remembered as one, as Close Tabs Below is: one ⇧⌘T brings both pages back side by side, as the pair they were, with the keys in the page that had them. Each page used to be its own entry, so ⇧⌘T brought back one page alone, and a second ⇧⌘T the other, apart. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#930](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/930))

- An extension switched off in Settings while it was still starting stays off. The start already under way finished after the switch, so the extension ran and kept its button with its switch showing off, until the next launch; removing one mid-start did the same. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#950](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/950))

- A download cancelled while it is still stopping says "Cancelling…" in the Downloads panel, whether Cancel was pressed while it ran or while a pause was under way. It said "Pausing…", as if Cancel had paused it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#967](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/967))

- Passkeys follow the switch of the Search they run in: a build of your own started beside a release turned them off for the release's new tabs while its switch still said on. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#737](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/737))

- A tab moved to a space with its own sign-ins while it played sound no longer keeps its speaker once its page is let go. It went on saying "playing sound" with nothing behind it, so it never slept or closed itself. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#958](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/958))

- Closing a page that a link just opened in front goes back to the page the link was clicked in, while that is still the tab you were on before it. It opens at the end of the row, so with Closing a tab goes back to the one before off, ⌘W landed on whatever tab was last there. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#963](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/963))

- An update downloading when the hourly check can't reach the feed goes on to be ready. The check forgot it was on its way, so the finished download was never said to be ready, and the next check swapped it in again, deleting the copy of Search still running until it quit. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#949](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/949))

- A link that opens a new tab in front — a plain click on one that opens in a new tab, or a page's own window.open — from a tab in a group joins the group, at its end. It landed outside the group, at the end of the row, though Settings says a link from a group stays in it and a ⌘-click's tab already did. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#962](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/962))

- Settings › Tabs › New tab position says which links After current puts beside their page: the ones you ⌘-click. ⌘T's new tab and a page a link opens in front go at the end. It said "a link", and a plain click on a link that opens a new tab, the usual kind, went to the end. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#964](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/964))

- Reading mode brings the whole article on a page whose article cites its sources: links are weighed by how much of a box they are, not counted, so Wikipedia and MDN come whole, a comment section stays out, and a page that is only links still offers nothing to read. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#774](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/774)), and [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) for reporting ([#757](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/757))

- A page opened just as Search's look changes, light to dark or back, wears its icon once it has loaded. It was asked for its icon before it had arrived, found none, and showed its letter until the look changed again. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#942](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/942))

- ⌘L held down keeps the address field up. Each of the key's repeats put the field away or raised it again, so it flickered and stayed wherever the count left it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#976](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/976))

- Closing the assistant while the on-device engine is still starting no longer leaves it writing an answer for nobody, which kept the engine running until it was done. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#970](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/970))

- Cancel during the on-device model download puts AI back to off, ready to turn on again. It said the download failed, "cancelled", with Try Again, and the progress bar could come back after it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#970](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/970))

- A window closed while Search waits on an import to stop before quitting stays closed. Quitting already counted as begun, so the window was kept as if Search were quitting it, and it came back at the next launch. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#957](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/957))

- A video floating in Search's window stays the only one out after picture in picture is chosen in Settings. Leaving another video's tab, or Search, sent that one into picture in picture too, and both were up at once. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#956](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/956))

- A video playing in a window on another screen — a TV, a second display — no longer floats out when you work in another app on this one.

- A Split View pair carried in the column or across the top comes apart only where that was shown: rest over another tab and the half that stays dims, with the same tick as a split shown over a page, and only then does letting go take the other half out. Let go a few points past the pair's own edge on its way to a new place, it used to lose the half being held with no sign it would. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#887](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/887))
- The address field says where Return goes. ⌘T's floating field reads "New tab: enter a web address", ⌘L's "This tab: enter a web address", and ⌘K's "Switch to an open tab"; a blank tab's still reads "Enter a web address". VoiceOver hears the same. All three said "Enter a web address", though ⌘K only finds open tabs. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#913](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/913))
- An address or words typed into ⌘K that no open tab answers to are offered as a last row, "Open in a new tab", and Return opens them there. They replaced the page ⌘K was pressed over, though ⌘T's field, which looks the same, opens a new tab; ⌘T then ⌘K did the same. On a blank tab the blank still takes them. ⌘K with nothing to offer says "No other tabs open" or "No open tab matches" instead of showing nothing. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#911](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/911))
- ⌘W with the address field up over a page puts the field away and leaves the page, whichever key raised it, as Escape does. After ⌘L or ⌘K it closed the page under the field; only ⌘T's floating field was put away. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#910](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/910))
- A tab brought back with ⇧⌘T comes back as it was: Back and Forward go where they went before it closed, and the page is where it was scrolled to. It came back as its address typed in again, with no way back to the pages before it but History. The same for every tab a Clear took, for a tab that was asleep when it closed, and for the tabs of a window brought back with ⇧⌘T. Private tabs are still never kept, and nothing more is written to disk. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#895](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/895))
- ⇧⌘T brings back History, Settings, Downloads, Bookmarks and Passwords when one of them was the last tab closed, Settings at the section it was on. They were never remembered, so ⇧⌘T did nothing, or brought back an older tab instead. Still one tab of each: if it has been opened again since, ⇧⌘T goes to that one. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#897](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/897))
- Back and Forward stay off on a pinned tab put down with ⌘W and clicked again, as there is nothing to go back to. Back showed lit from the page before, and pressing it did nothing. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#896](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/896))
- With Reduce Motion on in the Mac's Accessibility settings, the last three things that still moved keep still. The glow under the field on an empty tab holds still instead of swelling and fading for as long as the tab is open, and stills at once if the setting is turned on while it is showing. Going to another space by swipe, wheel or mouse button changes the tabs in place instead of sliding them a column's width, and the space's icon changes without turning over. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#901](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/901))
- Carrying a tab along the row across the top, every tab of the row wears the faint grey of a pinned tab until it's put down, so the tab just passed still shows. It lay half under the carried one, its title under it too, and seemed gone until you moved on. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#899](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/899))
- Claude in Chrome can act in Search: the window it opens to work in gives back its own tab first, not the space's pins, so its tab group is made, and a group made in one window is found from any other, as in Chrome. Before, every action failed with "No tabs to group". Thanks [@introbseso](https://github.com/introbseso) for working out why ([#872](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/872))
- Clicking a notification sent by a page opens the tab that sent it, in whichever window it is now, not the first tab on that site. A tab that has closed, gone to another site or sits in a space not on screen falls back to the first tab on the site as before, and so does a notification from a service worker, which no tab sent. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) for the report ([#776](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/776))
- A PDF saved from a private tab with the download button on the PDF no longer leaves its name and site in the Downloads list, which is kept on disk. It says "Saved …" and is listed nowhere, like any other private download.
- A tab waking from sleep shows the page as you left it, at its size, while it loads. The picture kept of it is taken at the screen's pixels, two to a point on a Retina screen, and was read back as twice the page: for a moment as the tab woke it could show at twice its size, cropped to its top left. It now says how many pixels make a point. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) for reporting, with a recording that showed it ([#781](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/781), [#871](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/871))
- Going back to a tab whose video is still full screen in its own Space no longer leaves the window stuck without its column, on the last tab's page with an empty band beside it, until Search was quit. The video leaves full screen and the tab comes back in the window, with the column
- A tab carried along the column or across the top stays under the pointer as it passes a Split View pair, and changes places with the pair at its middle. It used to run a row ahead of the pointer for each pair it passed, and land a place or more from where it was let go. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#884](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/884))
- The page under the address field, ⌘T's floating field, the ⌃Tab switcher, the hidden elements' list, Welcome, What's New or the Find bar hears nothing of the pointer, as under a peek or the column since 1.0.8. Links under them lit up, hover menus opened and the pointer turned into a hand or a text cursor, though a click there never reached the page. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#878](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/878), [#877](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/877))
