- With a site put in the address field with Tab, the empty field says "Search Reddit" (the site's name) instead of "Enter a web address", and says that again once the site is taken out. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#880](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/880), [#888](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/888))
- Your search engine's suggestions in the address field wear its icon, as the search for what you typed above them already did, instead of a magnifying glass: every row that searches Google shows Google's. Until this Mac has the engine's icon from a visit, every search row keeps the glass. A site put in the field with Tab wears the same icon as its rows, where Wikipedia's showed a glass. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#879](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/879), [#881](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/881))
- Discord no longer signs you out when you reload it with the column out. Since 1.0.9 a page read the whole window as its own, column included, so the window looked more than 160 points wider than the page, which Discord takes for its developer tools docked open. A page now reads the window as wide as itself, as in Safari, and Google Docs stays sharp. Thanks [@aminezar](https://github.com/aminezar) for finding exactly why ([#916](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/916))
- Turning the blocker off for one site works again: it's now a switch in the site's card (click the tab you're on), Block Ads and Trackers, and the page reloads. The switch in Settings › Privacy only showed for the page in front, which with Settings open was Settings itself. Thanks [@ugozlave](https://github.com/ugozlave) ([#861](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/861))
- With Settings › Appearance › Tabs show: Letters, a tab's letter is beside its title in the column and across the top, as the setting says, not only on pinned squares. Thanks [@ugozlave](https://github.com/ugozlave) ([#919](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/919), [#860](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/860))

### Added

- The site card has Site Permissions: see and change what a site may use — camera, microphone, location and notifications — each Ask, Allow or Refuse. Camera and microphone are kept apart, and refusing one stops it in that site's tabs at once. Thanks [@2470046](https://github.com/2470046) ([#940](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/940))

- Verification codes from Apple's Passwords app come up under the box that asks for one. With Apple Passwords on in Settings › Passwords, pick an account and the code goes into the box, after Touch ID if macOS asks; nothing is filled on its own and Search keeps no code. Only on https pages, never in a private tab. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#855](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/855))

- A second engine one key away: with Settings › Browsing › Shift-Return searches with set, Shift-Return in the address field sends what you typed there, while Return keeps your default. Off unless an engine is picked. Thanks [@aminezar](https://github.com/aminezar) ([#856](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/856))

- The look and layout of the tabs, the sidebar and the window follow a change made from Terminal while Search runs, `defaults write com.officecommun.search sidebar.position -string right` and the like: the switch in Settings moves to match and does what it does when flipped there. Before, the running app kept what it read at launch until the next one. Privacy, security, passwords, extensions and Let a script drive Search are still read only at launch. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#946](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/946))

- With links from other apps opening in a small window, a link to a page already open can go to its tab instead (Settings › Browsing › Links › Go to a tab that has the page, off unless turned on). Thanks [@Comradevergreen](https://github.com/Comradevergreen) ([#798](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/798))

- Choose a page translation language in Settings › Browsing › Pages, independently of the Mac's language. System Default keeps the previous behavior; automatically translated pages use the same choice ([#747](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/747)). Thanks [@2470046](https://github.com/2470046) ([#821](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/821))

- A video in full screen no longer takes its window with it, if you ask: with Settings › Browsing › Keep the window in use during full screen on, the window on its own desktop keeps its tabs and the folded column while the video plays on, and you can browse in other tabs. Esc over the video lands it back in its tab. Off unless turned on. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#980](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/980))

- Recent places in the new tab, in Settings beside the floating new tab and off unless turned on: ⌘T's floating field offers the last three places from History before anything is typed, leaving out pages already open in the window, which ⌘K finds. Nothing is picked until an arrow key picks it, so Return on the empty field still opens nothing; nothing is asked of a search engine, and a private tab offers nothing. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#914](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/914))

- A tab carried over a page shows where letting go would split it: the page's two edges, faint, as soon as the tab is over it, with the split itself shown as before once the pointer rests on one. Over the middle of a page nothing splits, and nothing used to say where would. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#936](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/936))

- The empty page of a Split View pair finds your open tabs by what you type, as ⌘K does: those that answer come first in its list, and a click or Return brings the tab in beside the other page. Its list of open tabs shows the six last looked at and goes once you type, and the field offered only places to load, so a tab past those six came in as a second copy, loaded again. A page offered as an open tab isn't offered again from History. Only there: anywhere else, ⌘K is the key for open tabs. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#934](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/934))

- Settings › Tabs › Links opened in front go beside their page, off unless turned on, with New tab position on After current. A plain click on a link that opens a new tab, a page's window.open on a click, or a ⇧⌘-click puts its page right after the page it came from, in the order you click, as a ⌘-click's goes, while you go back and forth between the page and the tabs it opened. Otherwise they go to the end of the row, as before. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#965](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/965))

- What plays in another tab can show at the foot of the sidebar: its icon and title, with its pause, and a click goes to the tab. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Tabs. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#764](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/764))

- Copy Address in a download's right-click menu, while it runs and once it is done, for files downloaded from now on, to hand it to another download app. Clearing history takes these addresses with it; the files stay listed. Asked for by [@ejzpan](https://github.com/ejzpan) ([#853](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/853))
- With a site put in the address field with Tab, the list offers what you searched that site for before, newest first, read from your history on this Mac and sent nowhere; the last few show before you type, and none in a private tab. A site shortcut ("yt naruto") offers the same. With Google in the field and Google your search engine, Suggestions from your search engine shows its suggestions there too; words typed to any other site never go to your engine. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#880](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/880), [#882](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/882))

### Changed

- A link's small window opens where you last left it, at that size; if no screen shows most of that place any more, it opens centred. Thanks [@Comradevergreen](https://github.com/Comradevergreen) ([#789](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/789))

- The address field's history suggestions weigh how often you go to a place against how well it matches what was typed, so a page you use every day comes before one seen once, typo or not, and a site counts the visits to its pages too. A bookmark found only through a typo comes after where you have been. Typing in a long history is faster too. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#944](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/944))

- ⌥⌘N on a Split View pair puts it apart, both pages staying as tabs, with the one that had the keys on screen: the key that made the split takes it away, as ⌘T, ⌘L and ⌘K each put away what they raised. It went to the right-hand page, which ⌃⌘→ already does. On a pair, Tabs › Split Current Page reads Separate Split Tabs. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#932](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/932))

- Settings › Appearance › Motion › Save power in Low Power Mode (was Reduce animations) also lets idle tabs sleep after ten minutes at most while in Low Power Mode. Off by default. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#745](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/745))

- In Split View, the page your typing, Back and ⌘F go to has a clearer line round it: two points of mid-grey, where it was one point at a little over half strength. Against a white page it stood about 2:1 in light and 1.7:1 in dark, little more than the other page's own edge; now about 3:1 in both. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#938](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/938))

- The key that raised the address field puts it away: ⌘T again closes ⌘T's floating field, ⌘L again closes ⌘L's, and ⌘K pressed afresh closes the switcher, as Spotlight's key does. ⌘K held still steps down the list. ⌘T again used to leave the field up with what was typed cleared, ⌘L again put the address back over an edit, and ⌘K, let go, ⌘K switched to the second tab in the list. What was typed is kept: switching between ⌘T, ⌘L and ⌘K changes only where Return goes, and a ⌘T put away without opening anything comes back with its words, selected. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#912](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/912))
- The downloads button gives one quick pulse each time a new download starts, so a file that comes in fast still shows it began. A button just coming in has its usual scale-in instead, a resume or a retry doesn't pulse, and with Reduce Motion it holds still. Asked for by [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#830](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/830))
- A search's results page you have been to reads as the search it was in the address field's list, "naruto vs one piece" with Reddit beside it and the mark of a past search, instead of its address (reddit.com/search/?q=naruto%20vs%20one%20piece). It still opens that page, and an address being typed still finishes in grey as before. Your engine's page for exactly what you typed isn't shown twice. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#880](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/880), [#889](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/889))

## 1.0.9 — 4 November 2026

### Fixed

- An extension's own requests to a site it has been allowed on carry that site's sign-in, as in Chrome. Claude in Chrome's side panel said "Log in" while its tabs were signed in: WebKit held back the site's SameSite=Lax cookies from every request an extension makes. ChatGPT's side panel still shows signed out: it shows chatgpt.com in a frame, and frames wait for WebKit's own fix ([#837](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/837))
- Pinched in, a swipe back within the same page, after a footnote, a #section link or an address the page changed, lands where you were and lined up, no longer about 50 points past the page's left edge with a blank strip down that side, still at the footnote. The step back waits for the page's sides to stop stretching, about a quarter of a second; not pinched in, it goes at once as before. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) for the measurements ([#842](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/842))
- Darken light pages gets more of a page right: links, headings, prices and dates coloured like the text around them no longer stay dark grey, buttons a site marks as important no longer stay light, pages that set their colours on the page as a whole are darkened, and dark boxes with a light layer over them no longer turn grey
- A pin's right-click menu has Delete under Unpin: the pin is put away for good in one step, where it took Unpin, finding its row among the tabs and its ×. ⇧⌘T brings it back as a tab. Asked for by [@introbseso](https://github.com/introbseso) ([#834](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/834))
- A playing video follows when you switch spaces, just as when you leave its tab, if automatic floating is on. Returning to its tab brings it back into the page. Thanks [@2470046](https://github.com/2470046) ([#772](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/772), [#773](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/773))
- Reload Page (⌘R) and Reload Page From Origin (⌥⌘R) reload the page in a peek while it is open, leaving the page underneath alone. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#819](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/819))
- A pin square carried across the grid no longer stays floating mid-drag. Picking up a square that isn't the one on screen selects it, and when its page started loading under the hand, or its icon came in, macOS called the drag off without saying so: the square stayed up, shadowed, and the place it had opened among the rows stayed open, so a click there landed on the wrong tab. A drag called off now puts the square (or a row) down where it is, and the next click puts down any drag still left open. Thanks [@introbseso](https://github.com/introbseso) for the clear report ([#846](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/846))
- A page whose process dies as it loads no longer reloads itself over and over, or ends on a white page, until Search is quit. It is loaded again twice a minute at most, as the side panel's website already was; after that it says it kept crashing (or kept running out of memory), and Try again loads it fresh. Why each page's process ended, and what was done, goes to the Mac's own log: `log show --last 1h --predicate 'subsystem == "com.officecommun.search" AND category == "Pages"'`. Thanks [@timwuhaotian](https://github.com/timwuhaotian) ([#849](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/849))
- A link's right-click menu starts with Open Link in New Tab. WebKit's Open Link, above it, only did what a click on the link does, and taken for Open Link in New Tab it replaced the page you were on; Chrome, Firefox and Arc have no such item. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#844](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/844), [#843](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/843))
- An AI answer about a long page is checked for web addresses, emails and phone numbers that aren't on the page in a quarter of the time, and a bookmarks file imports in 40% less time. Both built their search patterns again for every word or bookmark; each pattern is now built once.
- Google Docs and Sheets are sharp on a Retina screen on macOS 26.3 and earlier. A page asking how big the window is was told it had no size, and Docs, on a Safari before 26.4, takes the window's width over the page's for the browser's zoom: from nothing it took the smallest zoom it knows, and drew the document at a quarter of the screen's sharpness. A page now reads the window's size and place, as in Safari. Thanks [@2cristo7](https://github.com/2cristo7) for reporting and for the numbers that found it ([#666](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/666), [#827](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/827))
- An extension's worker waits on one round trip fewer when it lists its open pages, reads its permissions, or (in a user-script manager) puts its scripts back as it starts. Each asked two questions one after the other; both now go out at once. Translate Page asks for the page's language while it reads the page's words, not after.
- Spaces brought over from Arc with one of Arc's own icons rather than an emoji keep a matching icon (a bulb, a leaf, a star…) instead of all coming in as a briefcase. Thanks [@introbseso](https://github.com/introbseso) for the report and the table ([#832](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/832))
- Copy Image copies at once: a picture of a few megabytes took up to a minute, since its bytes were read one at a time; a 3 MB picture now takes a fraction of a second
- A swipe back no longer leaves the page shifted to the side, with a blank strip down its left: the page's sides no longer stretch at its own size, only once pinched in, where panning on to an edge needs them. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann), whose [#816](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/816) found it ([#810](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/810), [#815](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/815))
- ⌘T from a page puts the keys in the new tab's address field every time again, not only the first: the field leaving from the last one took them back.
- Extensions work in a peek and in the small window for a link from another app: Stylus styles the page there, and an extension whose script on the page asks it for something gets its answer. They were refused ("Tab not found"), since neither page is in a window's row; they still aren't listed among the window's tabs, and a peek kept as a tab arrives there as a new one. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#818](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/818))
- Closing History, Settings, Downloads, Bookmarks or Passwords takes you back to the tab you were on, as closing their cards did before 1.0.7. It took you to the tab beside it, or to the last tab when ⌘Y brought forward a History tab kept at the end of the row. Other tabs still go to their neighbour unless Settings › Tabs › Closing a tab goes back to the one before is on. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#825](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/825), [#824](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/824))
- Going back to a page that keeps its own scroll position, such as a GitHub issue, comes back where you left it, as in Chrome, instead of at the top. WebKit brings such a page back from its cache at the very top, in Safari too; it now goes back to where it was, unless the page scrolls somewhere itself. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#814](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/814))
- A page you swipe back or forward to comes back where you left it. The glide that follows the fingers lifting reached the page coming back: it was left pulled sideways past its edge, with a blank strip down one side, and scrolled away from where it was being put back. ⌘[ and the Back button were never affected. Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#811](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/811))

### Changed

- Address field suggestions match words anywhere in a page's title or address, and forgive typos like "githb". They show below exact matches, from three letters. Address bar commands also show from part of their name: "down" or "dwn" offers Downloads, while Return still searches. Thanks [@pasteley](https://github.com/pasteley) ([#762](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/762), [#835](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/835))
- Pages in the address field's list and in ⌘K wear their site's icon, as in History and the bookmarks bar: an open tab in place of the grey dot, a page from history or a typed address in place of nothing, a bookmark with its mark moved to the end of the row. Only icons this Mac already keeps, nothing fetched while you type; a site without one wears its letter. Every row's words now start at the same place. ([#822](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/822)) Thanks [@ubeann](https://github.com/ubeann) ([#823](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/823))
- Tab hover feedback is instant, without a fade, in the column, across the top and with Icons only, including pinned tabs and the title/address swap. Other tab animations are unchanged. Asked for by [@evannotfound](https://github.com/evannotfound) ([#803](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/803))

### Added

- Downloads can open somewhere other than a tab: as the side panel's card floating by the downloads button, a quarter of the window's height, growing in from the button and gone with a click elsewhere or Escape; or in the side panel beside the page. The button, ⇧⌘J and History › Downloads… all follow the choice; the button closes the card or the panel it opened, and ⇧⌘J only ever shows it. A tab, as before, unless you choose otherwise in Settings › General › Open Downloads.
- AppleScript can read which space and profile a window is in, by name: `space of front window` and `profile of front window`, so a time tracker or a Raycast action can tell which space the page in front belongs to. Read-only, as the rest is. With a private tab in front, both are missing value, as `current tab` is. Inside `tell application "Search"`, `space` now means the window's space, not a blank, as `tab` does in Safari ([#836](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/836))
- Profiles in one window, as in Arc: with Settings › Spaces & Pins › Profiles in one window on, the space's menu lists every profile's spaces under its name, ⌃1–⌃9 count through them all, and a swipe past a profile's last space goes on to the next profile's. Choosing a space of another profile brings that profile's window into the same place, the same size and on the same screen, and the one you left steps out of sight with its pages still loaded, so coming back is instant. Each profile's pages still never use another's sign-ins. Closing it closes every profile's window in it, and ⇧⌘T brings them back together; quitting and opening Search again brings it back as one window. Off unless turned on. Thanks [@0xbentang](https://github.com/0xbentang) and [@nxcco](https://github.com/nxcco) for asking ([#726](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/726), [#459](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/459))

## 1.0.8 — 2 November 2026

### Added

- Open Link in Split View in a link's right-click menu: the link opens beside the page you are on, the two as a pair, and its page has the keys. A page already in a pair takes the link in place of its other page, which stays open as a tab of its own; a pin keeps its place, and its page goes into the pair, as when a pin is dragged onto a page in Settings › Tabs. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#765](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/765))
- Close Tabs Below in a tab's right-click menu, beside Close Other Tabs (Close Tabs to the Right with tabs across the top). Keeps the clicked tab, the tabs above it and pinned tabs; folded groups below are closed too. Closed as one entry in Recently Closed, so one ⇧⌘T brings them all back, with their groups and Split View pairs. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#710](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/710)), asked for on [#704](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/704)
- With Icons only, the tab you're on shows a close button in place of its icon while the pointer is over it. A click closes that tab; another tab still selects first. In Split View, the cross closes only the focused page. Thanks [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) ([#720](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/720))
- Coding agents can drive Search through `./bench-mcp`, an MCP server over the bench: each session gets a hidden test world of its own, wiped when it closes; pages are read as roles and names, clicks are real ones, typing is checked against the field, and a stale handle or a covered button is an error rather than a guess. Its tool contract follows ArcMod's agent browser tools ([CryogenicPlanet/arc](https://github.com/CryogenicPlanet/arc)). `python3 Tests/bench_mcp.py` checks it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#715](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/715))
- Closing the tab you're on can take you back to the tab you were on before it, as ⌃Tab would, instead of the one beside it. Only a tab still open in the same space and window; the other half of a Split View still comes first, a sleeping tab wakes, and with no tab viewed before it the neighbour is chosen as always. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Closing a tab goes back to the one before. Thanks [@hnnsly](https://github.com/hnnsly) ([#732](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/732))
- File › Save Page As… keeps the page as one web archive, as Safari's Save As does: the page with its pictures and styles in one file, which opens again in Search or Safari, offline too. It sits beside Export as PDF…, with no shortcut of its own unless you give it one in Settings. Thanks [@hnnsly](https://github.com/hnnsly) ([#733](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/733))

### Fixed

- Typing in a new tab carries on with Split View on: after ⌘T from a page, the first letter or two went into the field and every key after them made the Mac's error sound and typed nothing, which looked like the grey ending stopping the typing. The field over the page being left took the keys back as it went, and left them with the window
- With the floating video set to Picture in picture (Settings › General), the button at the top right of the video's little window takes you back to it: Search comes forward on the video's tab and the video goes back into its page. It did nothing when that tab was behind another one or behind another app, which is how it nearly always got there, because macOS puts a video back only into a page that is on screen
- A video filling the screen can be clicked again once paused: clicking it, or its play button, plays it again, as the space bar did. The view macOS lays over a paused video to pick out its text and subject took every click, and is now kept out of the way while the video fills the screen
- A right-click on a picture that is also a link, as on a grid of posts, has the link's items again: Open Link in New Tab, Open Link in Split View, Copy Link. Only a picture with no link gets the picture's own menu; on a link's picture, Copy Image and Download Image are Search's own, as in that menu, rather than WebKit's, which on some sites do nothing.
- ⌘W with tabs picked in the column (⌘- or ⇧-click) closes every picked tab, not only the one you are on, and a right-click on one of them offers Close 3 Tabs (as many as are picked) in place of Close Tab. Each goes as ⌘W takes one: a pin is put down, a tab in a group is put away. They close as one entry in Recently Closed, so one ⇧⌘T brings them all back, Split View pairs with them; with nothing picked, ⌘W closes the tab you are on as before
- With tabs across the top, a Split View pair is as tall as the tabs beside it (it stood 40 points high beside 28), and its close button stays under the pointer: moving onto it no longer took the hover from the half under it, so it no longer faded out or changed which page it closes on the way. Reported on X
- Moving the column to the other side while it is folded away leaves the red, yellow and green buttons in the bar that comes down with the menu bar in full screen. They went missing from it until the column next came out. With several windows open, a window's buttons no longer follow another window's column when the side changes. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#767](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/767))
- A window brought back in full screen at launch, or opened with ⌘N from a window in full screen, has its red, yellow and green buttons at the top of the screen when the menu bar comes down, as any window in full screen does. They showed at the bottom-left corner of the window instead, and sank out of reach as the pointer went to them, until the window left full screen. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#766](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/766))
- History › Clear Browsing Data… brings History out on its clearing controls, from the menu, from ⇧⌘Delete and from a shortcut of your own. It set the controls' state without bringing History out, so asked for with History closed, which is how it is usually asked for, nothing happened at all and giving it another shortcut changed nothing. Asked for with History already open it worked, as it still does, and ⌘Y still opens History on its list. Thanks [@mickaphd](https://github.com/mickaphd) for reporting ([#749](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/749))
- Pressing 1Password's button no longer quits Search on macOS 15. WebKit hands out an extension's errors as stand-ins for real errors, and reading one the way Swift reads errors gave a bad pointer there; Search now asks each one for its domain and code directly
- Claude's side panel opens on the page instead of saying "Claude isn't available on this page" once you are signed in. The panel asks which tab it is in, and Chrome answers "none" there while Search answered with an empty value, which the panel couldn't read. The panel is also listed among the extension's open pages, as in Chrome
- Vimium C's keys work on pages again, its own mappings included. Its worker keeps what it knows of each tab on the connection the page's script opened, and WebKit handed its listener another object for that connection, so every key a page sent it failed there and nothing happened. Each listener of a connection is now handed the one its end was given, as in Chrome. Reported by email
- Picking another site from View › Side Panel with one already up no longer turns the panel white until it is closed and opened again. The new page was made, but the panel kept the one it replaced. The same held for one extension's panel replacing another's
- ChatGPT's button opens its side panel again: since its October update the extension keeps its panel off on every tab and turns it on for the tab whose button you press, and Search, opening the panel itself, found it off and did nothing. As in Chrome, a press over a tab with no panel turned on now goes to the extension
- An update no longer costs every site its service worker when no extension is installed. Search cleared all of them at the first launch after nearly every update, there only for extensions' stale workers (#266), so Google Docs, Gmail, X or YouTube each started again from the network and set up its worker anew, and pages felt slower for a while after the update. With an extension installed they are still cleared, as before
- Closing the last window, with Search still running, lets its pages go, as Safari and Chrome do: its tabs sleep, keeping their place, history and picture, and the Dock icon, ⌘N or a link brings the window back with the same tabs, the one in front woken. Five heavy tabs held about 800 MB after their window closed, now about 70 MB. A tab playing sound, on a call, downloading, with its video out, holding something typed or on a site kept awake stays awake, as with any tab left alone.
- A space can go anywhere among the spaces, the first one included: moved away from the left, it jumped back as soon as the column redrew. The order you choose holds in another window, after a relaunch and with spaces off, where the window still opens on the first space; a new space still goes at the end. Thanks [@Dodothereal](https://github.com/Dodothereal) ([#731](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/731)), reported by [@Qddog23](https://github.com/Qddog23) on [#729](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/729)
- A muted tab comes back muted: reopened with ⇧⌘T or from Recently Closed, from a closed group, or with a window or space brought back at launch. The mute is put on the page before it loads, so it never makes a first sound. Thanks [@Dodothereal](https://github.com/Dodothereal) ([#735](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/735)), asked for by [@mastercoder26](https://github.com/mastercoder26) on [#727](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/727)
- GPTZero can start again: WebKit sent its worker an empty cookie-change notification, and left Chrome's install-reason constants out of content scripts. Both stopped parts of the extension as they began, including its copy tracker, LMS bridge and recorder. Thanks [@mastercoder26](https://github.com/mastercoder26) for reporting ([#724](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/724))
- The address field's page cover fades without scaling, so its edges stay over the page while the field opens and closes. The field keeps its small zoom and the same animation timing, with Split View on or off. Thanks [@mocki-toki](https://github.com/mocki-toki) ([#718](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/718))
- Settings scrolls with the pointer anywhere over its page, the empty margins beside the rows included; it scrolled only over the column of rows. Thanks [@mocki-toki](https://github.com/mocki-toki) ([#719](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/719))
- The page under a peek or under the column out over it no longer reacts to the pointer, the buttons beside a peek included: links there stopped lighting up, menus that open on hover stayed shut and the pointer stayed an arrow over the column and the dimmed page, instead of turning into a hand or a caret for the page underneath. A click that somehow reaches the page under a peek puts the peek away, as a click beside the panel does, and is never the page's (idea 418)
- Bookmarks in the address field: typing matches your bookmarks by name or address, the words in any order, from two letters on, each with a small bookmark mark and before the places in your history, three places in all as before, and the grey ending can finish towards one. Only the window's profile's bookmarks, and quick with thousands of them imported from Firefox or elsewhere (idea 417)
- Links opened behind a page — ⌘-click, the middle button, Open Link in New Tab — go after it in the order you clicked them, as in Safari, Chrome and Arc, whatever the New tab position: with Top they went to the top too, away from their page, where Arc keeps new tabs on top and links under their page; with After current each went straight after the page, so the last one clicked came first, and a link a page's own script opened on a ⌘-click (Gmail's) went to the end of the row. Going to another tab, or the page going somewhere else, starts again right after the page. In a group they stay in it, and a pair in Split View keeps them after both its pages. Reported on X
- Search a site from the address field picks the site you mean: "goo" then Tab is Google, not Google Maps, which "google m" or "maps" names. Google is among the built-in sites; the words name a site whole, or the shortest name they begin, before a longer one; and a site you have learned and go to far more often than the other can win. Reported on X
- The column scrolls back up with more tabs than it holds: a space with many folded groups could come up with its rows running off the bottom and its top out of reach — after a relaunch, on going to that space, or with the column hidden and brought out at its edge — and nothing scrolled it back to the folders. The tab you go to is still scrolled into sight, with roomier rows, Icons only and the column on either side (idea 443). Reported by email
- With pinned rows on and nothing pinned yet, the line over your tabs stays hidden: it comes with the first pin, or a group, which it keeps apart from the tabs Clear would close, in the column and with Icons only (idea 444). Reported by email

### Changed

- Settings says less and sits flatter: no line of explanation under each group's heading, every row of a card at the same level (Darken pages that stay light, Hide the column and The page as a card no longer indented under the row they depend on), and the hairlines between rows run from one edge of the card to the other. Every setting is still there, and still found by its words.
- ⌘W on a tab in a group puts it away rather than closing it, as Arc keeps a folder's tabs: its page is let go, its row stays in the group, and a click opens it again at the page it was on. Its cross shows a minus while the page is open; on a row already put away, the cross or Close Tab closes it for good, and ⇧⌘T brings it back into its group. A page of a Split View pair in a group is put away out of its pair. Close Other Tabs, Close Tabs Below, Clear and Close Group close as before (idea 398). Asked for by email

## 1.0.7 — 25 October 2026

### Added

- Shield blocks YouTube's ads: on youtube.com, YouTube Music and YouTube videos embedded in other sites, the ads YouTube schedules in its player's own data are taken out before the video starts, and the player's ad reports aren't sent. It is part of Settings › Privacy › Block ads and trackers, on with it, and Block on youtube.com turns it off for YouTube alone. An ad that plays all the same goes by muted and fast, and the video's sound and speed come back after it. YouTube changes its pages often, so an ad may get through until the next update
- Tabs as icons only: a narrow column of each tab's icon, pins as they are, the title in a tooltip and nothing that grows or slides out under the pointer. A pair in Split View is one tile with both icons, a tab group a short heading with its first letter and a thin bracket beside its tabs; a playing or muted tab has a small speaker in its corner, one an extension records from a red dot, and the address the tab you're on would say is in its tooltip. Dragging, closing with the middle button or the menu, the new tab button and the space's button work as in the column; a click on the tab you're on opens the address field over the page. The window's buttons and back, forward and reload stand one above the other at the top. Off unless you pick Icons only in Settings › Appearance › Tabs
- Apple Passwords can be offered under a sign-in box: on a site where Search keeps no account, the first time you're in its sign-in box, one quiet row at the bottom says "Use your Apple passwords?". A click turns Apple Passwords on and asks for your Mac's code right there; its ✕ means don't offer again. At most once per site, and never again once it has been passed by twice; never in a private tab, on plain http or in a frame from another site, and only on a Mac that has Apple's helper. Apple Passwords itself stays off until you say so
- Fold All Groups and Unfold All Groups, as in Arc: every tab group of the space at once, from the space's menu or View, or ⌥⌘[ and ⌥⌘] (beside ⌘[ and ⌘], Back and Forward). Each group keeps its tabs and its place, and a folded one still shows the tab you're on if it is one of its own.
- Press Escape twice to leave full screen, as in Arc, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing › Video › Press Esc twice to leave full screen. In a video's full screen or the window's own (the green button, ⌃⌘F), the first press shows "Press Esc again to exit full screen" and a second within two seconds leaves; the find bar, the address field, a peek or a panel still take a single Escape first. Holding Escape down counts as one press. Off, one Escape leaves, as before. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#678](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/678))
- Reading mode can follow light and dark: in a dark window the article is light on dark, and a small switch in its corner, faint until the pointer reaches it, makes an article light, dark or as the window is, kept for the next one. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing › Reading mode. Thanks [@dima237](https://github.com/dima237) ([#566](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/566))
- Reading mode can read a site's own Markdown when it publishes one, instead of working the article out from the page: the page's Markdown twin, named in its head or at its own address with .md on the end, as Anthropic's, Mintlify's, Vercel's, Cloudflare's and Bun's docs have. It's drawn in the same reading page, marked "· markdown" beside the site's name, and a Markdown file opened in a tab reads the same way. Only the page's own site is asked, only an answer that says it is Markdown counts, and nothing in it can run: its text is escaped and only plain tags and web links are kept. A page without one is read as before, a moment later. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Browsing › Reading mode › Read the site's own Markdown. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#687](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/687))
- Sized website pop-ups can open in small windows of their own instead of tabs: a call, a sign-in, a chat asking for a window its size gets one, as in Safari. They keep their opener, sign-ins and space, close when their page does, and never come back with the session. The line over the page names the site that is on screen, never an address still on its way. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › New tabs › Open sized pop-ups in small windows; pop-up blocking still applies. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#660](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/660), [#552](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/552))
- A site asks before it shares your screen, as in Safari: a card names the site and offers Share a Window, Share a Screen or Don't Allow, and says so when the page also wants your Mac's sound. Only then does macOS's own picker open, so nothing is shared until you pick; nothing is remembered, and a tab behind waits until you're on it. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) and [@sxivansx](https://github.com/sxivansx) ([#651](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/651), [#530](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/530), [#528](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/528))
- Hold Back or Forward, or right-click either, for every page that way, as in Safari and Chrome: the nearest at the top, each with its site's icon. Pick one and you go straight there, however many steps away; a click still goes one step. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#567](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/567))
- Animations can go easy on a Mac that's saving power: in Low Power Mode, or while the Mac is running hot, the sidebar, tabs and panels move in a short 0.16 s ease-out instead of a spring that takes about half a second to settle. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Motion. Pages are left as they are: WebKit already draws them at 30 frames a second in Low Power Mode, as in Safari. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#688](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/688))
- Tabs can give a little under the pointer while you press them, in the column and across the top, and spring back when you let go, as Arc's do. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Tabs › Tabs give when pressed; nothing moves with Reduce Motion on. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#686](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/686))
- Profiles, as in Chrome and Arc: separate sets of spaces, each with its own sign-ins and site data, its own history and its own bookmarks, in windows of their own. A window of one profile shows nothing of another's: not its spaces, not its tabs in ⌃Tab, not its history in the address field. Make one in Settings › Spaces & Pins › Profiles; once there are two, a letter beside the space's icon says which profile the window is in and switches to the other one's window, as does File › Profile. Everything you have now is the profile Personal, where it was, and a space with sign-ins of its own keeps them. Importing from Arc brings each Arc profile in as a profile of its own, with its spaces. Extensions, passwords, downloads and settings stay shared by every profile. Thanks [@0xbentang](https://github.com/0xbentang) for asking and [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) for offering to build it ([#459](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/459))
- Translate Page, on this Mac: right-click a page › Translate Page to your Mac's language, done by macOS's own translation on this Mac, so nothing is sent anywhere (each language is downloaded once, the first time, in Apple's own sheet). What's on screen is translated first, then the rest, then whatever the page adds; links, bold and code stay where they were in the sentence, frames are translated too, and a quote in another language is translated from its own language or left alone if it's already in yours. Code, fields, editable text and what a page marks as not to translate stay as they are. A small sign beside the tab's title says the page is translated; a click on it offers Show Original, which puts back exactly what was there, and Always Translate <language>. Settings › Browsing › Pages lists the languages always translated. Needs macOS 15 or later. A private tab can translate, and remembers nothing
- Passwords from Apple's Passwords app under the sign-in box, as in Safari, without the iCloud Passwords extension. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Passwords › Passwords from Apple's Passwords app, and the first time you want one of its accounts, Connect Apple Passwords… under the sign-in box asks for the six-digit code your Mac shows, right there, once per launch of Search (Apple's helper keeps no pairing between launches; nothing is asked at launch). Only the names are asked for while you are in a sign-in box; a password is asked for when you pick its account, macOS may ask for Touch ID, and it goes into that page alone. Only accounts for the site you are on, only on https pages, never in a private tab or a frame from another site, and nothing is kept by Search. Unofficial: Search talks to the helper Apple made for its Chrome extension, as that extension, so a macOS update could stop it working; the extension stays the way in then. Asked for on [#425](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/425)
- The tab you're on can show where its page is, without a click: its site in place of its title, the subdomain dim before it, the title back under the pointer. Look-alike names in other alphabets show as the punycode they are (xn--80ak6aa92e.com, not a Cyrillic "apple.com"), plain http says Not secure, and a file on this Mac is named by its file name. In the column, across the top and on the half you're on in Split View. Every page says it, unless you turn off Settings › Tabs › Show the address on the tab you're on; with it off, only a page over plain http and a look-alike name are said, as warnings
- Two tabs picked in the column (⌘- or ⇧-click) go side by side with Open 2 Tabs in Split View in either one's menu, or ⌥⌘N: the first in the column on the left, each keeping its page as it is, with nothing loaded again. A tab in another pair leaves it first, as when it is dragged; a pin stays a pin and its page goes in, as with one tab. With more than two picked the item is there but off, and ⌥⌘N splits the page in front as it does without a selection. The selection is cleared after
- ⌃Tab's recent-tabs switcher can be on a key of your own, as in Arc: Settings › Keyboard & Mouse › Shortcuts › Tabs › Switch to Recent Tab. Hold the key's ⌃, ⌥ or ⌘ to keep the switcher up and let go to take the tab; with ⇧ it goes back. ⌃Tab can then be given to another command, such as Next Tab, or left to the page.
- Move Group to Space, in a tab group's menu: every tab of the group goes to the other space, in its order, and is the same group there, folded or open as it was. A space with its own sign-ins asks first only when a page in the group has something typed and not sent, once for the group.
- Websites in the side panel: Claude, ChatGPT, Gemini, Perplexity or the page you're on, beside the page without an extension, from View › Side Panel or a tab's Open in Side Panel. It's a page of the space you're in, signed in where that space's tabs are and never a private tab's; its address is on the panel's own line, a link it opens in a new window goes to a tab, and Open in a Tab takes it into one. Always there, with no switch
- The floating video can be macOS's own picture in picture instead of Search's window: Settings › Browsing › Video › The floating video. ⇧⌘P, and leaving the tab or the app when those float a video, then hand it to the system's window, the one Safari uses, and coming back to the tab brings it home. Search's window stays the default; it floats a video without a click and keeps its page alive in it
- A pin's ⌘ number pressed again while you're on that pin takes you back to the tab you were on before it, and once more back to the pin. Always there, with no switch
- Copy Clean Link copies an address without the parameters that only track where a click came from (utm_ tags, fbclid, gclid, msclkid, mc_eid, igshid and a few more, and a YouTube or Spotify share's si). Everything else stays as it was, in its order, and an address with a signature is copied unchanged. It's under Copy Link when you right-click a link, on the page's own menu when its address has any, in a tab's menu and in the Edit menu, ⌥⇧⌘C. A menu item, so there is no switch. Asked for on [#594](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/594)
- A right-click on the bookmarks bar opens a menu, as in Safari and Chrome. On a bookmark: open it in a new tab, a new window, Split View or a private tab, rename it, change its address, copy its link, move it into a folder or remove it. On a folder: open the bookmarks in it in new tabs, in a new window or in a tab group named after the folder, rename it, make a folder inside it, move it or remove it. On the bar's empty space: New Folder…, Show Bookmarks… and Hide Bookmarks Bar. Open in Split View and Open All in Tab Group are there when Split View and tab groups are on. Thanks [@bacecek](https://github.com/bacecek) ([#438](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/438), [#439](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/439))
- Settings › Tabs › Show tab numbers while ⌘ is held: while ⌘ is held, each tab ⌘1–9 picks shows its number, in the column and across the top; a folded group counts once. Off unless turned on. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#654](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/654), [#658](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/658))
- Settings › General › Confirm before quitting asks before ⌘Q or Quit Search closes the browser. Cancel keeps the windows and tabs open. Off unless turned on. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#659](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/659))
- Pins and tabs dragged across, as in Arc: a tab carried onto the pins becomes one where you let it go, the squares making room for it on the way, and a pin carried down among the tabs becomes a tab there, a line opening where it will land. Either keeps its page as it is, with nothing loaded again. With pinned rows on, a tab can be let go among them, and a square or a row taken to the other kind. A pin let go over a group's name goes into the group. With no pins yet, carry a tab up beside the window's buttons to make the first. A pair in Split View isn't pinned by a drag (Pin Tab still pins the half you ask for). A pin dropped in makes a pin of this space, after the ones every space shows; one of those dragged out leaves every space, as Unpin does
- Two more switches in Settings › Privacy, both on unless you turn them off. Remove click trackers from links takes the IDs an ad network adds to a link so the site can tell whose click it was (gclid, fbclid and the like) off the address before the page loads, as Safari does; campaign tags (utm_) stay. Tell sites not to sell or share your data sends Global Privacy Control with every request, which some laws make a site honour. Both need macOS 27, and their switches aren't shown before. Private tabs always do both, as in Safari's Private Browsing. A switch you move reaches open tabs from their next page. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#701](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/701))

### Changed

- The edges you pull to resize — the column's, the side panel's and the one between two pages in Split View — no longer draw a line the window's whole height. A small rounded handle sits in the middle of the edge instead: hidden until the pointer is on the column's or the panel's edge, faint between two pages, a little longer and darker under the pointer and while held. The whole edge still takes the pointer, and a double-click still puts it back. A Split View pair in the column now reads as one thing, with a faint ground of its own and a fine line between its two tabs, and the pointer on one tab of the pair while the other is open no longer leaves a notch between the two greys
- Settings, reorganised so you can see what you turn on: ten short sections (General, Appearance, Tabs, Browsing, Spaces & Pins, Passwords, Privacy, Extensions, Keyboard & Mouse, About) instead of two pages of 24 and 28 identical lines. Each section is in titled groups with an icon and a line saying what the group is for; each row has its own small icon, a title in plain words and one short line, with the rest behind its (i). A row that only counts while another is on sits indented under it and is only there while it counts (the column's options with the tabs down a side, Back and forward on the left with the tabs across the top). Tabs Top / Left / Right, Light / Dark / Auto and the page as a card are small pictures to pick from, as in macOS's own Appearance pane, and Sleep tabs says under its choice what happens. Find a setting, at the top of the sections (⌘F on the Settings tab, Escape empties it), finds any setting by its words or by others you might type ("vertical tabs", "pip", "autoplay", "dark"), shows each with its section and group and its real control, and goes to it with a click. search://settings/shortcuts and /downloads still open, at Keyboard & Mouse and General
- Set Up Like Arc, in Settings › Spaces & Pins: tabs on the left, Spaces, pinned rows, pins that keep their site and the floating new tab in one click, with Undo, beside Bring Arc's spaces and pins over
- Fewer switches: Split View, tab groups, websites in the side panel, Translate Page, Keep selected sites awake, searching a site from the address field (red, Tab), pins in every space, a pin's number pressed again going back, and the ⌃Tab switcher's keys are always there, since each only adds a command that does nothing until used; an old "off" no longer hides them. Show where links go and flicking the floating video are always on too, but an explicit off made before stays off. Correct spelling as you type moved to Edit › Spelling and Grammar, as in Safari
- Two switches become one choice, reading and writing the same settings as before: Tabs Top / Left / Right, Sleep tabs Never / Later / Normal / Sooner, Float the video on its own Never / Switching tabs / Tabs or apps, Pins keep their site (Pins go back home with Pins stay on their site), and Peek at a link Off / Shift-click / Option-Shift-click / Option-Command-click in Keyboard & Mouse
- On for everyone who never changed them, a choice made before standing: Pins keep their site, Pages at 120 Hz On power (an old 120 Hz off stays Never), Hold a swipe to pick from history, and Show the address on the tab you're on
- About no longer repeats a dozen shortcuts (Keyboard & Mouse lists them all, as you set them), Passwords no longer repeats Bring Things Over, and Let a script drive Search is under About › Developer
- Settings, History, Downloads, Bookmarks, Passwords, Bring Things Over and What's New open as pages in a tab of their own, as in Chrome, instead of a card over the page. ⌘, ⌘Y ⇧⌘J and the menus bring the open one forward rather than opening a second; an empty tab in front becomes the page. Each has its own address (search://settings, search://history…), goes with ⌃Tab, sits beside a page in Split View, closes with ⌘W and comes back after a relaunch. Back and Forward go between them, and from a page you opened out of History or Bookmarks back to the list. From a private tab they open beside it, as ordinary tabs
- With the tabs in the sidebar, back, forward and reload sit a little further from the window's red, yellow and green buttons, as a toolbar's first button does in Safari; a narrow column keeps them where they were

### Fixed

- Saved accounts are offered only in real sign-in fields, as in Safari and Chrome: a password box, a name box beside one, or the name step of a sign-in that asks for it first (Google, Microsoft, Okta, impots.gouv.fr) when the field says it takes a name, email or phone and the page looks like a sign-in. A project's name in the Google Cloud console, a repository's name, a message box, an email's To field, a search box, a newsletter or a checkout's email no longer show the list, and accounts from the site's other addresses (accounts.google.com on console.cloud.google.com) come only in a real sign-in field ([#631](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/631))
- Closing the last tab that isn't in a group or pinned leaves an empty tab, as Arc does, instead of taking you to a page out of one of your tab groups and opening that group. ⌘T then gave the floating field over that page rather than a new tab. An empty tab closed there stays, and so does the window
- Escape in a new tab clears what you typed first, the tab staying, and a second Escape closes the tab you didn't use, as in Arc. With a word typed, Escape did nothing
- With Split View turned on, the saved accounts under a sign-in box hang right under it again, and a click on one fills the form. The list was drawn down and to the right of the box, often in the window's bottom-right corner, where a click on it did nothing
- Search gives memory back once an ad blocker's rules are built. WebKit compiles an extension's blocking rules inside the app, all of them each time any change: AdBlock's at every launch and every filter update, a gigabyte for a minute. What it freed stayed counted against Search, 1.1 GB around 44 MB actually in use; now it goes back to the Mac when the rules are done and when Search goes to the background. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#604](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/604))
- One Escape leaves a video's full screen, as in Safari, instead of two. A page filling the screen is in WebKit's own window, whose keys went to the browser behind it first, and Escape there put away whatever was left up behind the video, the find bar, the address field or a peek, before the page had it. Escape is now the page's while it fills the screen, in a Split View pair too. A tab drag whose end never came no longer keeps the next Escape for itself either. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#678](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/678))
- Reading mode shows an article's headline once when the article repeats the title, keeps a drawing in the text at the size the page drew it rather than the width of the column, and a link followed out of an article is read with ⇧⌘R rather than reloaded. Thanks [@dima237](https://github.com/dima237) ([#566](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/566))
- Reading mode keeps Bloomberg's photos, byline, takeaways and opening video. The photos sit in click-to-enlarge buttons, which went as clutter with what they held, and the rest sits between the headline and the text. A video its player feeds from the page now plays from the plain stream it names, fetching nothing until you press play, or shows its poster; newsletter boxes and a video player's controls stay out. Thanks [@dima237](https://github.com/dima237) ([#565](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/565))
- Reading mode keeps an article's charts and its opening photo. On the FT each chart was an empty band, as every frame but a video's was taken out; frames from the chart makers (Flourish, Datawrapper, Infogram, Tableau, the FT's own, Google Maps) now stay at the height the page gave them, the photo the FT keeps just above its article comes too, and a box left empty goes. Thanks [@dima237](https://github.com/dima237) ([#564](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/564))
- Reading mode leaves the page's own styles behind: its stylesheets, its fonts and the styles written on the article's elements. They reached the reading page, so a site's reset could take the numbers off its lists, or its font stand in for the reader's. What the site's styles hid stays hidden: a label for screen readers or a phone-only copy of a table doesn't appear. Search's own styles for the page stay, Dusk and Find on Page among them. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#689](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/689))
- Extensions with overlapping site permissions use less memory: a wildcard grant covers the hosts it names without a grant of their own, and WebKit keeps a copy of an extension's content scripts for every grant. The sites an extension can reach are the same. This lessens, without ending, the memory Lexirise and the like take. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#669](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/669), [#663](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/663))
- The Figma extension's "Allow additional permissions" page works: Next asks, as in Chrome, to read and change every website, and once you agree Figma goes on. A permission its manifest names but WebKit doesn't have (pageCapture) refused the whole request without a question; a click in the extension's own page wasn't counted as the click a request needs; and access to every website was taken back the moment it was given. It is now kept as every website, still never other extensions' pages or files on this Mac, and an extension that gives it back gives all of it back. Thanks [@TanguyCaruel](https://github.com/TanguyCaruel) ([#675](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/675))
- Apps on the same host each wear their own icon. A home server with Sonarr at host/sonarr and Radarr at host/radarr showed whichever icon was fetched first on every tab, pin, bookmark and history line for that host, for a week: icons were kept by host. They are now kept page by page, by the page's path alone, as Safari, Firefox and Chrome keep them, and go with the page when it goes from History. A page that declares no icon wears one from its own app, or its letter rather than another app's. Private tabs' icons stay apart and go with the last private tab. An icon that couldn't be reached is asked for again after a minute, where before it wasn't until Search was quit. Icons kept before carry over. Thanks [@AllDaGearNoIdea](https://github.com/AllDaGearNoIdea) ([#641](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/641))
- An extension's side panel waits while a private tab is in front, unless you have allowed that extension private tabs in Settings › Extensions, and the page takes the room it had meanwhile. An extension not allowed is given nothing of a private tab, so its panel beside one was a panel about a page it could not see. It is held back, not closed: it comes back as you left it on a tab the extension may see. A panel asked for by an extension over a private tab it may not see opens nothing at all. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#677](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/677), [#662](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/662))
- An extension's scripts keep their Unicode when it puts them into a page written in an older encoding. Backpack's wallet could fail with an invalid regular expression on windows-1252 pages, its UTF-8 script read in the page's encoding. Installed extensions are prepared again once, at the first launch. Thanks [@K-NRS](https://github.com/K-NRS) ([#521](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/521))
- The ⌃Tab switcher's cards and thumbnails have the same smooth corners as the rest of Search, instead of plain round ones. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#686](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/686))
- With the tabs down the side and fewer of them than the column holds, the pointer crossing a row no longer builds every row again, twice over: about 40 rows a crossing became 2, and the main thread's time for one went from about 26 ms to 4. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#694](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/694))
- A tab dragged onto a tab group goes in where you let it go, among the group's tabs, in the column and across the top, and a tab dragged within its group moves to its new place. It could miss the group, and when it went in it always went to the end. Let go on the group's name, it goes at the end and the group opens. Thanks [@mvanhorn](https://github.com/mvanhorn) for the fix and [@dalxds](https://github.com/dalxds) for reporting ([#693](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/693), [#673](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/673))
- New small windows open a step down and to the right of the newest one on screen instead of exactly on top of it. Five links from Mail were five windows that looked like one, the others hidden under it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#493](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/493))
- A video call goes on when you go to another tab. On a Google Meet call, the others saw your camera frozen until you came back to its tab, since a tab behind was taken out of the window and its page slowed as one nobody can see; a tab using the camera, the microphone or the screen now stays in the window, unseen, for as long as the call lasts, as Safari and Chrome keep it going. Any other tab behind is slowed and put to sleep as before
- An extension that sends a site to a page of its own, as Norma does with its block page, shows that page again. WebKit carried out such a rule only for extensions that ask for more than Chrome requires, so the site loaded instead, or, with Norma's Mac app blocking it, "No connection." came up; and the page then needed a view of its own and its `#` back. Its rules go no further than the sites it was given, and a page it doesn't open to websites is still never shown
- With uBlock Origin Lite installed, pages no longer go blank about half a minute after launch, a slip in the change above that never reached a release. Some of its rules name a pattern beside a list of sites, and WebKit drops the list and takes the rule for every site, so every page was sent to uBlock's warning page. A rule that redirects or changes headers in that shape is now left out, for any extension, since it would act far beyond the sites it names; its strict blocking then acts only through its rules that name a site themselves. When one of those sends a site to its warning page, the page shows, as in Chrome, where the tab stayed blank. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) for finding and tracing it ([#709](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/709))
- Dragging a tab or a pin in the column moves it under the hand again. In 1.0.6, since a tab opens as the button goes down ([#625](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/625)), it only jumped into place on letting go, and a pin held still a moment before letting go wasn't moved at all
- One letter in the address field finishes the site you go to most that starts with it, as in Safari and Chrome: "x" and Return go to x.com. Only the top row, and only a site you have been to; a page inside a site, or a letter inside a name, still waits for a second letter. Thanks [@murahovsky](https://github.com/murahovsky) for reporting ([#696](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/696))
- A peek opens at your default page zoom from Settings › General, or at the zoom you set for that site, as a tab does; it always opened at 100%. So does the small window a link from another app opens in. Thanks [@ralvs](https://github.com/ralvs) for reporting ([#691](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/691))
- A site whose only icon is an SVG wears it instead of its letter, and so does one whose icon is written into the page itself (an emoji, often). The SVG is drawn by WebKit in a sandboxed process of its own, the way a page shows one, and only the small picture it makes comes back: no new image decoder in Search. Thanks [@maxvoltar](https://github.com/maxvoltar) for reporting ([#680](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/680))
- 1Password's button in a field, while 1Password is locked, opens its popup to unlock again. 1Password asks for its popup only from a browser that says it is Chrome 149 or later, and Search told extensions it was Chrome 140; it now says 156, Chrome's version today, to extensions and to the Chrome Web Store. Thanks [@dalxds](https://github.com/dalxds) for reporting ([#685](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/685))
- Signing in with your Apple Account by passkey, on Apple's own pages and with Sign in with Apple, gets the Mac's passkey sheet, an iPhone's QR code included. Apple's sign-in is a frame from another of Apple's addresses in the page, and Search refused a passkey to any frame not on the page's exact address, so Apple said the browser needed updating. A frame of the page's own site may now ask, as in Safari, and the site is told it came from a frame; a frame from another site still can't. Thanks [@tecandrew](https://github.com/tecandrew) for reporting ([#679](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/679))
- An extension that moves a tab into a window of its own, with `chrome.windows.create({tabId})`, gets the tab there, page and all, instead of an empty new window and the tab left where it was. A popup-type window asked to take one of your tabs opens as a browser window, since a popup is never saved and goes when its extension is turned off, and your tab would go with it. A pin, already in every window, isn't moved. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#702](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/702))
- The Passwords page lists your passwords when opened from Settings › Passwords › Open…, or from its address typed on a tab. Reached that way it was never read and said "Nothing kept yet", while the sign-in box on a page offered the same accounts. It is now read each time it is shown.
- A pinned tab going between an extension's page and a website stays the same pin. It became a new one, so every other window closed its tab for the pin and made a sleeping one, losing the page it was on there; the page it was pinned at, where a double-click on it goes back to, became wherever it had just gone; and a pin every space shows was kept as this space's own. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#707](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/707))
- Local file tabs come back after quitting Search, in their tab groups too. They were left out of the saved session; private tabs are still never saved. Thanks [@mocki-toki](https://github.com/mocki-toki) for the fix and [@ralvs](https://github.com/ralvs) for the report ([#713](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/713), [#699](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/699))
- With Settings › Spaces & Pins › Opening and starting › New tab in a floating field on, ⌘T opens a tab, and Search asks there, when an extension you haven't answered has asked to replace the new tab page. The field came up over the page instead, so the extension was never asked and its page never appeared. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#714](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/714))

## 1.0.6 — 24 October 2026

### Added

- Suggestions from your search engine as you type, under the address field's own rows, each with a magnifying glass; choosing one searches it. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Suggestions from your search engine, since what you type is then sent to your engine as you type it. Google, DuckDuckGo, Bing, Ecosia, Startpage, Kagi, Brave Search and Qwant; a custom engine shows none. Never from a private tab, and never for an address, a file path, an email, something long or something that looks like a password or key. No cookies are sent with them
- A pop-up a page opens without a click just before is still refused, and now says so, as in Safari: a small sign beside the tab's title. A click on it offers the page the pop-up would have shown, to open in a tab of its own, and Always Allow Pop-ups from the site. An allowed site's own pages then open their windows without the click; a frame from another site on them still needs one. Settings › Privacy lists the sites you allowed, each with Remove. In a private tab, Allow lasts only as long as the tab, and nothing is kept
- An extension's side panel, as in Chrome: ChatGPT's, Claude's, Sider's or Merlin's panel opens beside the page instead of in a tab, on the side away from the tabs, as wide as you leave it (drag its edge; a double-click puts it back). With the page as a card it is a card of its own; edge to edge or with the tabs across the top, it meets the page at a fine line. In Split View the two pages share the rest. Opened for one tab, it comes and goes with that tab. A panel that can't open opens as a tab, as before
- The page as a card can be turned off, in Settings › Tabs › The page as a card, beside the sidebar's settings. Off, the page reaches the window's edges and the column is white with its fine line, as before 1.0.5. It stays on unless you turn it off. Asked for on [#607](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/607)
- A small window for a link from another app closes with a swipe back from the page it opened on, since back from there is back to before it opened. The disc shows a cross instead of an arrow when letting go will close it. Pages you clicked through inside it go back as usual first
- Settings › General › Home page, while an extension offers to replace the new tab page: a menu of Search's own page and each extension that asked, and the way back after one took over. One extension has new tabs at a time, and the newest one you said yes to wins; before, saying no to a newer extension also took new tabs away from an older one you'd said yes to. An extension added while another has new tabs is asked too, naming the one it would replace. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#472](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/472))
- How soon tabs sleep is a choice: Settings › Tabs › How soon tabs sleep puts a tab you aren't using to sleep after two hours (Later), half an hour (Normal, as before) or ten minutes (Sooner). Memory running short still puts them to sleep sooner, whichever you chose.
- A page can be exported as a PDF from the File menu, at its current full length and in the layout on screen rather than broken into sheets for printing. The export stays with the tab it was opened for, even if another becomes active before saving. Thanks [@Qddog23](https://github.com/Qddog23) for asking ([#446](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/446)), and [@Rahuletto](https://github.com/Rahuletto) for the implementation.
- Closing the last tab that isn't a pin can leave a new tab, as closing the only tab does, instead of taking you into a pin and waking it if it was asleep. The pins stay where they are, untouched, and ⇧⌘T brings the page back; closed behind a pin, it leaves the pin on screen. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Leave a new tab when the last tab closes. Thanks [@kingsleydon](https://github.com/kingsleydon) for reporting and [@Adithya-Sakaray](https://github.com/Adithya-Sakaray) for the fix ([#537](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/537), [#538](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/538))
- Import extension-only browser profiles and ego lite; ego lite passwords are shown as unsupported. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#507](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/507))
- Select several tabs with ⌘-click or ⇧-click, including Split View pairs, and copy their addresses from the tab menu. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#454](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/454))
- Remove All… in the passwords panel takes every password Search keeps out of the keychain at once, after asking and after Touch ID or your password, as showing one does, for when you move them to a password manager of your own. With the search box narrowing the list it reads Remove 12… and removes only what the list shows. Return is Cancel there. Passwords in Bitwarden, other apps and other browsers are not touched. Thanks [@Tekkiech](https://github.com/Tekkiech) for asking and [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) for building it ([#471](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/471), [#469](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/469))
- Search is offered under the Finder's Open With for PDFs, pictures, text files and JSON, as it was for web pages. It never becomes what opens them on a double-click. Thanks [@arvakme](https://github.com/arvakme) for asking ([#524](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/524))
- Pins go back home, off unless you turn it on in Settings: as in Arc, a pinned tab closed with ⌘W, or open when Search quits, comes back at the page it was pinned at instead of wherever you went from there. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#614](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/614))
- New tabs can open at the end of the list instead of just after the tab you are on: Settings › Tabs › New tab position › Bottom (Rightmost across the top). After current unless you change it. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#613](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/613))
- Roomier tabs, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs: taller rows and larger titles in the sidebar, for anyone who finds the column hard to read. Thanks [@CallMeAl3x](https://github.com/CallMeAl3x) ([#609](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/609), [#570](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/570))
- ⌘T can raise the address field over the page you are on, as in Arc, off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › New tab in a floating field. Return opens what you typed in a new tab; Escape, ⌘W or a click outside puts the field away and leaves the page as it was. On a blank tab, or with an extension's new tab page, ⌘T opens a tab as before. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#601](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/601))
- Pins in every space: a pin can be kept in every space, shown above each space's own pins, as Arc's favourites sit above every space. Right-click a pin and choose Keep in Every Space, or Only in This Space to take it back; unpinning it takes it out of every space. A space with its own sign-ins signs in to it its own way. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Pins in every space, which shows while Spaces is on. Thanks [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) ([#463](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/463))
- Right-click Reload for Reload Page From Origin, beside Reload Page, so ⌥⌘R isn't the only way to it. No menu while the page is still loading, when the button is Stop. Thanks [@theosementa](https://github.com/theosementa) ([#571](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/571))
