- Pages at 120 Hz can now be limited to when the Mac is on its power adapter: Settings › General › Pages at 120 Hz is Never, On power or Always, and On power switches by itself as the adapter is plugged in or pulled. An earlier "on" becomes Always. Thanks [@theosementa](https://github.com/theosementa) ([#572](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/572))
- Tabs can close themselves, as in Arc: a tab you haven't looked at for 12 hours, a day, a week or a month closes, unless you pinned it, named it or put it in a group. The time counts from when you last left the tab, while Search is quit too, so yesterday's tabs are gone when it opens. The page on screen stays, and so does a private tab, and one playing sound, on a call, downloading, asking something or holding something typed; a Split View pair closes whole or not at all. ⇧⌘T and History bring one back. Never unless you choose a time in Settings › Tabs › Close tabs you leave alone. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#429](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/429), [#445](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/445))
- Light pages darkened while Search is dark, as Dark Reader or Chrome's force-dark flag do, for the sites with no dark look of their own. A site that already has one is left as it made it: Search looks at the page's own colours just before it is drawn, and again when it switches its theme, rather than trusting what the site says about itself. The page keeps its own design in dark colours: every colour the site sets is taken to a dark one of the same hue, so its brand colours stay recognisable, and pictures and videos aren't touched at all. Dark logos and icons drawn on nothing are turned light, and writing laid over a photo keeps its colour. A frame in the page, a comment box or a widget, is darkened with it when it's light, and a video player isn't; so are the parts of a page built as web components. On macOS 14, pages are darkened by turning them over instead, and the brightest colours in pictures come out a little duller. ⇧⌘D, or the switch under the site's address, turns it off or on for the site you're on, and is kept; ⌥⇧⌘D pauses it everywhere, for showing someone a site as they see it, until you press it again or quit. A private tab darkens but keeps nothing. Nothing goes into a page Search leaves alone: whether a page is light is seen from a picture of it, from outside. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Appearance › Darken pages that stay light, which shows under Appearance whenever it is Dark or System. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#450](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/450))
- Small windows in the ⌃Tab switcher. The small windows for links from other apps sit beside your tabs like moons, in a panel of their own, up to nine, each named by its page's title so two threads on one site can be told apart, and the longer its page, the bigger the moon. In the browser window, ⌃Tab walks on into them after the tabs; in a small window, it starts on that window's moon, a quick press goes back to the small window you were in before, and walking on reaches your tabs. Picking a small window brings it forward without moving it into your tabs, and Escape puts the switcher away and leaves the window open. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#480](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/480))
- Keys in the ⌃Tab switcher: with ⌃ still held, a letter acts on the card picked, as its ⌘ key would. ⌃W closes it (a pinned tab is put down), ⌃R reloads it, ⌃M mutes it, and ⌃O takes a small window into your tabs; the switcher stays up after the first three, and the keys are listed under the tabs. They work with any keyboard layout on, Russian or Greek included. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Keys in the tab switcher. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#495](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/495))
- With the tabs across the top, a line stands between the pins and the tabs that come and go, as the column has had since 1.0.5. It shows only with something on both sides of it, and the run counts its width, so the tabs keep the room they had. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#630](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/630), [#449](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/449))
- Sites you choose stay awake, off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Keep selected sites awake: the site card then has Keep This Site Awake, and a site kept awake neither sleeps nor closes by itself, even when memory runs short, at the cost of the memory it keeps. In a private tab the choice lasts only as long as the tab. Closing it by hand works as always. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#627](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/627), [#623](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/623))
- New tabs can open at the top: Settings › Tabs › New tab position is After current (as before), Top or Bottom, read Leftmost and Rightmost with the tabs across the top. With Top, ⌘T's tab, a private one, a link and a page that opens in front all go first after the pins; a link from a group stays in its group. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) for building it and [@zhuxining](https://github.com/zhuxining) for asking ([#621](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/621), [#606](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/606))
- The keys for Spaces and the click that peeks at a link can be changed in Settings › Shortcuts, each shown while its feature is on. ⌃1–⌃9 can be moved, cleared or reset like any command; a peek can be a Shift-click (as before), an Option-Shift-click or an Option-Command-click. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) for building it and [@AtikshB](https://github.com/AtikshB) for asking ([#620](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/620), [#498](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/498))
- Pins stay on their site, as in Arc: a link from a pinned tab to another site opens in a peek over it, and the pin stays on its page, including when the site sends the link out by way of itself, as Google's results do. Links the page opens in a new window on a click, from `target=_blank` or `window.open`, go to the peek too; a window it opens by itself never does. Links within the site go as usual, and ⌘W takes the pin back to the page it was pinned at. While the peek is up, the pin wears its icon: on the corner of a square, at the end of a row. The peek is solid from the start, in the window's colours, with the shape of a page shimmering in it until its page draws. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Pins stay on their site. Thanks [@bent2685](https://github.com/bent2685) ([#577](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/577), [#581](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/581))
- File › Close Window, ⇧⌘W, closes the whole window with its tabs, as in Arc, Chrome and Safari; ⌘W still closes the tab. It can be changed in Settings › Shortcuts. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#640](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/640))
- With spaces on, the mouse's back and forward buttons over the tabs go to the space before or after, as in Arc. Over the page they still go back and forward in the page. Thanks [@Tom-Boscher](https://github.com/Tom-Boscher) ([#639](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/639))

### Changed

- A bookmark opens in a tab of its own, from the bookmarks bar, the Bookmarks menu or the list: opened from a pinned tab it used to take the pin's place. An empty new tab is used as it is, and ⌘ opens the bookmark behind the tab you are on.
- Pins in a space you aren't in go to sleep after the usual wait, as the tabs beside them do, and give their memory back: five spaces of five pins used to be twenty-five pages that never slept. Pins in the space on screen stay awake as before, and so does a pin whose site may send you notifications. Thanks [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) for measuring where the memory went, and [@Browny01](https://github.com/Browny01) and [@Quan018](https://github.com/Quan018) for asking ([#569](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/569))
- A tab in the column opens as the button goes down, not when it comes back up. Dragging a tab or a pin, right-clicking one, ⌘- and ⇧-click, the cross, renaming and a click on the tab you are on to edit its address work as before. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) for building it and [@wouter-deen](https://github.com/wouter-deen) for asking ([#625](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/625), [#598](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/598))

### Fixed

- A site you allow to know where you are now gets its position. Search answered the site but never asked the Mac, so pages waited out their timeout and Search never showed up in System Settings › Privacy & Security › Location Services. macOS asks once, the first time a site you allowed wants your location, and never at launch; if macOS says no, Search tells you so. Thanks [@tomaswarynyca](https://github.com/tomaswarynyca) for reporting ([#387](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/387))
- Swiping to another space shows its tab groups folded as you left them on the way in. Every tab of a folded group used to come out while the space slid in, and fold back away as it landed.
- A space you left on an empty tab opens on an empty tab again, after Search is quit and opened or from another window, instead of on the last tab of its row, often the last page of a folded tab group, which then came out of its folder.
- Passwords brought over from Chrome include the ones it keeps for your Google account, in a file of their own when Chrome is signed in without sync: read alone, the usual file could hold next to nothing. And Bring Things Over says how many passwords didn't decrypt with the browser's key, instead of counting them as nothing.
- Vimium C's options page opens instead of staying blank, and its keys work in pages. Three things stood in its way. WebKit hands out runtime.getPlatformInfo afresh at each read, and a copy called with another `this`, as Vimium C's worker calls it, never answered: the worker waited on it forever and turned every connection away, from its options page and from the pages its keys work in. A port's listener was handed another object for the same port, without what the worker had kept on it. And an extension page listing several module scripts, one waiting on a top-level await, ran the next one before the first had finished, as Chrome doesn't; such pages now load their modules as one, in their order.
- Start with a fresh window keeps your tab groups, in every space, and the tabs you named. It kept each space's pins and nothing else, so the folders brought in from Arc, which come in as tab groups, went at the next launch after ⌘Q, leaving the pinned tabs alone. It now keeps what tabs that close themselves keep: pins, groups and named tabs. And a space gone to after a fresh start opens on an empty tab beside its pins, as the first one does, instead of on its first pin.
- Stylus's popup and style manager open instead of staying blank. Stylus has the extension's own worker make its first data, as Chrome lets a worker do for an extension's pages; WebKit's worker never sees those requests, so a page that asks this way now gets its data by message instead. Its worker also no longer reports an error on install.
- A page on this Mac opens the files it links to in other folders, as in Safari and Chrome. WebKit lets a file page read only its own folder and dropped the click on a link out of it, so an .html report linking to `../other/page.html` stayed where it was and only the address pasted into a window opened it. The opened page reads its own folder, as one typed in does, and no more. A website's link to a file on this Mac stays refused, as in every browser.
- Settings › Videos wait for a click is followed by tabs already open from their next page or reload, and by a link opened from one of them. Before, a tab open when the switch moved, and any link opened from it, kept the old answer until it slept or Search restarted, so with the switch off again its videos still waited for a click
- A video file opened as a page plays, as in Safari, instead of ending on "The page didn't load". A download site's link to an .mp4 opened the video in a new tab, and the error page covered it while it loaded. A file the site sends as a download still downloads, and a video that can't be reached still says so
- An extension that signs in again quietly, as Claude in Chrome does each time the browser starts, does it out of sight as in Chrome, instead of opening its sign-in page in a tab in front. Claude in Chrome then gets its sign-in back without a tab, and the Claude app can reach it again when Search is signed in to claude.ai
- A link that opens in a new tab works again inside a part of a page drawn in a sandbox, as some AI chats draw their answers. Since the change for asynchronous sign-ins, a click there counted for nothing and the link did nothing; the click now counts for that part of the page and nothing else, so an advertisement in a sandbox still can't open a window from it
- Pinched in on a page, moving about it with two fingers no longer takes you back a page. On a site that can't scroll sideways by itself, a small pan to the left in the middle of the zoomed page went back; now it only moves the view, and a swipe goes back once you are at the page's left edge, as in Safari
- A link ⌘-clicked or middle-clicked in Gmail opens one tab, not two. Gmail's links open by themselves and its page opens them a second time from the same click; Search's own tab for the ⌘-click came on top of the page's. One click now makes one tab, kept behind as a ⌘-click asks, and a window the page opens after it is refused
- Leaving a video's full screen, on YouTube or anywhere, the window no longer corrects itself four or five times in a row: the tabs, the page's card, the bookmarks bar, the side panel and Split View's other page come back at once, while the video is still on its way home, and the page is laid out once, at its own size. Going full screen, the page no longer grows in the window a moment before the video leaves it
- Claude in Chrome signs in: approving it on claude.ai no longer spins for good. A website's message to an extension that isn't installed now fails as it does in Chrome, so claude.ai goes on to the Claude extension you have. Its tab groups are kept with tab groups off too, where it needs them to work on a page, without a heading in the sidebar
- A pin moved to another space with Move to Space stays there. Moved to a space you didn't open before quitting, it was gone after a relaunch. Thanks [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) ([#587](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/587))
- A website whose name begins with what reads as a private address is opened over https. The address field chose plain http for anything typed that started with "10." or "192.168.", which is how a server on this Mac or on the local network is reached, but those were tests on a name rather than on an address: 10.io, 10.ai, 10.org, 10.net and 10.fr are all registered websites, and so is 10.0.0.1.example.com. http://10.io/ answers without redirecting, so the page arrived unencrypted and stayed that way. A local server still gets http, now only when what was typed is four numbers and nothing more, as Dialogs.isLoopback already had it ([#590](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/590))
- Find on Page's close button and other controls respond to clicks with Split View enabled, instead of letting them reach the page underneath. Clicks outside the find bar still reach the page. ([#615](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/615))
- A folded column comes out on the tab you are on, as it was meant to: with more tabs than the window holds, the scroll to it could be lost as the column slid out, and the tab could be out of sight. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#599](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/599))
- Shortcuts Search reads itself, rather than through its menus, work with a Russian, Greek or any other non-Latin keyboard layout on: ⌘W in the small window, for one, did nothing. They were matched by the character the key types, Ц instead of W; they are now read through the Latin layout that goes with it, as macOS reads the menus' own. Latin layouts, AZERTY included, are unchanged. Thanks [@hnnsly](https://github.com/hnnsly)
- A sideways scroll swipes back or forward only when the Mac's own Swipe between pages is on, in System Settings › Trackpad or › Mouse, as in Safari. With it off, a one-finger Magic Mouse scroll inside a page such as a Google Sheet went back a page once the sheet's edge was reached. Thanks [@nourspace](https://github.com/nourspace) for reporting and [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) for the fix ([#464](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/464), [#467](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/467))
- With the window in full screen, the page goes edge to edge: no room around it and no rounded corners, as with a video filling the screen. Out of full screen, the card comes back. Asked for on [#607](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/607)
- The column's greys stay on the strip across the top, in Settings and in the panels: the live tab of a Split View pair, a tab group's name and the buttons there took the darker greys made for the column beside the card.
- The native page accepts the window layout's offered size, so its previous bounds do not set the browser's minimum layout width.
- AdBlock blocks ads again. Its background asked WebKit to watch every kind of request Chrome names, two of which ("webtransport" and "webbundle") WebKit doesn't know, and WebKit refused the listener and stopped the background there: the few network rules built into its lists still applied, but nothing else did, so ads hidden by the page's own layout stayed and its button had nothing behind it. Those two are now left out of the list and the rest is watched as asked
- A build of your own put in place of a release stops offering passkeys on its first run, as it can't do them: sites took the passkey path and failed every time. A build that can offers them again on its first run; from then on the switch is yours. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#608](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/608))
- Prevent cross-site tracking, turned off in Settings › Privacy, lets a site shown inside another site's page keep its cookies, as the switch says. WebKit's own cookie policy still dropped them, so a Kaltura video in a Brightspace course said "your browser is blocking 3rd party session cookies" with the switch off too. Thanks [@Aaditya2605](https://github.com/Aaditya2605) ([#550](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/550))
- An app an extension talks to no longer has its reply cut off when it quits right after writing it. Search called the conversation over as soon as the app quit, and a reply still on its way was lost; it now reads to the end of what the app wrote, as Chrome does. Thanks [@AllDaGearNoIdea](https://github.com/AllDaGearNoIdea) ([#483](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/483))
- Asynchronous sign-ins that submit a POST form into a new tab now open after a real click or key press. Automatic popups remain blocked, with one new tab allowed per recent interaction. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#509](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/509), [#511](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/511))
- Browser import distinguishes macOS refusing access from an empty or missing data folder, keeps partially unreadable profiles visible alongside readable ones, and shows permission guidance in Welcome even when another browser is available. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#415](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/415), [#583](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/583))
- A browser folder chosen for import is kept only for the current session and shown in the import sheet; Chromium JSON reads are limited to 64 MB and checked through the opened file. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#415](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/415))
- Signing in to Affinity Pathfinder from its panel on a website works. Connect Affinity reloaded the website instead of opening the sign-in window: an extension's page in a website's frame that opens, moves or closes a window now has it done by the extension's background, as its other calls already were, where WebKit ended the website's process. Once signed in, the panel never heard of it: a content script reading the address of its own extension's frame got a hidden one, and posting to it threw. And an extension's popup window whose page closes itself now closes, instead of staying open on a blank page. Thanks [@etienne-asc](https://github.com/etienne-asc) ([#612](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/612))
- The address in the tab offers places as you type, as ⌘L's field does: pages from your history, a search, and the rest of an address you have been to, typed in ahead so Return takes it. ↓ and ↑ walk the list, a click takes a row. Thanks [@shohruxsaidov](https://github.com/shohruxsaidov) for asking ([#534](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/534))
- A text file on this Mac opens as text: a hosts file, a .yaml, a Dockerfile or anything else that reads as text was copied to Downloads, and text files that did open showed every accent as two strange letters. Files that aren't text are still downloaded. Thanks [@arvakme](https://github.com/arvakme) ([#525](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/525))
- A regular file typed as an absolute path or a path starting with ~/ opens when the address is submitted. Typing suggestions and dropped text never check the disk for paths. Spaces and special characters in the filename are kept. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#557](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/557), [#523](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/523))
- The folded column and the folded tab bar come and go as quickly as Arc's and Dia's sidebars, timed frame by frame on the same Mac. Out over the page, they're 90% of the way there about 170ms after the pointer reaches the edge, where it took over 300ms. Going back, they're 90% gone about 160ms after the pointer leaves, where they waited 300ms before moving at all. Met by the pointer on their way back in, they turn around within about 10ms. ⌘S folds and unfolds on the same quicker springs. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#585](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/585))
- The folded column starts coming out about 45ms sooner, about 50ms after the pointer reaches the edge where it took nearly 100ms, and the folded tab bar about 25ms sooner. Each was built again, every tab of it, each time it came out; both are now kept just off the window's edge while folded and slid in. Folded, they stay where VoiceOver can reach them. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#586](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/586))
- The site card's corners are round on macOS 26 too. A pale square of the menu's material showed past each rounded corner, and the card's shadow was square; the mask that rounds them before macOS 26 now rounds them on every version. Thanks [@mihxlko](https://github.com/mihxlko) ([#576](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/576))
- Extensions keep checking for updates while Search stays open. Updates needing more access default to Cancel and wait for a browser window. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#589](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/589), [#539](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/539))
- Extensions hear once when Search actually restarts their worker, from content scripts and their own pages alike, including a recovery requested by another page. Old ports close without polling, replaying messages or reconnecting behind the extension’s back. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#453](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/453), [#514](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/514))
- Floating videos fit without cutoff from a player’s minimum size, margins, borders, padding, clips or masks. The page’s styles return when the video does. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#619](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/619), [#591](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/591))
- The floating video goes out and comes back in one step, by ⇧⌘P or by switching tabs or apps. Going out, the little window showed the video at a fraction of its size before it filled the window; coming back, the tab showed the video alone at the little window's size, then the page around it, then the video growing into its place. The little window now opens on the video's last frame, and the tab on the page as you left it, each until the page underneath has settled, never more than a second and a half. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#562](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/562))
- A link from another app opens in its small window alone. The browser's windows stay where they were: closed, in the Dock or behind another app. A launch made by the link keeps every saved window off screen until one is wanted. Open in Search, another link, the Dock or ⌘N brings them all back. Copying the address, reloading and zooming in the small window act on its own page and say so at its foot. They used to reach the browser's tab behind it, and on a launch made by a link, ⌘W closed a tab in a window you couldn't see. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#431](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/431))
- A window closed while others stay open is let go. It stayed behind with its views, its browser and its key monitor, one more for every window opened and closed, and each closed window's monitor still ran on every key press and click. Thanks [@Aaditya2605](https://github.com/Aaditya2605) ([#549](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/549))
- A window put away in the Dock can be found again: it shows in the Dock icon's menu and the Window menu, named for its page, as Safari's windows are. Its windows had no name, and macOS leaves an unnamed window out of both, so one minimized with a tab moved into it could not be brought back. A private tab's window is only called Private Tab, and the traffic lights stay in line as the name changes. Thanks [@Qsev](https://github.com/Qsev) ([#554](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/554))
- A copy of Search built with Xcode 27 looks like the macOS it runs on: the window's buttons and controls come out in macOS 27's style on macOS 27, where every one of them was drawn as on macOS 14. It still runs on macOS 14 and later, as before. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#489](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/489))
- A tab narrow enough to be down to its mark keeps a faint ground, the same one the pinned squares wear, so a run of them still reads as tabs. Past the width that holds a title a tab had no ground at all unless it was the live one, selected, or under the pointer, which after an import is a row of marks with nothing behind them. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#629](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/629), [#449](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/449))
- The built-in blocker matches whole domain names: a rule for segment.com no longer blocks unrelated names such as segment.company. Subdomains and explicit ports still match. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#626](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/626), [#622](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/622))
- ⌘T puts the caret in the address field every time, and what you type straight after it lands there. The field could arrive a moment after it was asked for the keys, more often with Split View on, and then the request went nowhere: no caret, and typing did nothing until you clicked the field. Letters typed before the field arrived went missing too; they now wait for it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#635](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/635))
- Extension popups such as Google Translate keep their width when the Mac always shows scrollbars, instead of shrinking to a narrow strip ([#648](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/648), [#645](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/645))
- Twitch's chat is open again when the floating video comes back to its tab, as it was when the video went out. In its Studio mode, Twitch folds the chat away in a page as small as the little window and never opens it again by itself ([#642](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/642))
- The column's tab highlight reaches the tab you pick sooner, with the quick motion and no spring trailing behind when you switch fast. Reduce Motion still makes it immediate ([#649](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/649), [#466](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/466))
- Showing the list of saved accounts under a sign-in field no longer reads every account's password: only the one you pick is read, so a password kept by another build of Search asks for the keychain once, not once per account each time the caret goes in. Thanks [@FabianoC13](https://github.com/FabianoC13) ([#637](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/637))
- A paused video gives the media keys back. Once a tab had played sound, the play/pause keys and AirPods taps went to it even after the video was paused, so Spotify or Music couldn't have them back until they played something again. Now, ten seconds after a tab goes quiet, what was playing there is muted while it is paused, which lets the keys go, and unmuted when it plays again; a press of play/pause soon after pausing still resumes it. While something is playing in a tab, the keys stay with it. Thanks [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi), and [@jesusrp98](https://github.com/jesusrp98) for reporting and for finding the way ([#512](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/512), [#605](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/605))
- Extensions work in private tabs, content scripts and blocking rules such as uBlock Origin Lite's included, once you allow each one from its … menu in Settings › Extensions. Each starts off, and the old switch for all of them isn't carried over; one whose manifest refuses private tabs stays out. An extension you allow sees which tabs are private, as in Chrome. Turned off again, it loses them at once, though a script already running in one stops only when the page reloads. Private tabs opened before any extension was allowed need reopening. Thanks [@Vedaant-Rajoo](https://github.com/Vedaant-Rajoo), and [@AtikshB](https://github.com/AtikshB) for reporting ([#508](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/508), [#536](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/536))
- Password suggestions stay out of search and issue-title fields, including GitHub's search box, while two-step sign-ins still offer saved accounts. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#631](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/631), [#647](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/647))
- Signing in with a security key no longer crashes when the key returns no user handle, as reported with Okta. The site receives a null user handle, as WebAuthn allows. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee) ([#548](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/548), [#646](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/646))
- A username in Settings › Passwords can be selected and copied, as the password beside it already could. It was the one half of a sign-in the list would not give you back. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech), and [@BeNDYGo](https://github.com/BeNDYGo) for asking ([#634](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/634), [#652](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/652))
- Closing a call tab, or putting down a pinned tab, stops its camera and microphone even while the page is still held in memory, so the camera's light goes off with the tab. Calls in other tabs keep going. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee), and [@dalxds](https://github.com/dalxds) for reporting ([#655](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/655), [#657](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/657))
- Waking a sleeping tab keeps the column in place, even after the window was resized while it slept: the picture of the page shown while it wakes stays within the page's room. Thanks [@BryanZaneee](https://github.com/BryanZaneee), and [@dalxds](https://github.com/dalxds) for reporting ([#664](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/664), [#670](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/670))
- A tab dragged along the row across the top moves itself again, not the whole window. macOS starts a title bar's drag before the app sees the press, so making the window unmovable at the press came too late with a real mouse; it is now unmovable while the pointer is over the row, and movable everywhere else for Move & Resize and tiling. Thanks [@etienne-asc](https://github.com/etienne-asc) ([#668](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/668))
- With Split View on, the saved accounts under a sign-in field show again. The list came up, but under the page, so nothing appeared and a click there went to the page; it now hangs over the page, as the find bar does, and a click off it still reaches the page. Thanks [@S-Efekan](https://github.com/S-Efekan) ([#667](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/667))

### Known issue

- Claude in Chrome's side panel may ask you to sign in again: WebKit doesn't send claude.ai's session cookies to an extension's own pages. WebKit has fixed it upstream; it comes with a macOS update.

## 1.0.5 — 14 October 2026

### Added

- With the tabs down the side, the page sits in the window as a card, with a little room around it and rounded corners, apart from the column. Two pages side by side are a card each, the bookmarks bar lies above the card on the window's ground, and the card keeps its room with the column folded away. A video filling the screen, and the tabs across the top, look as they did.
- Arc's pinned list comes in with the shape Arc gave it, when Pinned rows is on: a page Arc keeps loose becomes a row under the pins, a page it filed in a folder stays a tab in a group of that name, and the sheet says so before you bring anything over. A pin is never in a group, so filing the folder's pages as rows would have been the folder's name thrown away; with Pinned rows off, the sheet offers to turn them on right there, beside Arc's line, and without them the whole list comes in as tabs, as before. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#582](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/582), [#478](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/478))
- Close Group, in a tab group's menu, closes the group and every tab in it. One ⇧⌘T brings it all back, every tab in its place and the group named as it was, however many tabs it held. Asked for by email; thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) for bringing it back whole ([#473](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/473))
- Pinned rows, as in Arc: with the tabs down the side, a pin can be a row under the squares instead of a square. The squares are for the sites you live in, the rows for pages you keep, and a line sets them apart from the tabs that come and go. Right-click a tab and choose Pin as Row, or Show as Row and Show as Square on a pin you have. A row is a pin in every other way: ⌘W puts it down rather than closing it, it's the same in every window, and Back to Pinned Page in its menu takes it home. Clear, at the end of the line under the pointer, closes the tabs below it; pins and tab groups stay, and one ⇧⌘T brings back everything it closed, each tab in its place and the one you were on in front. Turned off, rows are drawn as squares and come back when it's on again. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Pinned rows. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#426](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/426), [#183](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/183))
- Bringing things over offers the bookmarks bar at the end, beside Show bookmarks, when bookmarks have just come in and the bar is off. One press turns it on and puts the sheet away, so the row appears where it will live; nothing is turned on by itself, and the switch stays in Settings › Tabs as before. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#485](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/485), [#449](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/449))
- The ⌃Tab switcher takes the pointer: resting on a card picks it once the pointer has moved, and a click takes it, ⌃ held or not; a click outside puts the switcher away. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#358](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/358))
- Search a site from the address field, as in Arc: type the start of a site's name, like red or yout, press Tab, and what you type next searches that site. Reddit, YouTube, X, ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Wikipedia, GitHub and a few others come built in, and a site you visit that says where its search is joins them by itself. Your own site shortcuts work as before. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Search a site from the address field
- Search can start with a fresh window: your pinned tabs are there and last time's other tabs aren't. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Start with a fresh window ([#406](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/406))
- Websites can ask for your location, as in Safari: a card names the site and offers Allow once, Always allow or Don't allow, and Always allow and Don't allow are kept per site (Settings › Privacy › Forget choices, now for camera, microphone and location). Only the page on screen asks, for itself or a frame of the same site and address; a frame from elsewhere is refused, and a private tab keeps nothing. macOS asks once whether Search may use your location at all. The camera and microphone card, like this one, takes no click in its first half second. Thanks [@tomaswarynyca](https://github.com/tomaswarynyca) for asking ([#387](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/387))
- Search runs on Intel Macs, with a download of its own beside the Apple Silicon one: one app for both would have weighed twice as much for everyone. Each updates itself from a feed of its own, so neither kind of Mac is ever handed the other's. Thanks [@Assim-Genshi](https://github.com/Assim-Genshi) for asking ([#46](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/46)), and [@geoffreybautistadesign](https://github.com/geoffreybautistadesign) for running it on an Intel Mac first ([#175](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/175))
- Passkeys under the sign-in field, as in Safari: on a site that offers them there (GitHub, Google and others), the passkeys this Mac holds for it show first in the list under the name box, beside the saved passwords, and a click on one brings up the Mac's sheet for that passkey and signs you in. Nothing reaches the site until you pick one; a site in a frame is offered none. Thanks [@RayBytes](https://github.com/RayBytes) for asking ([#17](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/17))
- Find on Page says where you are, as in “3 of 17”, and goes round from the last match to the first. Its menu can match case or whole words only. Without Match case, a letter typed without its accent also finds it with one, as before: “ete” finds “été”. On a very long page the count stops at 1000 and shows “1000+” until Next goes further, as Safari's does. PDFs say whether there is a match, without a count. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#377](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/377))
- Split View, to see two tabs side by side: drag a tab onto the left or right edge of a page, choose Open in Split View in a tab's menu, or press ⌥⌘N for an empty page beside the one you're on, which lists your open tabs to bring one in (Esc if you change your mind). Drag the divider between the pages: it settles at even halves, a double-click evens them out, and a right-click on it swaps the pages, separates them or closes both. ⌃⌘← and ⌃⌘→ go from one page to the other, ⌘W closes only the one you're in, and ⇧⌘T puts it back beside the other. A pinned page splits as a copy beside the other page, and the pin stays where it is. A peeked link can be kept beside the page it came from (the peek's new button, or ⌥⌘Return). A page of the pair that asks something (an alert, a confirm, a prompt) asks over its own half, named for the site asking, and the other page goes on working. In the column a pair is two lines, one under the other. Both pages stay awake, each Space keeps its pairs and their widths, after a restart too, and turned off, Split View keeps your pairs for when it is back on. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#381](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/381)), and [@SamarthaB10](https://github.com/SamarthaB10) for the first version ([#280](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/280)), and [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) for Find on Page over the page you're in ([#470](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/470))
- Downloads can be paused and resumed from the Downloads panel, and one that stops on its own keeps the reason and can go on from where it was, when the server allows it, or start again. The ring, the Dock and Finder go on from what had already come in. Only while Search stays open; a download sent by a form isn't asked for again. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#378](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/378))
- Find on Page paints every match on the page yellow, the one you're on orange, as Safari and Chrome do. The selection alone was drawn pale grey while the find bar had the keys, so a match was hard to see. Matches in fields keep only their selection. Thanks [@ductan2](https://github.com/ductan2) ([#409](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/409))
- Websites can send notifications, as in Safari. A site that asks gets a card over its own page, with Allow and Don't allow. Its notifications then come as the Mac's own, named by the site and with its icon, and clicking one brings its tab back to the front. Settings › Privacy lists the sites you allowed, each with Remove, under Let sites ask to send notifications, which you can turn off. Forget choices takes them all back. A private tab is never asked. Thanks [@willian](https://github.com/willian) for reporting ([#328](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/328))
- Extensions that record your screen work: Loom, Screencastify, Awesome Screenshot, and those that record from an offscreen page, such as Tella, ScreenPal and Vidyard. The first time one asks, Search asks you, naming it; after that, it asks again unless you've just used that extension (its button within the last minute, or its own pages). Then the Mac's own picker asks what to share, every time. While an extension records, or uses the camera or microphone, a small pill at the bottom of the screen says so, with Stop, and its tab wears a mark; a website sharing your screen gets the pill too. Settings › Extensions lists the extensions you let record, each with Remove. Loom's microphone and camera bubble work too. What WebKit can't give: a single tab on its own (a recorder that falls back to the screen, as Loom does, records the window), and the sound of the screen or a tab. Asked for by email
- A bookmark can be its icon alone on the bookmarks bar, as in Chrome and Safari: empty its name in the card ⇧⌘B opens, or with Rename… in the bookmarks list, and the bar keeps only its site's icon, in a square as tall as the rest, which saves room. The Bookmarks menu, the bookmarks list and the folders' menus show its address in place of a name, so they still read. Asked for on X
- A site's videos can stay in their page: Don't Float Videos Here, in the site card of YouTube, Netflix, Twitch and the other sites a video floats from, keeps it from floating out when you switch tabs or apps. It is kept per site and forgotten with the other site choices (Settings › Privacy › Forget choices); ⇧⌘P still floats a video by hand. Thanks [@merttopuz](https://github.com/merttopuz) ([#433](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/433), [#267](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/267))

### Fixed

- Security hardening across extensions, sign-in sheets and site icons, from a third careful private report. Thanks again to Vahagn Yengibaryan. No extension is ever given another extension's pages, whatever its manifest or a later request names, and a tab showing another extension's page gives an extension nothing of it: no script, no address, no picture. A site's name-and-password sheet, a warning about a site's certificate and "Open app?" from a tab in the background wait until you go to that tab, as its alerts already did. An extension opens a file it downloaded only right after you click or type in it, talks to an app on this Mac only when its manifest asks to, and a sign-in it starts and nobody finishes ends after ten minutes. Hiding something on a page in a private tab is no longer remembered, site icons are read only in the usual picture formats and sizes, and ⌘L no longer shows a name and password written into an address.
- A page in a tab you aren't looking at asks for the camera or microphone, or to choose a file, only once you go to it, and the question names the site, as its alerts do. In Split View, the other page's questions wait the same way.
- A camera or microphone answer given to an extension's frame inside a website is kept for the extension, not the website.
- Extensions stay off files opened from this Mac, as in Chrome with file access left off. An extension loaded from a folder keeps only the links that lead to its own files, and its downloads go through one of your own tabs, never a private one.
- A picture's menu opens only from a real right-click, Copy Image fetches the picture without your sign-ins and up to a size limit, a popup window shows the address it is really on and stays on screen, and only web links can be dropped onto the tabs.
- Importing from Chrome, Brave, Edge, Arc and the like takes only the passwords the browser encrypted, as every one of them has for years; one lying in the clear in a profile's files is left out.
- A password saved for a sign-in with no name, only a password or access code to type, is offered again on your next visit and shows in Settings › Passwords. It was saved but never read back.
- Sign-ins that ask for your name or email first, then your password on the next page — impots.gouv.fr, Google, Microsoft — offer your saved accounts on the first page too, and the name you gave there is the one saved with the password.
- Your Mac's text replacements work as you type in pages, as in Safari and every other app, following the Mac's own setting (System Settings › Keyboard). Edit › Substitutions turns them off or on, and smart quotes and smart dashes too, which stay off in pages unless you turn them on there. Asked for by email and on X
- A website an extension lists in its manifest can send it messages, as in Chrome: claude.ai, for one, hands Claude in Chrome its sign-in that way, and approving it ended in "Authorization failed". Only the sites an extension names get to, by Chrome's own rules, and Google's sites are left out. A page already open when Search starts needs a reload first. Thanks [@cunyiles](https://github.com/cunyiles) for tracking it down ([#496](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/496))
- Popovers keep their rounded corners before macOS 26: on macOS 15 the bookmarks list, the card for a bookmark, the extensions list and the site card showed square corners past their rounded edge. Asked for by email
- Pinned extensions no longer push the column off the side. With more pinned than the bottom row had room for, the whole column slid left and was cut off at both edges, with the puzzle and bookmarks buttons out of reach. Now the pinned ones that don't fit give way, from the last, and are still in the puzzle's list; the space, puzzle, bookmarks and downloads buttons stay put. Thanks [@Vedaant-Rajoo](https://github.com/Vedaant-Rajoo) for the measurements that pointed straight at it ([#535](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/535))
- The Bookmarks menu keeps its bookmarks while a tab's title keeps changing. With a download page showing its speed in its title, they disappeared from the open menu at each change. Asked for by email
- ⌃Tab brings up the switcher with its previews while Find is open, too: it used to skip straight to the next tab. The find bar stays open and looks for the same words on the tab you pick. Thanks [@rafe-sh](https://github.com/rafe-sh) for reporting it ([#553](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/553))
- Each page of Settings opens at its top. Going to another page after reading down General opened it as far down. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#479](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/479))
- An extension's row in Settings › Extensions keeps its actions in a … menu, also on right-click. Pin, Show in New Tabs, Options and Remove were pills beside the name that wrapped a letter at a time when they didn't fit. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#479](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/479))
- The hidden column's shadow leaves with it. Sliding back out of the window, the column was gone while its shadow still lay along the window's edge, then vanished at once; it now fades as the column slides and is gone when the column is. The same for the tabs across the top, folded away. Thanks [@Qddog23](https://github.com/Qddog23) for reporting and [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) for the fix ([#441](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/441), [#462](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/462))
- ⌘W closes what's in front first: a peek, then a panel over the page (Settings, Bookmarks, Passwords, History, Downloads, What's New…), and only then the tab, in the same order as Escape. With Settings open, ⌘W used to close the page behind it. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#452](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/452))
- An extension's items in a page's right-click menu show once: WebKit already puts them there, and Search added them a second time. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#456](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/456))
- The bookmarks list opens tall enough for its folders. With one folder at the top, as bookmarks brought in from Dia are, the list under the bookmark button was a single row high, and opening the folder showed a sliver of it above Add This Page: the list took its height from the closed folders as it opened and never grew. It is now as tall as the whole tree would be with every folder open, up to the height it always had at most, and a top level that is one folder alone opens with that folder open.
- The column comes back after a video's full screen. Leaving a video's own full screen with Escape, on YouTube for one, could leave the tabs gone until the column was switched off and on again in the View menu: the window heard of a page going full screen and coming back only when something else changed, and coming back there sometimes was nothing else.
- A live video no longer jumps every few seconds, on X and wherever a player keeps up with the broadcast by nudging its speed. X's switches between normal speed and a touch faster all the time, and each switch through normal speed made WebKit rebuild the sound and hold the picture still meanwhile. Once a page has nudged a video's speed, Search plays its normal speed a hair off exactly 1 (a tenth of a millisecond a second), so the switches go through without a break; the page still reads the speed it asked for.
- An extension's popup window answers for itself: windows.getCurrent from its page gives that window rather than the one in front, so Bitwarden's passkey window finds and sizes itself, and the window is tied to its page before the extension hears of it. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#408](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/408))
- The floating video fills its window on players built like Twitch's: an ancestor with a transform, a filter or containment placed the video inside its own box, so part of the picture or none of it showed, and one drawn only on screen or faded out left the window black.
- Many tabs no longer slow the tabs down: with tab groups on, each tab worked out the width of every other, so a space of 300 tabs took more than a second to leave; it takes a few hundredths now. Going back to a space no longer reads its whole file again either.
- Signing in with a passkey works on GitHub again: after Touch ID the site couldn't read the passkey Search handed back (its sign-in code sets a method on it that Search had made read-only). Thanks [@neerajyadav](https://github.com/neerajyadav) ([#407](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/407))
- A game played on the arrow keys, or any page that reads keys without taking them, no longer makes the Mac beep on each press. Search kept only the last key quiet, so keys pressed quickly or held down beeped, and WebKit hands most such keys to the view around the page rather than back to the page, where nothing wanted them and they fell off the end of the responder chain. Pages that scroll still scroll with the arrows, page up and down and the space bar. Thanks [@pyrossh](https://github.com/pyrossh) for reporting and [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) for the fix ([#402](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/402), [#410](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/410))
- Search is built for size now, at the same speed (launch, scrolling and typing measured side by side), and its icon catalog is packed tighter with every Dock style kept: everything this version adds fits in 6.8 MB, where 1.0.4 was 6.1.
- Large exported files and folders are brought in in the background, with progress and a Cancel button. Folder imports only visit the chosen folder and its immediate subfolders; deeper trees are left alone. Cancelling an import stops further work and says if anything was already brought in. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#380](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/380))
- Pages that change constantly stay quick: a chat writing out its answer, a feed filling as you scroll. Search looked through the whole page for a sign-in form each time the page changed, and a long conversation got slower with every word it wrote (3,000 words took up to 3.7 s instead of 0.3 s, climbing as it went). It now looks a quarter of a second after changes, at most four times a second, and no longer on every frame of a scroll unless the caret is in a box.
- Extensions hear what their own background sends their pages. WebKit delivered none of those messages, so Bitwarden's passkey window stayed blank and its sync timed out; each is now passed on to the extension's pages as well. Thanks [@dttdrv](https://github.com/dttdrv) ([#383](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/383), [#388](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/388))
- An extension's popup follows the width its page asks for, narrower as well as wider: Bitwarden set to narrow no longer leaves an empty strip down the popup's side. Thanks [@dttdrv](https://github.com/dttdrv) ([#384](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/384))
- Search no longer offers to save a password typed into an extension's own page, such as Bitwarden's unlock PIN. Thanks [@dttdrv](https://github.com/dttdrv) ([#385](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/385))
- An extension can move and size a window with windows.update, as in Chrome; WebKit refused it as not implemented, and Bitwarden's pop-out logged an error each time it opened. Thanks [@dttdrv](https://github.com/dttdrv) ([#386](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/386))
- History keeps pages that differ only by http or https, by port, or by the case of their path as separate places, so localhost:3000 and localhost:4000 no longer overwrite each other; typing still matches either case. A name and password written into an address are no longer kept in the history. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#374](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/374))
- Search › Check for Updates… follows the update as it goes: Install Update when updates are set to wait, Downloading Update… while it comes in, Download Update… when Search can't swap itself where it is, and Restart to Update once it's in place. The line at the foot of the window points there. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#373](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/373))
- An extension that fails to install, update or reload no longer takes the working copy with it: the one you had stays, running, with its settings, and only a copy that is fully in place replaces it. Thanks [@lulkebit](https://github.com/lulkebit) ([#376](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/376))
- Bookmarks inside a folder wear their sites' icons again, in the folder opened from the bookmarks bar and in the Bookmarks menu, as the ones on the bar itself do. One whose site has no icon kept yet, brought in and never visited, wears the bar's letter in its place, so its name lines up with the others; a folder inside a folder too. Asked for on X
- An extension's popup window, such as a password manager's vault or an extension's sign-in, opens as a small window of its page, at the size the extension asks for, with the site over it, instead of as another whole browser window. ⌘W closes it; it isn't brought back with the other windows after a restart, and it closes when its extension is turned off or removed. NordPass's vault and Affinity's Connect open this way.
- In full screen, the tabs across the top and the back, forward and reload buttons in the column start at the edge, where an empty corner was kept for the window's buttons that macOS takes away there.
- Copy Image works on pictures a page makes itself, such as the photos in WhatsApp Web: it said it couldn't copy them, since they only exist inside the page, and they are now read there.
- A pinned tab you drag to another place keeps up with the pointer, as a tab does: each move of the hand redrew the whole column.
- Tampermonkey installs a script from a link on Greasy Fork, OpenUserJS, GitHub, GitLab and the other script sites it knows, as in Chrome: the rules it catches those links with weren't in a form WebKit takes, so none of them loaded. A .user.js link anywhere else still needs the script pasted into its editor, since WebKit can't hold that one rule beside the others.
- Escape on a new tab you haven't typed in or gone anywhere from closes it and takes you back to the tab you were on, as if the ⌘T hadn't happened. Anything typed keeps it, and so does it being the only tab. Thanks [@bhanni01](https://github.com/bhanni01) ([#393](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/393), [#389](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/389))
- Words typed into a new tab's address field and not yet sent are still there when you come back to it from another tab, with the cursor after them. They are kept in memory only, and go once you send them or close the tab. Thanks [@bhanni01](https://github.com/bhanni01) ([#394](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/394), [#390](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/390))
- An extension page shown inside a website can run a function in a tab with scripting.executeScript, as in Chrome: the function was lost on its way to the extension's worker, and the call refused. Aino's web clipper works again. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#403](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/403))
- SingleFile saves pages again: its worker failed as it started, and a file it made in its worker couldn't be downloaded. Thanks [@quanru](https://github.com/quanru) ([#405](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/405))
- The bookmarks list lines up again, in the dropdown and in the manager. With no folders, each bookmark kept an empty place for a folder's arrow in front of it, and an empty band sat under the last one. The line showing where a dragged bookmark would go no longer stays behind once it is let go somewhere else. Thanks [@tomaswarynyca](https://github.com/tomaswarynyca) for reporting ([#391](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/391))
- Extensions that read pages in an offscreen document work, Lokal among them. The document was answered as ready before it had loaded, and a failed or reloaded one was left behind. A site framed inside it couldn't answer the extension. Now each extension gets one document that is finished loading before it is used, never shown, and closed when the extension is reloaded or turned off. Thanks [@K-NRS](https://github.com/K-NRS) ([#192](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/192))
- The address being edited in a tab scrolls with the caret: the field was as wide as the whole address and the row cut it off, so ← and → moved the caret out of sight and the end of a long address never showed. Thanks [@StefanTodorov32](https://github.com/StefanTodorov32) ([#421](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/421), [#419](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/419))
- The site card opens above the address of a tab near the bottom of the screen, when there is no room under it. Pushed up to fit, it covered the field the address was being edited in, and what was typed there. Thanks [@StefanTodorov32](https://github.com/StefanTodorov32) ([#420](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/420), [#418](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/418))
- Two sites on the same address with different ports, such as localhost:3000 and localhost:4321, each have their own icon: icons were kept by the host alone, so two local projects wore whichever icon came last. Thanks [@wouter-deen](https://github.com/wouter-deen) for reporting ([#413](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/413))
- A site that changes its icon after it has loaded, as GitHub does with its theme, shows the new one, and a site's light and dark icons no longer take each other's place. Thanks [@keyding](https://github.com/keyding) for reporting ([#423](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/423))
- Pins and pinned tabs brought over from Arc wear their icons at once, taken from Arc's own icons as the bookmarks' are, instead of a letter until each page was opened. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#424](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/424), [#416](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/416))
- Esc on a question over a panel answers the question. With a bookmarks folder's “Remove?” up over the bookmarks panel, Esc closed the panel and left the question up. Thanks [@karadoganyi](https://github.com/karadoganyi) ([#502](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/502))
- A small window for a link from another app says so when its page fails to load, with Try again, as a tab does. It used to stay blank. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#491](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/491))
- A long address can be read and edited all the way along, in the tab and in the address field on a new tab: the field never scrolled, so what ran past its edge was cut off and the caret, Home and End, ⌘← and ⌘→ and a click couldn't reach it. Thanks [@shohruxsaidov](https://github.com/shohruxsaidov) for reporting and [@razbyskov](https://github.com/razbyskov) for the fix ([#517](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/517), [#555](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/555))
- ⌘N from a window in full screen opens the new window in full screen too, in a Space of its own, as Safari and Chrome do. It used to open over the full-screen window, as large as the screen. Thanks [@wouter-deen](https://github.com/wouter-deen) for reporting and [@jorgelso](https://github.com/jorgelso) for the fix ([#515](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/515), [#531](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/531))
- Bringing Arc over twice no longer gives a site two pins. A favourite was matched against a pin by its address, letter for letter, so a pin at mail.google.com that answers at mail.google.com/mail/u/0/ looked like a different site and came in again; a favourite whose site is already pinned is now the pin you have. Two favourites of Arc's own on one site are still two. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#482](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/482), [#500](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/500))
- A tab that goes from one local site to another on the same host, such as localhost:3000 to localhost:5173, changes to the new site's icon at once instead of keeping the old one until the page has loaded, and an icon brought over from another browser for a page on a port never falls back to the one for the same host without it. Thanks [@theosementa](https://github.com/theosementa) ([#575](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/575))
- Bringing Arc's spaces over keeps the names of its folders even when tab groups are off, as the session already keeps them: the folders were thrown away as they arrived, so turning tab groups on afterwards found nothing left and the only way back was to bring Arc over again. Thanks [@Chahine-tech](https://github.com/Chahine-tech) ([#478](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/478), [#494](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/494))
- Download Linked File in a link's right-click menu saves the file. It did nothing: WebKit fetched the file and had nowhere to put it. Thanks [@Aaditya2605](https://github.com/Aaditya2605) ([#505](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/505))
- The folded column slides out over the page more smoothly, in full screen above all: its shadow was drawn again from every row and icon in it on each frame of the slide, down the column's whole height, and now falls from the plain ground under it, which looks the same. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#499](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/499))
- The tab you're on can be closed with a click when the tabs are down to their icons: with the pointer on it, its icon becomes the cross, as in Chrome. The other tabs keep their icon, so a click to go to one never closes it. Before, only ⌘W, a middle-click or the menu closed a tab that narrow. Thanks [@Aaditya2605](https://github.com/Aaditya2605) ([#504](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/504))
- An extension whose background file is a link to another of its own files gets Search's Chrome APIs, as StopTheMadness's `background-143.js` does: the link is replaced by a copy of the file it points to, so the additions can go at its top. Thanks [@AllDaGearNoIdea](https://github.com/AllDaGearNoIdea) ([#481](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/481))
- Open Link in New Tab, in a link's right-click menu, leaves you where you are: the tab opens behind the one you're on and next to it in the row, as in Safari and Chrome and as with a ⌘-click. It used to take you there, so a tab in the background needed ⌘ held or a mouse's middle button. Hold ⌥ with the menu open for Open Link in New Tab and Go to It, which does what the menu did before. Thanks [@oddharsh](https://github.com/oddharsh) ([#551](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/551)), and to the people who asked for it by email
- An extension loaded from a folder gets the id Chrome gives it, worked out from where the folder is, so the app it comes with accepts it through its native messaging host, as StopTheMadness's does. Each one got a made-up id before; one loaded that way keeps it until it is removed and loaded again. Removing an extension clears what it kept, so one loaded later from the same folder starts with nothing of it. Thanks [@AllDaGearNoIdea](https://github.com/AllDaGearNoIdea) ([#440](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/440))

## 1.0.4 — 27 September 2026

### Added

- The downloads button can stay beside the other buttons all the time, and open Downloads in one click; while a file comes in it fills as before. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Downloads › Always show the downloads button. Thanks [@hkhrithik007](https://github.com/hkhrithik007) for asking ([#353](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/353))
- A site can play sound by itself, as with Safari's Allow All Auto-Play: Play Sound by Itself in the site card (a click on the tab you are on, or Site Information… in its menu) lets that site's videos start with their sound, from its next page on. Off for every site unless you turn it on; private tabs never remember it, and with Videos wait for a click on (Settings › General) no site plays by itself. Thanks [@png125](https://github.com/png125) for asking ([#223](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/223))
- The floating video docks at the side of the screen, as in Dia: with Flick the floating video to a corner on (Settings › General), two fingers at the side the window is against pull it, heavily, and a strong enough swipe slides it off, leaving a sliver of it; not far enough and it springs back. A swipe back, a click on the sliver or a sideways wheel's notch brings it out again, and closed while docked it opens again where it was. Not at a side where another screen carries on: there a swipe flicks it as before. Thanks [@farchanrifai](https://github.com/farchanrifai) ([#229](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/229))
- After an update, a small card says what's new: the new switches, each with what it does and the switch itself, and the switches from earlier versions still off, so nobody misses them. Closed, it doesn't come back for that version, and a new install shows the welcome instead. Every version's notes, in lists, are in Settings › About › What's New…
- Several windows: ⌘N, or New Window in the Dock icon's menu, opens another window with tabs of its own, in the space of the window you're in; each window shows its own space. Menus and shortcuts act on the window in front, and a link from another app opens there. Closing a window while others stay open closes its tabs, and ⇧⌘T brings the whole window back; closing the last one leaves Search running, as Safari does, and the Dock icon, ⌘N or a link brings it back with its tabs ([#327](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/327)). Every window comes back after quitting, where it was. Pinned tabs are the same in every window: pinned, unpinned or moved in one, so in the others, each window keeping its own page for each pin. A tab moves to another window, or a new one, from its menu (Move to Window), or by dragging it out of the row and letting go over another window or anywhere else; its page goes as it is. Extensions see every window: windows.getAll, windows.create and windows.onFocusChanged work, and each tab says which window it is in.
- AppleScript can read your tabs, in Safari's words: `URL of current tab of front window`, and `name`, `tabs` and `count` beside it, in every window, so Raycast, Alfred and scripts like sesh's can take the page you're on. Read-only: nothing can be opened, closed, changed or run in a page, and private tabs are never shown — with one in front, the window has no current tab. Only apps you allow in System Settings › Privacy & Security › Automation can ask. Thanks [@joshmedeski](https://github.com/joshmedeski) ([#233](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/233), [#232](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/232))
- Site shortcuts: a word of your own before a search sends it to that site's search, whatever engine you've picked — `yt cats` to YouTube's. Settings › General › Site shortcuts takes the word and the site's search address with %s where the words go, and saves only an http or https address with one %s in it; there are none until you add one. Thanks [@merttopuz](https://github.com/merttopuz) ([#188](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/188))
- Tabs opened behind the page can wait to load until you go to them, as tabs brought back from last time do: a ⌘-clicked or middle-clicked link, a bookmark opened with the middle button, and a batch of links from another app. A tab opened in front, ⇧⌘-click included, loads at once, and tabs an extension opens always load. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › Tabs › Load background tabs when you go to them. Thanks [@dttdrv](https://github.com/dttdrv) ([#196](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/196), [#195](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/195))
- Bookmarks go in the order you put them: drag one above or below another in the list under the bookmark button or in Bookmarks › Show Bookmarks, or onto the middle of a folder to file it there; a closed folder held over opens. The bar and the Bookmarks menu keep the same order.
- A word in the address field can reach Search itself instead of the web: "settings", "new tab", "new private tab", "new space", "bookmarks", "history", "downloads", "passwords" and "sidebar" come up as the first suggestion, and Return goes there. Only when that word is all you typed: "settings for gmail" still searches. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Address bar commands. Thanks [@merttopuz](https://github.com/merttopuz) ([#212](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/212))
- ⌘Return in the address field opens what is typed there in a new tab, behind the one you are on, and leaves that tab as it was, as in Safari; ⇧⌘Return goes to the new tab. Return alone still takes the tab you are on there. Thanks [@merttopuz](https://github.com/merttopuz) ([#311](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/311)), and [@junguye-BRONGA](https://github.com/junguye-BRONGA) for asking ([#249](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/249))
- Hold a back or forward swipe to pick a page from history, as in Dia: once the swipe is far enough to go, keep your fingers down and the last pages that way appear in place of the arrow, the nearest lit. Moving up or down slides the list under the light, and letting go goes to the lit page. A quick swipe goes back or forward as before. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Hold a swipe to pick from history. Thanks [@farchanrifai](https://github.com/farchanrifai) ([#191](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/191))
- ⇧⌘B keeps the page and opens a small card off the bookmark button, to give it another name or file it in a folder, a new one included, or take it back with Remove. On a page already kept, ⇧⌘B opens the same card, and the button is filled. With the tabs folded away it says "Bookmarked", as before.
- Search your bookmarks in Bookmarks › Show Bookmarks: what matches is listed with the folders it is in, Return opens the first, and Show in Folder opens the list down to it. Thanks [@binaryfields](https://github.com/binaryfields) ([#370](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/370))
- A search in the address field's suggestions wears the search engine's own icon in place of the magnifying glass when this Mac already has it, from a visit to the site. Nothing is fetched for it; an engine whose icon isn't known keeps the glass. Thanks [@merttopuz](https://github.com/merttopuz) ([#364](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/364))
- Videos can wait for a click, as with Safari's Never Auto-Play: they don't start by themselves, even without sound. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Videos wait for a click; tabs already open follow once closed and opened again, or after they've slept.
- Switching spaces, the space's name shows for a moment beside its icon, where the tabs are, rather than at the bottom of the window; with the tabs folded away it still shows at the bottom.
- A double-click on the pinned tab you are on takes it back to the page it was pinned at, wherever you have wandered since; already there, it changes the pin's letter as before. Thanks [@armin-ahmadii](https://github.com/armin-ahmadii) for the idea ([#141](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/141))
- A pinned tab shows the loading ring while its page comes, as other tabs do, in the bar and the column.
- Downloads show while they happen: a small circle beside the other buttons fills as a file comes, turns into an arrow once it is in, and opens Downloads when clicked; it is there only while something downloads and a moment after. The file shows its progress in the Finder and on the Dock's Downloads stack, as Safari's do, and the "Saved" line at the bottom shows it in the Finder when clicked.
- Search › Check for Updates…, under About Search, as in any Mac app: the answer comes in the line at the foot of the window, and the item becomes Restart to Update once a newer version is in place. Search also looks by itself at every launch and then every hour, where it looked once a day.
- Three switches are on from now on: Show where links go, Peek at a link with a shift-click, and Flick the floating video to a corner. Correct spelling as you type follows the Mac's own setting rather than starting off. A switch you already turned off stays off.
- Passkeys can unlock what a site encrypted with them: the WebAuthn PRF extension now reaches the passkey and its result comes back to the page, where it was dropped and sites that derive a key from your passkey said it wasn't supported. macOS 15 or later. ([#312](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/312)) Thanks [@arvakme](https://github.com/arvakme) ([#313](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/313))
- Links from other apps can open in a small window of their own: the page, the site and Open in Search (⌘O), which moves it into your tabs as it is, after the pins and without loading it again. Escape or ⌘W closes it. Off unless you turn it on in Settings › General › Open links from other apps in a small window. The idea came from [@K-NRS](https://github.com/K-NRS) ([#227](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/227)), after Arc's Little Arc.
- ⌥⌘R reloads the page from origin, as Safari's Reload Page From Origin does, checking everything cached with the site again; ⌘R is now an ordinary reload, where it always went back to the site for everything, and ⇧⌘R stays Reading Mode. A page reloaded while in Reading Mode is no longer taken for one, so ⇧⌘R strips it down again instead of only reloading. Thanks [@ductan2](https://github.com/ductan2) ([#179](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/179), [#171](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/171))
- ⇧⌘⌫, or History › Clear Browsing Data…, opens History at its clearing controls, from a page too, as in Chrome. It deletes nothing by itself: history, cookies and sign-ins, and the cache each still wait for their own button. Thanks [@siiddhantt](https://github.com/siiddhantt) ([#344](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/pull/344), [#336](https://github.com/driceroland/Search/issues/336))
- Search's own movements happen at once when Reduce Motion is on in System Settings › Accessibility › Display: the tabs, the column and the bar with the window's buttons coming and going, panels, the address field and a refused address's shake. Pages move as they always did.
